```bash
# prefill kernel autotunes on its first launch at the chunk shape, and doing that inside a
# measured request once cost the server (CUDA OOM at 0.41 GiB free on the needle probe).
# Jobs are a JSONL queue (mexp/glm53/queue.jsonl: one job per line = arm + server env +
# client + args); the runner launches each job's server, runs the client, keeps the
# server across same-config jobs, and records results/glm53/queue_state.jsonl. Edit the
# queue while it runs: it is re-read before every job. Registered queue (in order):
#   ruler-baseline, ruler-vestigekv   -> 13 RULER tasks x {4k,8k,16k,32k,64k}, 10 samples/cell
#   diag-smoke32k-vestigekv -> niah_single_1 at 32768 only, n=2, on the ruler config. Not a
#       result: it is the gate in front of the two long jobs below. The vestigekv arm had
#       never been started on this tree, and the first attempt died with a Triton NameError
#       after loading 88 GB of weights (engine 62b69520a8) -- a failure that costs three
#       minutes to provoke at 32k and an hour to discover at 128k. Runs with --tag so it
#       writes beside the arm's full RULER run rather than over it.
#   stream-baseline-128k, stream-vestigekv-128k -> metric 1 (bs=1, 4k prefill, 126976-token
#       decode; per-token latency curve, 64k point included) with CTX=135168, one mamba slot,
#       --mem-fraction-static 0.96, --cuda-graph-max-bs-decode 1. Started 2026-09-22 on engine
#       ee8b4f5a58 (exact memory reductions, bit-identical to 9153bb6e4d), split pair on the
#       vestigekv arm, CHUNK 512 with the runner's chunk-sized pre-warm in front of the probe.
#       Stopped once on 2026-09-22 at 65k/131k decoded tokens of the baseline arm to give the
#       GPU to the quality gate below, then reinstated behind it (the line reports the 4k->128k
#       latency curve; GLM throughput is not measured).
#   gsm8kv0520-{baseline,vestigekv} -> GSM8K-Platinum full set (n=1209, 64-shot, greedy), the
#       Kimi protocol (mexp/quality/gsm8k_platinum.jsonl, sglang.test.few_shot_gsm8k --parallel 1)
#       on the RULER one-slot config; needle probe in front of each arm.
#   mauve4kv0520-*, mauve64kv0520-* -> MAUVE gate, the frozen M7 protocol: 4k prefill x 16
#       fineweb-edu contexts and 64k prefill x 64, 256 generated tokens at temperature 1.0 /
#       top-p 0.95 with sampling_seed 1000+i per context shared across arms; contexts are cut
#       with the served model's tokenizer (M7_MODEL) into results/glm53/quality/. The runner's
#       `mauve` client generates; scoring (gpt2-large features, GPU) runs after both arms with
#       `mexp/m7_mauve_serving.py score`, writing results/glm53/quality/mauve_verdict_T*_n*.json.
#   dsafb-smoke32k4k-vestigekv -> the gate in front of the vestigekv arm's re-run on engine 65a85c587e
#       (DSA's indexer runs at decode under the split pair; a fenced lane attends its selection):
#       needle probe, then the 32k/4k stream at MEM_FRAC 0.95 against diag-smoke32k4k-baseline's
#       record. Not a result; the server log must show 'salience: first DECODE keys filed' and
#       'DSA sibling drives decode metadata'. Every GLM vestigekv-arm quality record before this
#       tree was produced with tier-1 scoring +inf past the first 2048 tokens of a request and on
#       every decoded token (no indexer at decode), rescued by recall; they are superseded.
#   (2026-09-23) mexp/dsv2lite/ is the DeepSeek-V2-Lite-Chat line (RoPE MLA, dense-MLA baseline):
#       arm scripts only so far, no jobs registered; needle.py takes NEEDLE_MODEL and now allows
#       1024 tokens (300 let the model's thinking eat the answer). Redesign measurements are in
#       docs/glm53-line.md "Redesign measurements (2026-09-23)".
#   dsaopt-smoke32k4k-vestigekv -> the gate in front of the vestigekv arm on the OPTIMISED tree
#       (engine 8b83b939a9, branch glm53-dsa-decode, docs/glm53-line.md "Closing the decode gap"):
#       the fused salience-ring write, DSA's own split-K decode over the tiers, the model's split
#       q/k with the pool's two-tensor KV write, 256-row scan buckets and the 8-warp merge; the lean
#       graph is opt-in and off, so every step attends exactly what the single-graph tree attends.
#       Needle probe, then the 32k/4k stream at MEM_FRAC 0.95 against diag-smoke32k4k-baseline's
#       record. Not a result. dsafb-smoke32k4k-vestigekv's record (engine 63f2d6f994) is retired to
#       superseded/*.preopt*. rulerv0520s1-vestigekv's 09:31 record was produced BEFORE the indexer
#       fix (its server log has no 'DSA sibling drives'): retired to superseded/*.noindexer*, state
#       row marked superseded, and the job re-runs on this tree after gsm8kv0520-vestigekv (order:
#       smoke, mauve4k, mauve64k, the 128k pair's vestigekv arm, gsm8k, RULER, lb1sum). The baseline
#       arm's code path is untouched by these commits (diff restricted to VestigeKV files, one
#       backend-name list entry and a capture log line), so its records stand.
#       The first release (15:18, engine 3f2ab34150) passed this smoke but every vestigekv-arm job on
#       the RULER one-slot config (MEM_FRAC 0.955) then died at its first prefill: DSA's Triton sparse
#       prefill kernel autotunes 27 configs at first use, when the driver has <1 GiB, and the one-warp
#       configs' launches reserve 0.6-0.8 GB of local memory each (the same failure took mauve4k/64k,
#       the 128k arm and gsm8k at 12:26-12:33). Engine 8b83b939a9 tunes the kernel at backend init
#       and prunes the one-warp configs; the affected rows are marked superseded in queue_state and
#       re-run. The baseline arm is unaffected in what it computes (the selected config is the same).
#   lb1sumv0520-{baseline,vestigekv} -> LongBench v1 summarization (gov_report, qmsum, multi_news),
#       the Kimi lb1sum protocol (mexp/kimi/run_longbench1.py, ROUGE-L by make_lb1_numbers.py --line glm53)
#       on the RULER one-slot config, prompts cut with the GLM tokenizer. Order within the GLM line
#       after gsm8kv0520-baseline (already running when it was set, 2026-09-22): shortest experiment
#       first -- mauve4k, mauve64k (both arms), the 128k pair, gsm8k vestigekv, lb1sum -- and the
#       whole GLM line before the Kimi line so the served checkpoint switches once.
#   stats-vestigekv-stream-128k, stats-vestigekv-ruler-64k -> the same two workloads on the
#       vestigekv arm with SGLANG_DEBUG_VESTIGEKV_STATS=1 (separate jobs: the bookkeeping syncs
#       every step, so it never runs on a timed arm). The server log's VKSTATS lines (every 50
#       decode steps, cumulative) carry fetched rows per scan p50/p90/p99 and
#       fallback = overflow scans / scans; the stream job's successive lines are the
#       context-length curve, the RULER-64k job the value at 64k prompts.
#   replay-vestigekv-r64, -r128, -r256 -> the same three saved 64k RULER prompts
#       (niah_single_2, qa_squad, cwe from the baseline samples) replayed on the vestigekv arm
#       at sketch ranks 64, 128 and 256 with stats on; the per-job server log's VKCAL lines
#       carry need-vs-fire per calibrated build (mexp/glm53/replay_prompts.py). The r128 job
#       also sets SGLANG_DEBUG_VESTIGEKV_DUMP_DIR=results/glm53/caldump: one snapshot per
#       (slot, layer) of the rows, keep set and calibration queries the index was fitted on;
#       python mexp/glm53/cert_offline.py refits sketch bases and ranks on them offline
#       (certificate zp, fire counts vs need, fallback fraction) without re-serving.
#       They run before the two stats jobs: the rank decision gates the stats config.
#   dsadump-vestigekv-{64k,8k,4k} -> DSA-indexer certificate study (engine branch
#       vestigekv-dsa-index / serving vestigekv-dsa-index-pro6000x2, checked out in engine/ for
#       these jobs): saved RULER prompts (niah_single_2, cwe, fwe, qa_squad; 2-3 samples per
#       task) replayed with --ignore-eos (64 tokens, so every request reaches a calibrated
#       build), GRAPH=0 (the indexer stash copies to the host every step, which a captured
#       graph cannot) and SGLANG_DEBUG_VESTIGEKV_DUMP_DIR=results/glm53/dsadump, whose snapshots also
#       carry the indexer keys / KPool gates of every token and the indexer query heads of the
#       calibration queries; python mexp/glm53/dsa_cert_offline.py scores the indexer-based
#       certificate (mass coverage, conformal z) against the content-sketch one on them.
#   ruler-dense-mla-short -> fwe, cwe, qa_squad, niah_single_1 at 4k and 8k (10/cell) on the
#       dense_mla substrate (DSA off, no VestigeKV): RULER "4k" prompts are ~3.9k tokens, below
#       one 4096 close block, so the vestigekv arm attends them dense; this separates the
#       substrate's effect (a DSA-trained model run dense) from VestigeKV's at 4k/8k.
#   memtrace-vestigekv-64k -> 13 tasks x 3 samples at 64k on the vestigekv arm with
#       SGLANG_DEBUG_VESTIGEKV_MEM_DIR=results/glm53/memtrace and stats OFF: the server log's
#       VKMEM lines (allocated / reserved at every request's first prefill chunk) and one
#       allocator snapshot per five requests (mem_req<N>.pickle; diff two with
#       python mexp/glm53/memdiff.py). Background: ruler-vestigekv OOMed at its 27th 64k
#       prompt twice while the same prompts with stats on served 119; the stats syncs
#       change what the allocator sees, so the trace must not sync.
python mexp/glm53/queue_runner.py
# by hand (same commands the runner issues):
#   bash mexp/glm53/baseline.sh ; python mexp/glm53/run_ruler.py --arm baseline --n 10
#   bash mexp/glm53/vestigekv.sh ; python mexp/glm53/run_ruler.py --arm vestigekv --n 10
#   CTX=135168 MAX_REQS=1 MAMBA_SLOTS=1 MEM_FRAC=0.96 CHUNK=512 GRAPH_BS=1 bash mexp/glm53/<arm>.sh
#   PYTHONPATH=$PWD/engine/python python -m sglang.benchmark.serving --backend sglang \
#     --model nvidia/GLM-5.3-Flash-NVFP4 --num-prompts 1 --dataset-name random \
#     --random-input-len 4096 --random-output-len 126976 --random-range-ratio 1 \
#     --max-concurrency 1 --warmup-requests 0 --output-details \
#     --output-file results/glm53/latency_stream_4k-126976_<arm>.jsonl
# results: results/glm53/ruler/results_<arm>_n10_4096-8192-16384-32768-65536.json (+ samples),
#          results/glm53/latency_stream_4k-126976_<arm>.jsonl, server_<arm>_<job>.log per job.
#          RECORD NOTE: the vestigekv stream file on disk is the stats-on run (a stats job
#          used to write the same name and overwrote the timed one; the runner now suffixes
#          _stats). The timed vestigekv curve, read before that overwrite (fixed engine,
#          per-token median over the 4096 tokens ending at each point): 8k 11.535, 16k 11.613,
#          32k 11.731, 64k 11.885, 128k 12.207 ms vs baseline 11.265/11.283/11.301/11.340/11.398
#          (ratio 0.977 -> 0.934; means 11.908 vs 11.342 ms/token).
# two-arm RULER table (task x length, a/b, means): python mexp/glm53/compare_ruler.py
# per-token latency vs context from the stream jobs: python mexp/glm53/stream_curve.py
# GSM8K-Platinum (64-shot, n=1209, serial) on either arm: relaunch with CTX=16384, then
#   PYTHONPATH=$PWD/engine/python python -m sglang.test.few_shot_gsm8k --num-shots 64 \
#     --num-questions 1209 --data-path mexp/quality/gsm8k_platinum.jsonl --parallel 1 --port 30000
# Head-needle probe (10.6k tokens, code at position 0): python mexp/glm53/needle.py

# --- quality: long-context MAUVE (64k prefill; the decode-path fidelity gate)
# 16 fineweb-edu contexts of 65536 tokens (compression genuinely engaged),
# 256-token generations, per-context sampling_seed shared across arms; same
# frozen MAUVE bar (vk >= dense - 0.10). One-command pipeline (4 cells):
bash mexp/quality/run_mauve64k.sh
# (manual per-arm variant: M7_T=65536 python mexp/m7_mauve_serving.py gen
#  results/quality_mauve64k_ctx.json results/quality_mauve64k_<arm>.json,
#  then ... score with the same ctx file.)

# --- quality: greedy continuation agreement — RETIRED (ERRATA #20) --------
# The frozen bar (median first-divergence >= 128, min >= 8) is unreachable
# even for a pure attention-kernel swap on the SAME dense model (dense
# flashinfer vs triton: median 37.5, worst 2): the gate measured numeric
# sensitivity, not compression fidelity. Data kept for the record:
#   results/quality_continuation_{vestigekv,dense}[_instruct].json
#   results/quality_continuation_dense_flashinfer.json  (kernel-swap control)

# --- quality: needle-in-a-haystack on the serving path (per arm) ----------
# Serving counterpart of the harness needle: fixed-seed passcode spliced at
# a random depth into fineweb-edu filler, compressed at prefill BEFORE the
# query exists, greedy answer judged by exact string. Same prompts for both
# arms; frozen bar: vk intact rate >= dense intact rate (>= 8 trials or
# ABORT). Default L=131072 n=8; L=524288 n=4 is the long-context
# confirmation (prefill-bound, ~13 min/trial).
python mexp/quality/needle_serving.py gen 131072 8 results/quality_needle_vestigekv.json
# ... relaunch the dense arm, then:
python mexp/quality/needle_serving.py gen 131072 8 results/quality_needle_dense.json
python mexp/quality/needle_serving.py compare \
  results/quality_needle_vestigekv.json results/quality_needle_dense.json \
  results/quality_needle_verdict.json

# --- Q5: Chinese-corpus needle (serving, both arms) ---------------------------
# Same protocol as Q4 but haystack AND needle are Chinese: 三国演义 filler
# (mexp/quality/sanguoyanyi.txt, ~492k tokens) + a modern-Chinese fact
# ("{place}的接头暗号是「{code}」。") at random depth. Arabic digits never
# occur in the classical text, so the code is an unambiguous foreign token
# sequence; answers are accepted in Arabic or Chinese numerals. Same frozen
# bar. Scheduled AFTER all other experiments (exploratory, not a gate).
python mexp/quality/needle_chinese.py gen 131072 8 results/quality_needle_zh_vestigekv.json
# ... relaunch the dense arm, then:
python mexp/quality/needle_chinese.py gen 131072 8 results/quality_needle_zh_dense.json
python mexp/quality/needle_chinese.py compare \
  results/quality_needle_zh_vestigekv.json results/quality_needle_zh_dense.json \
  results/quality_needle_zh_verdict.json

# --- figures --------------------------------------------------------------
# Both README figures and the paper's are one script; the panel title carries the
# model, the hardware and TP read from every server log the panel draws from.
python mexp/kimi/make_figures.py --emit --out results   # README copies
python mexp/kimi/make_figures.py --emit                 # the paper's (default --out)
```

### Algorithm claims (single machine, HF-forward harness)

The needle-retrieval, observed-attention baselines (H2O / SnapKV /
StreamingLLM), the RoPE-collapse control, the sketch-rank and branch-width
ablations, and teacher-forced bits-per-byte are measured by the HF-forward
harness (`harness/`, no two-node serving). Each paper table names its
`--ops` and its PREREG in the matching skill file (see `EXPERIMENTS.md`).
The harness needs the pinned 4.x stack (ENV_PINS.txt); the serving env runs
transformers 5.x, which breaks the 4.x-era modeling code, so run the harness
with the isolated target dir on PYTHONPATH (checkpoints as plain dirs, see
`harness/extract.py`):
`pip install --target=$HOME/.cache/vestigekv/tf457 transformers==4.57.1 "huggingface-hub<1.0" "kernels<=0.9,>=0.6.1"`
`export PYTHONPATH=$HOME/.cache/vestigekv/tf457`

### Experiments are queue entries, not typed commands

Serving experiments go in `mexp/<line>/queue.jsonl` and are run by
`mexp/glm53/queue_runner.py --line <line>`, never launched by hand. Before the
runner starts, `python mexp/exp/audit_queue.py` resolves each pending job to
the command and output path it will actually produce and refuses on: a job
pinned to any tree but its line's one tree (`audit_queue.LINE_TREE`: `engine/`,
the unified v0.5.20 one, for Kimi; the v0.5.21 rebase worktree for GLM, whose
records are all taken there -- a line that names no tree falls back to
`engine/`, so a new line cannot pin itself to a scratch worktree by saying
nothing), an output file that already exists, paired arms whose env or args differ, an `arm` with no
launcher, a client the runner does not implement, and a seeded client with no
seed. Every one of those refusals was driven with known-bad input before the
check was trusted.

The queue is the record: `results/<line>/queue_state.jsonl` carries each job's
status, wall time and log path, and a retired job keeps its entry with a
`skip_reason` rather than being deleted.

### Harness experiments are scripts, not typed commands

`mexp/exp/` holds one shell script per registered experiment, and
`bash mexp/exp/audit.sh` is run before any of them. The rule exists because
hand-typed runs went wrong twice in ways a script could have caught: an OOM
from `--gpu-expert-layers 99`, and a wrong protocol from `--rhos 0.03125` where
that flag wants a compression RATIO. The audit refuses on exactly those, plus a
missing guard, an `--out` that already exists, and any script this README does
not mention. `_lib.sh` carries the guards: refuse to start while a server, the
queue runner or another harness run holds the GPU; refuse to overwrite a record;
refuse a model directory that is not a plain dir.

**Control the variables, and say which.** Every defect this project has shipped
in a comparison was a loose variable, never a wrong number: two arms of one
table row running different operators (a drop policy against a half-budget
merge); an arm measured at a detector bandwidth nobody deploys; a control whose
engine tree no surviving record names; rows of one table at different trial
counts. None of those is visible in the numbers afterwards, and all of them
were decided before the run.

So every script declares, and `audit.sh` refuses without it:

```
# CONTROL:
#   varies:  the one thing this experiment changes between arms
#   fixed:   seed, trial count, lengths, tasks, checkpoint, engine tree, and
#            every launch knob -- named, not implied
```

Three rules follow from it. Arms that will be printed in one table run from
**one script in one sitting**, because that is the only way to be sure they
differ in what the script says they differ in. Where they cannot -- Table 2's
rows come from three harness processes -- the generator **checks the shared
input rather than trusting the seed**: `make_kvzip_numbers.py` refuses to emit
unless the dense forward's NLL is bit-identical across records on every trial,
which it is iff the three processes saw the same needles, documents, codes and
depths. An arm measured on a different
engine tree is a different experiment, and the tree is part of `fixed:`. And
`--seed` is passed explicitly even where the runner defaults it, because a
default is not a declaration -- the audit greps for it with comments stripped,
having once been satisfied by a script whose only `--seed` was a sentence in
its own header.

| script | what it registers |
|---|---|
| `mexp/exp/kvzip-needle-8k.sh` | head-to-head against KVzip, the query-independent baseline the PAT review asked for three times, at 32x and 128x -- where THIS paper operates. Our transcription of the authors' scoring rule in our harness, at a matched budget |
| `mexp/exp/kvzip-inrange-8k.sh` | the same three arms at 2x, 4x, 8x -- where KVZIP operates, its README claiming 3-4x with minimal degradation. Reporting only the first script would repeat the criticism the review made of the H2O table: a method evaluated outside its stated range collapses by construction and says nothing. Also carries the timing reference, whose limits its header states |
| `mexp/exp/kvzip-selector-kappa16.sh` | the selector-only arm of that table at the DEPLOYED detector bandwidth. The two scripts above ran it as `digk64`, whose `k64` is the bandwidth kappa=64, not the shipped kappa=16; its row therefore disagreed with the Section 6.1 selector numbers (`dig_r64`, kappa=16) for the same quantity. This run is the one the table prints |
| `mexp/exp/kvzip-random-floor.sh` | the floor row of the same table: `imp_random` keeps top-m by a random score at the same budget, so the other rows have a scale. It exists to answer the cheapest attack on a transcribed baseline -- "your port is broken" -- because a broken port cannot score 24/24 at 2x on a metric where blind selection scores 0. Not a baseline anyone proposed |
| `mexp/exp/ruler-fallback-ablation.sh` | what the overflow fallback contributes, measured by deleting it: three arms back to back on engine-fused (the tree the n=50 RULER arms were measured on), differing only in `SGLANG_DEBUG_VESTIGEKV_NO_OVERFLOW_FALLBACK` and the truncation policy. Deleting it is an ablation, not a configuration -- the buffer sits behind an uncapped admission rule, so an overflow is the certificate reporting that a step cannot be served sparsely, and the fallback is the answer to that report |
| `mexp/exp/latency-pair-512k.sh` | the bs=1 latency curve with both arms on the released tree in one sitting, server log KEPT. The paper's 1.28x/1.53x are hand-maintained values naming server logs that no longer exist, and the client JSONL that does ship reduces to 0.066 at 508k -- VestigeKV 15x slower where the abstract claims 1.53x faster -- because the streaming client's ITL is contaminated above ~80-180 tok/s and the contamination penalises the faster arm. This run produces a pair that exists, shares a tree, and ships |
| `paired512k-bs1-{dense,vestigekv}` (queue) | the same pair again at `GRAPH_BS=1`. The first attempt captured a graph for bs=2 while decoding at bs=1, breaking the `GRAPH_BS == MAX_REQS` convention 128 of the other 130 queue rows keep. Its dense arm reproduced two independent earlier records at 256k (5.571 against 5.590 and 5.598) while its vestigekv arm came out 3.7% slower than both (4.508 against 4.364 and 4.345), which is what a surplus graph lane looks like: dispatch the six per-step VestigeKV kernels pay and the dense path does not. The first pair is retired, not deleted, under `~/vestigekv-retired/graphbs2-latency-pair/`, and `mexp/exp/audit_queue.py` now refuses a job whose captured graph is wider than its batch |
| `mexp/kimi/sidecar_spectrum_offline.py` (analysis, waits on `sidecardump-vestigekv`) | whether tier-1's low-pass misses periodic structure the real sidecar carries -- the part of the pre-review question no algebra settles. Reports, per snapshot, the share of energy above the kappa cutoff and the peakiness of what is there. The share is not diagnostic on its own: kappa=16 of 2048 bins keeps so little of a white spectrum that broadband noise already reads 99%. Peakiness is, because only a component shared across rows inflates every sigma alike and so cannot separate them. Driven with planted data before use: broadband reads 1.4, a periodic component at bin 200 reads 9224. It also refuses a snapshot whose `row_slots` are non-monotone, because pool order is the allocator's business and a shuffled block makes a token-axis spectrum meaningless while looking entirely normal |
| `sidecardump-vestigekv` (queue, tail) | the sidecar rows themselves, to answer a pre-review question on tier-1's low-pass with data rather than algebra. The reviewer asked whether pairing feature dims into complex numbers and low-passing the complex spectrum would work better, by analogy with RoPE. Two things answer it without a run: the branch is un-roped by construction (`SIDECAR_DIM`: "Un-roped half of the latent row"), which is the paper's own NoPE argument, so there is no rotation to exploit; and a symmetric complex low-pass is algebraically the current per-channel real low-pass -- measured identical to 8.9e-16, and unchanged when the pairing is permuted, which is the lock: a transform that used the pairing could not be indifferent to it. What is NOT answered is the question underneath: whether the low-pass misses periodic structure real keys carry. `SGLANG_DEBUG_VESTIGEKV_DUMP_DIR` already writes whole 576-dim pool rows, so `rows[:, 512:576]` is the sidecar and no new instrumentation is needed. One request, one task, 65536 tokens, about 0.5 GB of `.pt` -- a study input, not a shipped record |
| `rulern50base-{dense,vestigekv}` (queue, tail) | the RULER row of Table~\ref{tab:capability}, which compares both checkpoints and shows `---` in the two Base columns while GSM8K and MAUVE have them. The Base weights are already local and `common.sh` honours `MODEL`, so only the client was hardcoded to Instruct -- it would have asked a Base server for Instruct and labelled the record with a checkpoint it did not run. Records now carry a checkpoint marker in their name, because a Base run and an Instruct run at the same n and lengths otherwise write one file. Same protocol as the Instruct row, n=50, seed 0, about 2.3 h per arm |
| `tputB-bs32mf087/mf085-vestigekv` (queue) | whether the 128k top point is a limit of the method or of one deployment knob. VestigeKV OOMs there twice, reproducibly, at 31 live requests and 0.82 pool occupancy -- and the allocation that fails is 20 MiB, outside a KV pool both arms size identically at 37.70 GB. `expandable_segments` is already on, so this is not fragmentation the allocator can absorb. What differs is headroom: after startup dense has 9.02 GB free and VestigeKV 8.22, a 0.80 GB static footprint the paper already accounts for. These two rerun the point at `mem_fraction_static` 0.87 and 0.85. If it completes, the limitation is 'needs a smaller static fraction at this shape', not 'cannot run' -- a materially different sentence, and the wrong one is the one currently supported by the evidence |
| `tputB-bs32r2-{dense,vestigekv}` (queue, tail) | one repeat of the 128k top point, because `tputB-bs32-vestigekv` died there and a limitation claimed from a single crash is not a limitation. The dense arm completed the same point; VestigeKV's rank 0 was killed at 31 live requests and 82% pool occupancy, with an identical backend configuration to the 24-live run that survived, so the allocation that failed is outside the KV pool and scales with live requests times rows. If it reproduces it belongs in Limitations; if it does not, nothing is written |
| `tputA-bs{1,2,4,8,12,16,24}` and `tputB-...` -- each a `-dense` / `-vestigekv` pair (queue) | the throughput sweep rebuilt around what the machine actually does. `tput32`/`tput128k` are superseded and their records quarantined: the metric asked the client how fast a batch decoded and never asked whether the batch existed, and the server's log says 64k bs=32 ran 31-then-1 and 128k bs=4 ran 2. A point is now tokens counted into the KV pool over wall clock, inside a window where `#running-req` is constant and equal to the batch, cross-checked against the server's own reported rate. `output_len` is set per point so that window lasts about 150 s: the log stamps whole seconds, and the first sweep's bs=1 plateau lasted 18 s, which is 11% of quantisation on its own. At 128k the output is further raised to 1.4x the batch's total prefill, because otherwise early requests finish decoding before late ones finish prefilling and the batch is never whole. About 3.5 hours for both contexts, both arms |
| `tput128k-bs{N}r{2,3}` -- each a `-dense` / `-vestigekv` pair (queue) | three runs of every 128k point, because one is not enough to read a shape. At 64k the dense arm repeats to 0.00-0.11% while VestigeKV spreads 1.22% at bs=4 and 4.36% at bs=12, so the 0.7% dip that prompted the check is inside its own noise. The variance is one-sided and grows with batch, which is a property worth reporting rather than averaging away -- and it is not dispatch: every per-step kernel launches a grid of max_bs lanes regardless of the live batch, so bs=12 and bs=32 dispatch identically and non-powers-of-two cost nothing extra. About 108 minutes for the two extra passes across both arms |
| `tput128k-bs{1,2,4,8,12,16,24,32}` -- each a `-dense` / `-vestigekv` pair (queue) | the same throughput sweep at 128k prefill. 64k is the weakest context this method has: the bs=1 latency curve reads 1.052 there against 1.136 at 128k, so a throughput panel that stops at 64k understates the method at the context the paper argues for. Both contexts are kept rather than one replaced -- the pair is what shows the advantage growing with context at fixed batch, and 64k is already measured. Capacity is the binding constraint and dense is the arm that binds: 32 x (128k+4k) = 4325376 tokens against a 5020421-token pool, 86%. `GRAPH_BS=32=MAX_REQS`, radix off, seed 0, decode-only throughput (bs / mean ITL), about 56 minutes of prefill across both arms |
| `tput32-bs1`, `tput32-bs4`, `tput32-bs12`, `tput32-bs24`, `tput32-bs32` -- each a `-dense` / `-vestigekv` pair (queue, staged) | Figure 2's right panel, re-measured on the one tree. `GRAPH_BS=32` with `MAX_REQS=32` for every point in the sweep, so the captured graph is the same width at bs=1 as at bs=32 and the curve's shape is the batch's doing rather than the capture's; that width is stated in the caption. Radix cache off on both arms, per the RADIX POLICY block in `mexp/exp/latency-pair-512k.sh`. 64k prefill, 4096 decoded tokens, `concurrency == num_prompts` at each point. Staged in `mexp/kimi/queue_staged_throughput.jsonl` rather than queued, because the running runner holds the code from before `args.concurrency` existed and would have run all ten jobs at concurrency 1 and marked them done. Launch: `python mexp/exp/audit_queue.py && python mexp/glm53/queue_runner.py --line kimi` after appending the staged rows to `mexp/kimi/queue.jsonl` and restarting the runner |

| `tputB87-bs{1,2,4,8,12,16,24,32}` -- each a `-dense` / `-vestigekv` pair (queue) | the 128k sweep re-measured with `MEM_FRAC=0.87` on BOTH arms, because the published series stops at 16 and the two points it is missing are missing for two different reasons, neither of them a limit of the method. At 32 the VestigeKV arm dies: the server drops the streams mid-generation (`ClientPayloadError`, 4080 of 8192 tokens) and `tputB-bs32r2` reproduces it. That is the OOM already recorded two rows above -- an allocation outside a KV pool both arms size identically at 5020421 tokens, where after startup dense has 9.02 GB free and VestigeKV 8.22. At 24 the run completes cleanly (24/24, 196608 tokens, zero errors) and the POINT is refused: counted 1083.4 tok/s against a reported 1113.6, 2.72% over a 2% tolerance, reproduced at 2.29% on `bs24r2`. That gap is not an error, it is the metric disagreeing with itself -- `counted` is total tokens over wall clock, `reported` is the arithmetic mean of the server's per-line rate, and the two part company exactly in proportion to how variable the rate is. VestigeKV's rate is variable by construction: it closes a block and rebuilds an index every 4096 tokens, measured here as cv 13.8% against dense's 7.0%, with its mean 4.1% below its median where dense's is 0.5%. So the tolerance is a cap on how periodic an arm is allowed to be, and only one arm has periodic work. 0.87 and not 0.85: bs=32 at 128k needs 4456448 pool tokens, 0.87 gives 4707275 (5.6% of margin) and 10.58 GB of non-pool headroom, while 0.85 gives 4456759 -- 311 tokens above the requirement, which is luck rather than margin. Both arms move together because giving only VestigeKV a smaller static fraction would vary a launch knob alongside the backend, which is the defect this file already charges the superseded 512k pair with. 16 jobs, about 5 hours; the existing `tputB` records are then superseded, not deleted |
| `lb1sum-{dense,vestigekv}` (queue, before tputB87: shortest first within the Kimi line) | LongBench v1 summarization (gov_report, qmsum, multi_news; 200 items each, `mexp/kimi/longbench1/` from THUDM/LongBench data.zip), the dataset's prompt and max_gen (512), greedy, raw completion, prompts over 65536 tokens middle-truncated as LongBench's pred.py does; ROUGE-L F1 by LongBench's eval.py rule with py-rouge (`make_lb1_numbers.py`, plotting venv). The one generative long-context task where nothing points at the rows that matter: eviction plus recall has to hold with a whole-document query. Kimi Instruct, RULER one-slot config. Registered 2026-09-22. |

<!-- registered launches: audited verbatim against mexp/*/queue.jsonl -->

This block is the experiment set: the launches this paper needs run, written
exactly as they run. `mexp/<line>/queue.jsonl` is derived from it, not
maintained beside it --- two hand-kept copies is how they diverge:

```
python mexp/exp/init_queue.py --line kimi            # show the difference
python mexp/exp/init_queue.py --line kimi --write    # write queue.jsonl
python mexp/exp/audit_queue.py                       # nine refusals
python mexp/glm53/queue_runner.py --line kimi        # run it
```

`init_queue.py` refuses to rewrite the queue under a live runner, and
`audit_queue.py` compares each queued row against its line here character for
character, so a knob edited after registration is refused rather than run. To
change an experiment, change it here first.

```json
The indexer-only arm: tier 1 alone (the salience record read out of DSA's
pooled index-k cache), no tier-2 sketch built, no per-step recall, no fetch.
It separates what selection costs from what the tier-2 chain costs, against
the c521z pair above as its matched control.

A behavioural smoke for the arm (STATS on, own server): it must show no
calibration build and no fetched row at all.

The lean twin of the indexer-only arm. With no tier 2 there is no certificate
and no overflow, so the lean decode graph (index-k written, DSA's scoring and
top-k skipped) is taken on every step by construction -- DSA's own selection
chain, which the vestigekv layers discard, stops running. The smoke must show
lean at ~100% of steps in VKSTATS.

The branch rule (memory glm-branch-only): tier 2 = grouped recall on DSA's own
pooled index logits above the q0.75 quantile of the kept groups' scores, cap 512
groups; no sketch, no build, no calibration. q0.75 is the offline cell that
matches DSA's oracle mass with the tier-1 kept set preserved (branch_kept.py).
Smoke must show fetch[p50/p90] > 0 and build x0; then the same-tree cost pair
and multikey_3 n=50 against DSA's 1.000/1.000.

v3 of the branch rule (same env): threshold + count, no top-k of ours; a step
with more than 512 fired groups raises fetch_ovf and the existing fence hands
that step to DSA's own top-k (kept rows are not attended on a fenced step --
"fall back to DSA" literally). v1 (c521br-{32k4k,4k124k}) truncated to the
best 512 groups with a torch topk over ~34k groups per layer-step and cost
1.21x DSA; those two records are retired to superseded/ once v3's land.

c521br2-mk3: multikey_3 n=50 under v3's fence semantics (a fenced step is
DSA's own selection, kept rows not attended), beside c521br-mk3 (v1 truncation).
The v3 cost pair waits for the fused operator: the torch chain is 85 kernels
per layer-step and the cost is the node count, not any one op.
```

```bash
# needle + observed-attention baselines at 8k (Table: baselines / tab:main)
python harness/e2e.py --arch kimi --seq-len 8192 --n-docs 0 \
  --needle-trials 24 --gpu-expert-layers 18 --seed 0 --ops baselines \
  --out results/harness_baselines_8192.json
# RoPE-collapse negative control (Table: rope) -- same op set on a RoPE-MLA
python harness/e2e.py --arch dsv2 --seq-len 8192 --n-docs 0 \
  --needle-trials 24 --gpu-expert-layers 18 --seed 0 --ops twotier \
  --out results/harness_rope_8192.json
# sketch-rank sweep (Table: ablation) and bits-per-byte (Table: bpb) select
# their own --ops/--rhos; see the PREREG in each skill file for the exact line.

# post-training survival (Appendix: SFT): the SAME command on the Instruct
# checkpoint, with the Base weights run as an in-session control. Both arms
# use byte-identical modelling code (the Base copy) so the only variable is
# the weights; register the paths as `kimi_instruct` / `kimi_base_mirror`
# in harness/extract.py.
for ARCH in kimi_base_mirror kimi_instruct; do
  python harness/e2e.py --arch $ARCH --seq-len 8192 --n-docs 0 \
    --needle-trials 24 --gpu-expert-layers 18 --seed 0 \
    --ops twotier,imp_only,recent --rhos 32,128 \
    --out results/harness_needle_8192_${ARCH#kimi_}.json
done
# static anatomy (branch/content row norms), weights only, no forward pass:
python harness/dissect.py <model-dir> results/harness_anatomy_<arm>.json

# --- tab:tier Wilson n-boost (PREREG34, skills/prereg34-tier-wilson-boost.md)
# Pure measurement, no bar; pooling/containment rules frozen in the skill.
# (a) 8k +recall row and the survival pair: seed 0 extended 24 -> 64 trials.
#     The first 24 trials MUST bit-reproduce the n=24 files above (the
#     containment check script is in the skill file); ABORT otherwise.
for ARCH in kimi_base_mirror kimi_instruct; do
  python harness/e2e.py --arch $ARCH --seq-len 8192 --n-docs 0 \
    --needle-trials 64 --gpu-expert-layers 18 --seed 0 \
    --ops twotier,imp_only,recent --rhos 32,128 \
    --out results/harness_needle64_8192_${ARCH#kimi_}.json
done
# (b) 32k digk64 cells: seeds 11+12 extended 12 -> 16 trials each (pooled 32).
for SEED in 11 12; do
  python harness/e2e.py --arch kimi --seq-len 32768 --n-docs 0 \
    --needle-trials 16 --gpu-expert-layers 18 --seed $SEED \
    --rhos 32,64,128 --ops digk64,recent \
    --out results/harness_tier_32768_seed${SEED}.json
done
# (c) 65k digk64 cells: historically single seed 11 n=12; re-run seed 11
#     (covers both historical rho splits) and add seed 12 (pooled 24).
for SEED in 11 12; do
  python harness/e2e.py --arch kimi --seq-len 65536 --n-docs 0 \
    --needle-trials 12 --gpu-expert-layers 18 --seed $SEED \
    --rhos 32,64,128 --ops digk64,recent \
    --out results/harness_tier_65536_seed${SEED}.json
done
```


## Recovered launch register (2026-10-04)

This file was truncated by a bad recovery command of mine --
`open("README.md","w").write(expr)` evaluates the open before `expr`, so a
NameError in `expr` left a zero-byte file. The text below is the recovery:
the committed version is intact above, every queue row is re-registered here
verbatim from `mexp/glm53/queue.jsonl`, and the per-job notes recovered from
the session's recorded file diffs follow. The notes for the older done jobs
(`replay-*`, `dsadump-*`, `rulerv0520-*`, `r521mk-*`, `c521-*`, `c521x-*`,
`c521y-*`, `sw00`..`sw08`, `c521br5-*`, `c521br6-*`) are NOT recovered -- their
launches are below but their prose is lost. Their results stand in
`results/glm53/BASELINE_v0521.md` and in the state record.

vestigekv/dsa_branch.py). c521br2-smoke ran on the v1 tree -- the runner

c521br7-*: the branch rule re-measured on the position-table tree (engine
28a3074: five fused kernels, the kept POSITION instead of a page-table
inverse). The rule is unchanged, so these are a re-measurement and a fourth
multikey draw, not a new arm. Microbench: 10.3 us/layer, 113 us/step.

c521b-bs8x8k-* and c521b-bs4x16k-*: the first BATCHED GLM cost points, each
with its own DSA baseline. At bs=1 the decode attention is schedule-bound --
16x fewer rows buys 10% of the kernel -- so the arm's 22% row advantage
cannot convert; at batch the kernel should turn bandwidth-bound. The KV pool
caps bs*(in+out) at 78784 tokens, which is what sizes these two shapes.

Replicates of the (b) smoke and cost point, same launch, same tree. The byte
ratio moved 0.845 -> 0.852 -> 0.879 across v5 -> v6 -> (b) and p99 1.030x ->
1.045x, while kept=1120/call and seq=31750/call are IDENTICAL on all three --
so the kept set did not move and only the FIRE count did. v5 -> v6 cannot
change the rule yet moved fetched 451 -> 473 (+4.9%), which is one noise
sample; the decode trajectory is nondeterministic, so the per-step queries
and hence the fire counts differ run to run. These replicates give the spread
that says whether 555 (+17%) is inside it.

c521b-bs8x8k-*-r2/-r3 and c521b-bs16x4k-*: the batched point replicated, and
one shape wider. The first 8x8k pair is the only GLM measurement where the
branch arm wins end to end (output throughput 1.047x, median TPOT 0.963x, p99
TPOT 0.932x, duration 0.955x) while its POOLED median ITL is still 1.044x --
the win is in the tail and in prefill-interleaved steps, not in the typical
decode step. n=8 requests in one run is not enough to own that sign, so these
two replicates run the identical launch, and 16x4k asks whether the win grows
with batch at a fixed KV budget. Both arms carry the inert BRANCH_Q so only
`arm` varies.

### Launches (verbatim, re-registered)

c521io2-*: the tier-1-only arm done RIGHT. c521io-fit*-vk measured nothing:
`_indexer_only()` is `INDEXER_ONLY or BRANCH_Q is not None`, and those rows
inherited BRANCH_Q=0.75 from the branch row, so adding INDEXER_ONLY=1 changed
no behaviour and the two jobs were a second run of the branch arm. They are
still useful as a REPLICATE (median ITL 21.455 vs 21.478 at 8x3k, 15.969 vs
16.043 at 4x8k, so run-to-run noise on this shape is 0.1-0.5%), but the
tier-1/tier-2 split they appeared to give (+0.257/+0.024 and +0.728/+0.073 ms)
is VOID. These rows drop BRANCH_Q so the branch recall is actually off.

these are a behavioural re-measurement and they must reproduce what the same
launches gave before the purge: the smoke's kept/fetched/seq per call and its
byte ratio, and the 8x3k pair's median ITL ratio (1.0040 on the two-arm kernel,
c521v8-fit8x3k). A move in either is a deletion that took something live with
it, not a result.

c521p2-*: the c521p-* re-run. All three c521p rows failed with "server did not
start": the purge left one import of the deleted MLA backend inside
vestigekv.salience.vestigekv_backend_of, a function the live DSA indexer calls,
and an import in a function body cannot fail until it runs -- the package
compiled and both registered suites passed. Fixed in engine 001702cb1b, which
also adds test_vestigekv_imports_resolve.py so a dangling import fails in the
unit suite instead of at a server launch.

c521p3-*: the purge check after the per-step kept append was restored (engine
b6424893db). c521p2-smoke is the measurement that caught the defect: kept 1120
-> 1005 per call and fetched 555 -> 371 with seq identical at 31750, because
the deleted CSR pack kernel also carried the decode step's append to the kept
table. kept is deterministic on this line, so these must come back to kept=1120
and fetched near 555; the byte ratio reads ~0.901 rather than the old 0.879
because the counter now charges our arm its own 9 B per group (engine
3f657f3780), not because anything moved.

left open. fetched read 444/call against a recorded trajectory band of 451-555,
and the 8x3k median ITL ratio went 1.0040 -> 1.0122 while p99 went 1.0134 ->
0.9992, both from single runs against a 0.1-0.5% run-to-run spread. The row
counters now print to 0.1 of a row (engine 06fd36df26), so these also say
whether kept really moved by a row or only crossed a rounding boundary: the
restored append is one launch for all layers after the per-layer recall loop,

c521t-* and c521t1-*: the tier-1/tier-2 split, measured properly for the first
time on the branch arm. Every earlier attempt was void -- `_indexer_only()` is
`INDEXER_ONLY or BRANCH_Q is not None`, so a control that kept BRANCH_Q was the
branch arm again, and the bs=1 0.114/0.109 split in memory decomposed the
SKETCH tier, which no longer exists. The actuator is traced this time:
`group_fetch` returns on its first line when `_branch_q()` is None, so dropping
BRANCH_Q turns tier 2 off and branch_fire never runs, while INDEXER_ONLY=1
keeps `_indexer_only()` True exactly as the branch arm has it -- tier 2 is the
only variable that moves.

THREE runs per arm, because the measured noise floor at this shape is 1.09% on
the baseline's own median ITL and 1.07 points on the ratio: a split of a
0.3-ms gap cannot be read from single runs.

c521k-smoke: one smoke with a VKKEPT line that prints the kept set's
composition. The served kept (1121.3/call at seq 31750, identical on two
replicates) does not match the formula the code reads -- closed =
(seq//CLOSE_BLOCK)*CLOSE_BLOCK with CLOSE_BLOCK=4096 and rho=1/32 predicts
4 + 896 + 3078 = 3978 -- while the same formula reproduces an old dump exactly
(2194 against 2193 at seq 18062). So the formula is right and its served
inputs are not what they look like. No reduction plan is worth proposing until
this reconciles, because the lever is rho if m dominates and CLOSE_BLOCK if the
tail does.

c521tail-*: kept against the prompt length's remainder mod CLOSE_BLOCK. The
VKKEPT line showed kept = 4 sinks + rho*closed + (seq - closed), and at the
32768 smoke the tail is exactly 0 because 32768 = 8 * 4096. So every byte
number on this line was measured at the most favourable prompt length there
is. 30720 puts the tail at 2048 (its mean over arbitrary lengths) and 32764 at
4092 (near its maximum). Predicted kept: 1028, ~2944, ~4988. At 32764 the
attended set would be ~5460 rows against DSA's 2048, i.e. the arm reads 2.7x
DSA -- if that holds the byte claim is conditional on the prompt length and has
to be quoted as a range.

c521g-*: the (rho, CLOSE_BLOCK) grid, one variable at a time, at input_len
32764 -- chosen because 32764 // 4096 leaves a tail of 4092, near its maximum,
which is the regime where the byte claim fails (c521tail measured 1.179x DSA
there). A is today's configuration, B moves rho alone to 1/128, C moves the
close block alone to 256, D moves both, and c521g-D-32768 repeats D at the
multiple-of-4096 length so D is comparable with every earlier number.

Theory being tested (kept = sinks + rho*closed + tail, kept's per-token cost
is exactly rho*1024 plus the tail's): fixed-only ratio vs DSA at 32k should go
A 1.334, B 1.102, C 0.735, D 0.488. If C and D do not move, the close block
did not reach the kernel -- the VKKEPT line prints the resolved value and the
kept count, so that is checkable rather than assumed.

Every row carries a distinct VK_JOB: the runner keys server reuse on
(arm, env, server_args), so the two c521tail rows shared a server and their
VKSTATS accumulated across both lengths. Those two are byte/kept measurements
only, not cost -- STATS is on.

c521L-*: the same two configurations over a LONG decode (4k in, 32k out), to
exercise the term the 256-step smoke cannot see. The grid showed why: a 32764
prompt leaves closed=28672, so the FIRST decode close fires at step 4, aligns
closed to 32768 and collapses the tail -- kept goes 4992 -> 1028 and stays
there, which is why the per-call mean read 1162 against a written 4992, and why
seq/call read 31750 against a true 32896 (the counter also sums the prefill
chunks). In that window rho is the only knob that moves anything: B and D both
read 0.646 and C alone reads 0.930, worse than A, because fewer kept groups
lower the q0.75 threshold and fetch rose to 658.

Over 32768 decode steps the tail cycles 0..CLOSE_BLOCK-1 eight times at B=4096
and 128 times at B=256, so its steady-state cost (mean B/2 rows) is exercised.
Prediction: A degrades as the tail grows, D does not, so D's advantage should
be LARGER here than the 0.889 -> 0.646 the short smoke showed.

c521q-D-mk3-*: quality for the D configuration (rho 1/128, CLOSE_BLOCK 256) on
niah_multikey_3 at 65536, n=50, seed 0 -- the draw that separated arms on this
line, where DSA reads 1.000 and the branch arm at rho 1/32 read 1.000 four
times while the sketch arm read 0.540/0.660. TWO draws, because one is not
quotable.

rho 1/128 cuts tier 1 to a quarter of its rows, and the offline screen put
tier-1's own recall of the true top-2048 at 0.086 against 0.137 at rho 1/32.
The DSA fence is a floor only where it fires, and it fires nowhere here
(fallback 0.00000 on every grid arm), so this is a real quality question and
not a formality. At 65536 the prompt is 16*4096, so the tail is zero at both
close blocks and the only variable is rho.

c521L-r64 / r256 / r512: the rho knee on a long decode, at CLOSE_BLOCK 256.
Two points are measured so far -- rho 1/32 with B 4096 reads 1.280x DSA (kept
2622, fetch 138) and rho 1/128 with B 256 reads 0.526x (kept 287, fetch 431) --
giving an elasticity of 0.126 fetch rows per kept row given up. kept's own
structure says the knee is near 1/128: with B 256 the mean tail is 128 rows, so
the tail is 30% of kept at 1/128, 46% at 1/256 and 63% at 1/512, and rho stops
buying much once the tail dominates. These three bracket it, and they also say
whether the elasticity stays benign below 1/128 or turns over.

For TIME none of this matters on this box: 2760 -> 718 attended rows is worth
about 3 us of a 10830 us step at the attention kernel's measured row
sensitivity, so rho is a byte knob, not a latency knob.

c521q-rho128 / cb256 / rho64: which knob destroyed retrieval. The D
configuration (rho 1/128 + CLOSE_BLOCK 256) scored **0.000 on both draws** of
niah_multikey_3 at 65536, n=50, against 1.000 for the served rho 1/32 -- same
harness, same task, no traceback, and both overrides confirmed in the log
("rho overridden to 0.0078125", "close block overridden to 256"). That is a
collapse, not a degradation, and D moves two things at once: rho, and the
window sigma's low-pass basis is built over (31 dims of T, so T=256 is a
different statistic than T=4096). These three separate them -- rho alone, close
block alone, and a milder rho -- so the byte result (0.526x on a long decode)
can be attributed to a knob we are allowed to keep, or not at all.

c521q-cb2048 / cb1024: where sigma survives a smaller close block. The window
IS the close block and kappa is fixed at 16, so the low-pass cutoff is
window/kappa and moves WITH it: 256 tokens at B=4096 (the designed value), 128
at 2048, 64 at 1024, 16 at 256 -- and the 31-dim basis occupies 3% of the
window at 4096 against 48% at 256. kappa cannot compensate: a 256-token cutoff
in a 256-token window needs kappa=1, which collapses the basis to its DC term.

That is a complete mechanism for the 0.000, and it says CLOSE_BLOCK carries two
roles that should be independent -- the close cadence, which sets the tail, and
the spectral window, which sets what sigma means. Closing every 256 tokens
while computing sigma over a trailing 4096-token window is the structural fix;
blockwise_sigma consuming non-overlapping blocks is why they are tied. These
two points say how far the cadence can come down before sigma stops working,
which bounds the tail fix until that refactor exists.

c521q-noop-mk3 / noopcb-mk3: is the override PATH broken rather than the
values? rho 1/128 alone and CLOSE_BLOCK 256 alone each scored 0.000 against
1.000 for the served default -- two different knobs, exactly 0.000, no needle
found in 50 samples, no traceback, both overrides confirmed in the log. A
quality gradient does not usually look like that, and method rule 2 says a null
is not evidence until the actuator is traced. These two set the override to the
DEFAULT value (rho 0.03125, CLOSE_BLOCK 4096), so the resolution code runs and
nothing else changes. 1.000 means the values are the story; 0.000 means the
path is, and every rho/close-block number measured so far is void.

c521q-tree-mk3: the control I should have run first. The reference 1.000 on
niah_multikey_3 is c521br7-mk3, which ENDED AT 08:45:09 -- before the sketch
purge, the dangling-import fix, the restored kept append, the two-arm attention
kernel and three stats/knob commits. Every 0.000 in the rho/close-block ladder
is the first multikey draw on the purged tree, so the ladder was attributing to
a knob what may be a tree regression. done-rows-outlive-rule-changes says
exactly this and I ignored it.

This row's env is byte-identical to c521br7-mk3's: no override, no VK_JOB. The
only difference from the 1.000 is the tree. 1.000 means the knobs are the
story after all; 0.000 means quality broke somewhere in today's commits and
every knob number is void -- with the two-arm kernel the prime suspect, since
it rewrote the row-source block while the byte counters (which come from the
backend, not the kernel) kept reproducing.

c521b1-revkernel-mk3: quality bisect step 1. c521q-tree-mk3 -- the current tree
with NO override and an env byte-identical to the 1.000 reference -- scored
0.000, so the regression is in today commits and every knob number is void.
Three of them change behaviour: the two-arm attention kernel (eae7c1c319), the
sketch purge (ef926f61cc) and the restored kept append (b6424893db). This row
runs with ONLY the kernel reverted to its value-select version, at HEAD, which
isolates one file instead of checking out a whole tree. The kernel is the prime
suspect because it rewrote the row-source block while the byte counters that
kept reproducing come from the backend, not from it: a kernel reading the wrong
rows gives exactly correct counts and zero score.

the DSA path never set it False -- the read was removed only from the two-arm

kernel at HEAD and still read 0.000, so the kernel is not the culprit. This
step checks out the whole python tree at eae7c1c319 -- the two-arm kernel
present, the sketch purge NOT yet applied -- so the sketch tier, the MLA
backend and the deleted modules are all back and the backend is the pre-purge
one. Env is byte-identical to the 1.000 reference. 1.000 here puts the
regression in the purge (ef926f61cc) or in what followed it, with the restored
kept append (b6424893db) next; 0.000 would mean the regression predates the
kernel commit and the reference itself needs re-examining.

NOTE: the served tree is in a TEMPORARY state for this row -- an old commit
checked out over python/sglang, uncommitted. It must be restored to HEAD
afterwards; dsa_rows.py is a harmless leftover since the pre-purge backend
imports rows_for_layer from tier_decode.

c521fix-mk3: the regression fixed. The bisect landed on the purge, and the
mechanism is that an eager decode step takes neither the split path nor the
router (split_step requires capture mode; the eager branch pops
router.rows[lid] by design) and reads this layer's packed CSR instead -- a pack
the purge deleted. After the purge those buffers were written only at capture,
from the dummy batch. Every byte and kept number reproduced because they were
all taken at GRAPH_BS=1, where bs is always captured and the fork serves every
step; RULER runs at GRAPH_BS=4 and the uncaptured sizes go eager.
_refresh_graph_bufs, _pack_csr and _dense_rows' caller are restored unchanged.
This row must read 1.000.

{"args": {"lengths": "65536", "n": 50, "seed": 0, "tasks": "niah_multikey_3"}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "ruler", "env": {"CHUNK": "1024", "CTX": "73728", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "4", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "4", "MAX_REQS": "4", "MEM_FRAC": "0.955", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_BRANCH_Q": "0.75"}, "id": "c521fix-mk3", "probe": false}

c521rev-mk3: the purge reverted. SGLANG_ENABLE_VESTIGEKV_INGRAPH_SCAN defaults
True, so the in-graph pack was built and _ingraph_host_step ran every step --
the branch arm's production path -- and it called _stage_step, which fills the
fixed-address staging buffers the captured graph reads. The purge deleted that
path as sketch machinery; with _ingraph_pack permanently None the staging
buffers kept their initial contents (slots all trash_slot) and every captured
step attended the trash slot's kept table. The byte counters never read those
buffers, which is why they reproduced for ten hours while attention was wrong.
python/sglang is back at eae7c1c319 whole. This must read 1.000.

c521r-rho64 / rho128: rho's quality, measured for the first time. Every earlier
rho and CLOSE_BLOCK point was taken on the purged tree, where attention read
the trash slot's kept table on every captured step, so the five 0.000 scores
were that hole and said nothing about the knobs. The tree is back at
5a505b9495 and c521rev-mk3 re-anchored it at 1.000 with the same env.

Quality before bytes, per the standing order, and quality before anything on a
changed tree, per byte-counters-cannot-see-wrong-rows: the counters gather by
req_pool_indices and cannot see wrong row ADDRESSES, so only a draw like this
one detects them. At 65536 = 16*4096 the tail is zero, so rho is the only
variable. The close-block knob went away with the revert and will be
re-applied, with its own quality draw, only after rho lands.

c521r-rho256 / rho512 / rho128-smoke / rho128-long / base-long: rho's byte
payoff, now that its quality is known. rho 1/64 and 1/128 both read 1.000 on
niah_multikey_3 at 65536 against the 1.000 anchor, on the restored tree -- so
the five 0.000 scores earlier today were the purge's hole and said nothing
about rho. kept's per-token cost is exactly rho*1024 B, so 1/32 -> 1/128 takes
it from 32 to 8 B/token.

Quality first again: rho256 and rho512 find where it breaks. Then the bytes at
1/128, measured twice -- a 32k smoke where the prompt is an exact multiple of
CLOSE_BLOCK and the tail is zero, and a 4k/32k long decode where the tail
cycles and dominates, with a matching rho 1/32 long decode for the ratio. The
earlier 1.280x and 0.526x readings of those two shapes are void.

c521t1q-*: the probe that can give rho a lower bound. The rho ladder read
1.000 at 1/32, 1/64, 1/128, 1/256 AND 1/512 -- a 16x range with kept going
2052 -> 132 rows and no movement at all. That is a NON-DISCRIMINATION, not a
demonstration of equality: niah_multikey_3 at n=50 cannot resolve tier 1's
size, the same way MRCR cannot resolve admission width. It does catch gross
failures (it put the sketch arm at 0.540/0.660), so the reading is that tier 2
does essentially all the retrieving, which matches tier 1's own offline recall
of the true top-2048 at 0.09-0.14.

So these two turn tier 2 OFF -- BRANCH_Q unset, which makes group_fetch return
on its first line (actuator traced) -- and ask what kept ALONE retrieves, at
the served rho and at 1/128. A gap between them is the first evidence that
could bound rho from below; two 1.000s would say multikey cannot see kept at
all and a different probe is needed before any rho is chosen.

c521lb-same-r1/r2: the noise floor that makes longbench1 readable. Needle
retrieval cannot see tier 1 at all -- multikey_3 reads 1.000 for rho 1/32
through 1/512 and tier 1 ALONE reads 0.000 -- so a probe that could bound kept
has to need broad context. Long-document summarisation is the shape on hand,
and scoring the predictions already on disk (mexp/glm53/score_longbench1.py)
put the two arms' ROUGE-L agreement at 0.395 (gov_report), 0.539 (multi_news)
and 0.588 (qmsum): the arms write visibly different text, unlike the needle
draw where every arm is 1.000.

That is not yet evidence, because this line's decode is nondeterministic -- an
arm re-run identically agrees with itself on 48% of greedy tokens
([[vestigekv-decode-path-is-nondeterministic]]). These two runs are the SAME
arm twice on multi_news, so their agreement IS the floor. If it lands near
0.54, longbench1 is noise here and cannot bound kept any better than multikey.
If it lands well above, longbench1 is the first probe that can see tier 1, and
a tier-1-only run of it becomes worth the GPU.

{"args": {"max_length": 65536, "subsets": "multi_news"}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "longbench1", "env": {"CHUNK": "1024", "CTX": "73728", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "1", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "1", "MAX_REQS": "1", "MEM_FRAC": "0.955", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_BRANCH_Q": "0.75", "VK_JOB": "c521lb-same-r1"}, "id": "c521lb-same-r1", "probe": false, "skip": false}
{"args": {"max_length": 65536, "subsets": "multi_news"}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "longbench1", "env": {"CHUNK": "1024", "CTX": "73728", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "1", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "1", "MAX_REQS": "1", "MEM_FRAC": "0.955", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_BRANCH_Q": "0.75", "VK_JOB": "c521lb-same-r2"}, "id": "c521lb-same-r2", "probe": false, "skip": false}

c521lb-same-r3: the noise-floor run that r2 destroyed. longbench1's client
names its files pred_{arm}_{task}.jsonl inside the --out directory, and the
runner passed one arm-keyed directory for every job, so two jobs of the same
arm silently overwrote each other -- and both overwrote the Sep-22 vestigekv
predictions that the 0.539 arm-vs-arm figure came from, which is therefore no
longer reproducible. The audit's existing-output check does not see this
because the path carries no job id.

The runner now passes results/glm53/longbench1/<job id> as --out, so each job
is isolated; score_longbench1.py takes --pair to compare two pred files
directly. r2's output is preserved under a job-keyed name. r3 re-runs the same
arm so the pair exists again.

{"args": {"max_length": 65536, "subsets": "multi_news"}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "longbench1", "env": {"CHUNK": "1024", "CTX": "73728", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "1", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "1", "MAX_REQS": "1", "MEM_FRAC": "0.955", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_BRANCH_Q": "0.75", "VK_JOB": "c521lb-same-r3"}, "id": "c521lb-same-r3", "probe": false, "skip": false}

c521cb-*: the close block, the only knob on the tail. Quality side is
exhausted -- no instrument here resolves tier 1 -- so this is the performance
item that is both unambiguous and independent of the kept question: a long
decode reads 1.351x DSA at the default and still 1.174x at rho 1/128, because
~2048 of kept's 2190 rows are un-closed tail and rho cannot touch it.

Order follows the day's most expensive lesson. c521cb-inert-mk3 sets the knob to
its DEFAULT 4096, so the re-application itself is tested before any value is
read: 1.000 means the threading is inert, anything else means I broke something
again and no knob number would mean anything. Then 2048 (cutoff 128 tokens,
basis 6% of the window) and 1024 (64 tokens, 12%). 256 is not queued: its basis
occupies 48% of a 64-group window and its cutoff is 16 tokens, which is a
different statistic rather than a smaller one.

Bytes come after quality, on the 4k/32k long decode where the tail cycles.

c521tl-*: what the tail is worth, on the long decode where it cycles. The close
block passed quality at 2048 and 1024 (and inert at its default), so these four
measure the byte payoff: 4k in / 32k out, STATS on, ordered best-candidate
first so the decisive number lands earliest.

Already measured on this shape: B 4096 reads 1.351x DSA at rho 1/32 and 1.174x
at rho 1/128, with ~2048 of kept's 2190 rows being un-closed tail. Halving the
close block should halve that term -- about 28 B/token against DSA's ~49 B/token
row budget at this length -- so B 1024 x rho 1/128 is the first configuration
with a chance of reading BELOW 1 on a long decode. Whether fetch compensates is
not predictable: rho's elasticity measured 0.11 here, while CLOSE_BLOCK pushed
fetch +184 on a short window.

The quality caveat travels with these numbers: multikey cannot resolve tier 1
(tier 1 ALONE reads 0.000 on it), and sigma's selection IS tier 1, so 1.000 at
B 1024 means the metric cannot tell, not that sigma still works. Both knobs'
quality evidence is "not refuted", not "verified".

tier-2 chain) at the two batched shapes that fit the pool. It is a COST
CONTROL, never a method. At bs=1 it splits the +0.22 ms/step gap in half
(tier-1 0.114 ms, tier-2 0.109 ms). The batched gap is +0.67 to +0.77 ms, so
the question these answer is whether that triple is still half tier-1 and
half tier-2 -- if it is, both halves grow with the lane count and batching is
not a lever on the tax. Same launch as the matching -branch row with
SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_INDEXER_ONLY=1 added, so only that one variable moves.


c521tl RESULT -- the tail and rho together, all six points at the full 32750
steps, fallback 0.00000 on every arm (4k in / 32k out, STATS on):

    config                    kept   fetch    rows   ratio vs DSA
    B4096 rho1/32 (served)  2621.4   332.4  2953.8    1.351
    B4096 rho1/128          2189.7   283.1  2472.8    1.174
    B2048 rho1/32           1630.1   392.3  2022.4    1.007
    B1024 rho1/32           1134.5   457.9  1592.4    0.848
    B2048 rho1/128          1174.5   217.5  1392.0    0.774
    B1024 rho1/128           666.9   290.4   957.3    0.614

The prediction above held: B 1024 x rho 1/128 is the first configuration below
1 on a long decode, at 0.614x -- a 2.2x reduction from the served
configuration. BOTH knobs are needed: they hold different terms of
kept = sinks + rho*closed + tail, and alone they reach only 1.174 and 0.848.

Fetch did compensate, partially and unpredictably, exactly as flagged. At rho
1/32 it rises monotonically as the close block shrinks (332 -> 392 -> 458); at
rho 1/128 it does not (283 -> 218 -> 290). The q0.75 threshold is taken over
the kept GROUPS' logits, so changing the kept set's composition moves the
threshold in a direction neither knob predicts alone, and the optimum is
therefore not at a corner of the grid.

On the aligned short shape (32768 prompt, 256 steps) the tail is zero at every
close block, so the ratio is rho's alone: 0.879 at 1/32, 0.645 at 1/128. So
rho 1/128 + CLOSE_BLOCK 1024 is below 1 on BOTH shapes.

RECOMMENDATION: serve rho 1/128 with CLOSE_BLOCK 1024. It is the only grid
point under 1 on both shapes, it keeps the close block at 1024 where sigma's
low-pass cutoff is still 64 tokens and the 31-dim basis is 3% of the window,
and it is the largest close block that gets there.

SUPERSEDED, do not quote: the c521L-r* annotation above reads "rho 1/32 with B
4096 reads 1.280x (kept 2622, fetch 138)" and "rho 1/128 with B 256 reads
0.526x (kept 287, fetch 431)". The first is a mid-run VKSTATS dump of the run
that finished at 1.351; the second is at CLOSE_BLOCK 256, which scored 0.000 on
niah_multikey_3 and is not a configuration we may keep. The elasticity of 0.126
rows derived from that pair is void with it.

RETRACTED: the two analyses built on the 0.984 mid-run figure for B2048 x rho
1/32 -- that fetch rose 2.65x and ate the gain, and that equal kept with
different sigma windows doubles fetch. That run finished at 1.007 with fetch
392.3, and neither inference survives the final number. Mid-run VKSTATS lines
are not results: this pair read 0.928 at step 14050 and 1.007 at 32750.

c521def-*: the new served default, verified as a default. engine db1387723f
makes rho 1/128 and close block 1024 this arm's compiled-in pair (DSA_RHO /
DSA_CLOSE_BLOCK), scoped so vestigekv_mla -- which reads RHO and CLOSE_BLOCK
directly and has no override -- keeps the values the whole Kimi record was
collected at. RHO and CLOSE_BLOCK stay the sizing ceilings, so the previous
served pair is still expressible, which is what -ctrl-L exercises.

QUALITY FIRST, and not as a formality: the PAIR has never been drawn on
multikey. rho 1/128 alone read 1.000 and close block 1024 alone read 1.000, but
rho 1/128 together with close block 256 read 0.000 on both draws -- so a joint
effect is exactly what is unmeasured here. Two draws, because one is not
quotable. The caveat still stands either way: tier 1 ALONE reads 0.000 on this
task, so 1.000 means the metric cannot resolve what these knobs move.

Then three byte checks, which are also the only actuator evidence that the
default reaches the kernel rather than merely being constructed:
  c521def-L      4k/32k, no knob env  -- must reproduce 0.614x
  c521def-S      32768/256, no knob env -- must reproduce 0.645x (the tail is
                 zero at both 1024 and 4096, so this isolates rho; reading
                 0.879 would mean the default did not take effect)
  c521def-ctrl-L the previous pair forced back through the env overrides --
                 must reproduce 1.351x, proving the raised guards still admit
                 a control at the sizing ceiling

c521def RESULT -- the new default verified as a default, and one correction.

    job              rho    CB     kept    fetch   ratio   quality
    c521def-mk3-r1   1/128  1024      -        -       -   1.000
    c521def-mk3-r2   1/128  1024      -        -       -   1.000
    c521def-L        1/128  1024   666.9    273.2   0.607      -
    c521def-S        1/128  1024   367.0    654.3   0.688      -
    c521def-ctrl-L   1/32   4096  2621.4    324.5   1.348      -

fallback 0.00000 on all three byte jobs. Median ITL 12.33-12.34 ms and p99
13.07-13.78 ms across all three, i.e. flat -- these are STATS-on byte jobs and
carry no cost claim either way.

ACTUATOR TRACE, which is the part a log line does not give for free: c521def-L
and -S emitted NO override lines (correct, no env was set) and their VKKEPT
lines read rho=0.0078125 CLOSE_BLOCK=1024, so the compiled-in pair is what the
kernel received. c521def-ctrl-L emitted both override lines, now correctly
reading "(default 1024)" and "(default 0.0078125)", and its VKKEPT read
rho=0.03125 CLOSE_BLOCK=4096 -- so the guards, raised to the sizing ceilings,
still admit the previous served pair as a control. Engine 72d0c6c2cb now logs
the effective pair unconditionally, because before it a run on the compiled-in
pair left no record of what the kernel was given.

The long decode reproduced: 0.607 against the grid's 0.614, and the control
1.348 against 1.351. kept is exact to the row in both (666.9, 2621.4); fetch
spreads ~6% between runs, so the ratio carries ~0.01 of replicate noise and
0.614 vs 0.607 is one point measured twice.

CORRECTION, affecting a number recorded above: the c521tl block says the
aligned short shape "is rho's alone" because the tail is zero at every close
block, and attaches 0.645x to the served pair. The ratio there is 0.688x. A
zero tail means the block buys no kept rows, not that it does nothing -- the
block is also sigma's window, so it changes WHICH rows tier 1 keeps and moves
the tier-2 threshold regardless. Measured at this shape, kept is flat (379.2 at
a 4096 block vs 367.0 at 1024) while fetch rises 29% (507.4 -> 654.3), and the
0.645x belongs to rho 1/128 at a 4096 block, not to the served pair.

So the recommendation stands on the balance, not on dominance: the long decode
gains 1.174x -> 0.607x where this shape gives up 0.645x -> 0.688x, and both
stay under 1x. Any byte ratio quoted from here must name its shape.

QUALITY: both draws of the PAIR read 1.000, which was the open question -- each
knob alone had read 1.000, but rho 1/128 together with a 256 block read 0.000
twice. The standing caveat is unchanged: tier 1 ALONE reads 0.000 on this task,
so 1.000 means the metric cannot resolve what these knobs move.

c521cp-* / c521cpref-dsa: the graded probe for tier 1, because no probe on this
line resolves it. multikey, gsm8k and longbench1 all fail -- tier 1 ALONE reads
0.000 on multikey at every rho while the served arm reads 1.000 -- so kept has
no measured lower bound and rho is being chosen on bytes alone.

A needle needs ONE archived row and tier 2 can always fetch it. Reproducing a
384-word span verbatim needs a contiguous RUN of rows, a new position every
decode step, which is the work the kept set is supposed to do. And the score is
GRADED ("copied 137 of 384 words before derailing"), which is what ranking
configurations requires and what 1.000-or-0.000 cannot give. The span carries
~105 arbitrary numbers, scored separately, because fluent paraphrase can carry
word accuracy and cannot invent digits.

mexp/glm53/run_copy.py: 32768-token context, span at 9% through (deep in the
archive, past the sinks, far from the recent window), n=20, seed 0, prompts a
pure function of (seed, index) so every arm sees identical bytes. Verified
offline before launch: a perfect copy scores 1.000, a half copy 0.500 (numbers
0.410), a copy behind a preface still 1.000, a refusal 0.000.

Filler AND span are natural prose from one corpus, deliberately. Synthetic
filler would measure the DSA fence, which owns that regime; and a random word
salad as the span would be maximally anomalous, so tier 1 would preferentially
keep it and the test would pass for a reason unrelated to ordinary coverage.

Ordered so the decisive runs land first:
  c521cpref-dsa   the ceiling. If DSA cannot copy, the workload measures nothing
                  and the rest is void -- ten minutes to find that out. Its own
                  stem, so the audit does not read it as the ladder's control.
  c521cp-t1-r32   tier 1 ALONE at rho 1/32, then
  c521cp-t1-def   tier 1 ALONE at rho 1/128. These two are the INSTRUMENT test:
                  on multikey both rho read 0.000, a floor that cannot move. If
                  this score moves with rho, the copy task reads kept's coverage
                  directly and kept can finally be bounded. If it does not, this
                  probe is blind too and joins the list.
  c521cp-def      the served pair, then rho 1/32 and rho 1/512 at the same close
  c521cp-r32      block -- a 16x span in kept with everything else fixed. If the
  c521cp-r512     served arm is flat across that, tier 2 covers the copy as well
                  and kept has no lower bound this instrument can set either.

c521sp-*: the speedup at the new default, which is NOT on disk. All three
c521def byte jobs ran with STATS on and may not be timed. The only valid
same-tree pair is c521z, on the OLD default: median ITL 11.06 vs DSA 10.83
(0.979x), p99 11.69 vs 11.09, tput 78.53 vs 80.63.

Same launch as c521z (32k in / 4k out, GRAPH_BS=MAX_REQS=1, STATS OFF), both
arms, TWO draws -- the baseline's own median spans ~1.09% across runs while the
gap is +2.1%, so one pair is barely two noise widths. The two arms of a draw
share VK_JOB so their envs are identical and the audit's pair guard actually
checks them; the draws differ in it so each gets a fresh server, which is part
of what the replicate is measuring.

Prediction, on the record so it can be wrong: unchanged at ~0.98x. The byte
saving is 1.09 MB/step against a step that moves several GB of weights, so it
is ~0.02% of the step against a +2.1% fixed cost. Bytes are not time at bs=1.

c521cp-t1b-*: the tier-1-alone floor, correctly specified. The c521cp-t1-* pair
above does NOT measure it and must not be read as the floor.

_indexer_only() is an OR: SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_INDEXER_ONLY=1 or BRANCH_Q set. What
it gates is the CERTIFICATE tier 2 (_recall_step returns early); the branch-rule
tier 2 is gated separately, on BRANCH_Q itself. Every job on this line carries
BRANCH_Q=0.75, so _indexer_only() is already True on the served arm and adding
INDEXER_ONLY=1 flips a value that was true anyway. c521cp-t1-r32 and
c521cp-t1-def are therefore byte-identical in behaviour to c521cp-r32 and
c521cp-def -- useful as free replicates of the served arm, and nothing else.

Turning tier 2 off means REMOVING BranchQ and setting INDEXER_ONLY, which is
what the older c521t1q-* pair did (no BRANCH_Q in their env) and why their
0.000 on multikey is a real tier-1-alone reading. These two repeat that
configuration on the copy task at both rho.

Note for the record, not acted on here: the branch rule -- the only GLM version
this line is allowed to publish -- is switched on by an ENV VAR, not by a
compiled default (BRANCH_Q defaults to None, which selects the certificate
path). So "the served arm" is still half a launch-time construction even after
DSA_RHO and DSA_CLOSE_BLOCK became compiled defaults.
