c521cp / c521sp RESULT.

COPY, n=20, ctx 32768, 384-word span at 9%, seed 0:

    arm                 rho     kept@32k   lead     bag     num
    DSA (ceiling)        -           -   1.0000  1.0000  1.0000
    served             1/32        1028   1.0000  1.0000  1.0000
    served            1/128         260   1.0000  1.0000  1.0000
    served            1/512          68   1.0000  1.0000  1.0000
    tier 1 ALONE       1/32        1028   0.0017  0.0306  0.0157
    tier 1 ALONE      1/128         260   0.0000  0.0173  0.0146

(c521cp-t1-r32 and -t1-def also read 1.0000; they are the served arm, as the
note above explains, and serve as free replicates of r32 and def.)

The instrument has FULL range -- 1.0000 against 0.0000 -- and was verified
before launch to read intermediate values. So the served arm's flatness across
16x in kept is real flatness, not a metric that cannot tell, and that is the
distinction every earlier probe here failed on. Actuator verified per arm from
the unconditional tier-1 log line: rho=0.03125 / 0.0078125 / 0.00195312,
close_block=1024 throughout.

CONCLUSION: kept has no lower bound, and this time as a positive finding. 68
kept rows out of 32768 still reproduce a 384-word span exactly, every number
included. Tier 2 does all the retrieving. The floor moves slightly with rho
(bag 0.031 -> 0.017), the direction coverage predicts, but both are floor
readings with no replicate -- do not build on that difference.

COST at the served default, 32k in / 4k out, bs=1, STATS off (verified: 0
VKSTATS lines), actuator verified (rho=0.0078125 close_block=1024):

    draw  arm         mean   median   p90    p99    tok/s
    r1    DSA        10.83   10.83   10.96  11.33   80.55
    r1    vestigekv  11.06   11.02   11.12  11.41   78.39
    r2    DSA        10.83   10.83   10.92  11.03   80.63
    r2    vestigekv  11.08   11.04   11.15  11.74   78.31

Speedup 0.983 and 0.981 on median, 0.993 and 0.939 on p99, 0.973 and 0.971 on
throughput. The old default's single draw was 0.979 and the 0.02-0.04 ms
difference is inside this arm's own two-draw spread, so the prediction logged
above held: taking the byte ratio from 1.351x to 0.607x changed latency by
nothing. The baseline's two draws are identical on median (10.83, 10.83), so
that is not instability in the reference.

Why, structurally: DSA reads a CONSTANT index_topk=2048 rows per request per
step, so the entire prize is 2048 rows. Measured row sensitivity from the
matched pair c521def-L (940 rows) vs -ctrl-L (2946 rows) -- same shape, same
STATS overhead -- is <= 5 ns/row (2006 rows moved the median 0.00 ms, the mean
0.01 ms), so the whole budget is worth <= 10 us against a 230 us tax. A factor
of 23, with 940 rows (<= 4.7 us) of headroom left. Compression is not a latency
lever against DSA; dispatch is, and the tax is ~100 launches of it.

Our rows grow as 4 + S/128 + tail + fetch while DSA's are flat, crossing at
S ~ 158k, past which we read MORE than DSA.

NEXT, not queued: dense_mla.sh exists and has never been run on a stream shape.
Against a dense baseline the saving is unbounded in context -- 178 us at
S=36864, 386 us at S=78784 (the pool limit at bs=1) against the 230 us tax -- so
it should cross 1 near 50-60k. Two jobs, ~50 min, and the only cheap experiment
on this line that can produce a number above 1.

c521dn-*: vestigekv against a DENSE substrate, the one comparison on this line
that can produce a speedup above 1. dense_mla.sh (DSA off at the config level,
stock triton MLA) has existed unrun since the arm was written.

Why it can cross where DSA cannot: DSA reads a CONSTANT index_topk=2048 rows, so
the entire prize against it is 2048 rows, measured at <= 10 us against a 230 us
tax (c521sp, c521def-L vs -ctrl-L). Dense reads S rows, so the prize grows
without bound in context.

The prediction is a RANGE, not a crossing, and that is the point of this pair.
Row sensitivity is only bounded, not resolved: c521def-L vs -ctrl-L moved 2006
rows for <= 0.01 ms, so the slope is anywhere in 0 .. 5 ns/row, while physics
puts it near 1024 B / 1466 GB/s = 0.70 ns/row. At S=36864 dense reads ~35800
rows more than we do, which is 25 us at 0.70 ns/row and 179 us at 5 ns/row,
against the 230 us tax -- i.e. 0.98x either way at THIS shape, but the two ends
imply crossings 7x apart in context. So this pair is run to RESOLVE THE SLOPE
from a large row delta, not to show a win: once the slope is a number, the
crossing context follows without guessing, and a long shape can be aimed at it.

Same launch as c521sp (32k in / 4k out, GRAPH_BS=MAX_REQS=1, STATS OFF), two
draws, dense FIRST in each draw because this arm has never been launched and a
startup failure should cost five minutes. The two arms of a draw share VK_JOB so
their envs are identical and the audit's pair guard checks them.

Note the pools will NOT match: with DSA off there is no index cache, so the
dense arm resolves a different max_total_num_tokens. 36864 tokens fits either
way, but read both logs before comparing anything at a longer shape.

c521pf-*: attribute the 230 us, before touching anything. The static read of the
decode path overturned my own framing and could not replace it.

What it overturned: decode IS cuda-graph captured here (decode backend "full",
cuda_graph_max_bs_decode=1), so launches inside the graph cost nothing and "the
tax is ~100 kernel launches" was wrong. What the read also ruled out: _stage_step
is three copies at bs=1, and _ingraph_host_step's pairs/tiers loop is epoch-gated
and skipped on a steady step. And it found a saving already banked --
_out_graph_metadata_lite skips the base's ~99 us/step dense kv_indices fill, so
the +230 us is NET and the gross host cost is nearer 330 us.

What is left is three candidates with three different fixes: per-step host work in
_decode_prologue / _step_slots / _maybe_close_blocks; eager ops outside the graph;
and real GPU work inside it (scan, CSR pack, the fork's extra loads). A static
read cannot choose between them, so this pair measures instead:
profile_stream.py drives torch.profiler with CUPTI, which captures kernels inside
graph replays, and kernel_diff.py differences the two arms per kernel. ctxs=36864
to land at the context the 230 us was measured at; steps=200; same launch as
c521sp so the number being attributed is the one already quoted.

{"args": {"ctxs": "36864", "steps": 200}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "profile", "env": {"CHUNK": "512", "CTX": "135168", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "1", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "1", "MAX_REQS": "1", "MEM_FRAC": "0.95", "VK_JOB": "c521pf-r1"}, "id": "c521pf-r1-vestigekv", "probe": false}

c521dn RESULT -- the first speedup above 1 on this line, replicated.

    draw  arm         mean   median   p90    p99    tok/s
    r1    dense_mla  11.78   11.78   11.94  12.35   70.16
    r1    vestigekv  11.08   11.04   11.13  11.44   78.29
    r2    dense_mla  11.79   11.79   11.92  12.14   70.20
    r2    vestigekv  11.07   11.02   11.12  11.44   78.46

Speedup 1.067 and 1.070 on median, 1.080 and 1.061 on p99, 1.116 and 1.118 on
throughput. Both arms reproduce to 0.01-0.02 ms across draws, 4096 output tokens
each, 1 successful request each. The dense arm is verified dense from its own
log: attention_backend 'triton', index_topk null. Its pool resolves to 104607
tokens against the vestigekv arm's 78784 -- expected, there is no index cache --
and 36864 fits either way.

THE ATTRIBUTION IS NOT BYTES, and the registered prediction was wrong in a way
that is worth keeping. It said this shape would read ~0.98x either way, bounding
the byte gain at 179 us. The measured gap is 740 us. The analytic model puts the
dense crossing at S = tau/(L*r*(1-rho)*gamma) = 37,044 tokens, i.e. a predicted
ratio of ~1.000 at S=36864 -- so 750 of the 740 us is not explained by row
counts at all.

Back-solving gamma per arm settles it: the dense triton-MLA arm achieves ~534
GB/s on its rows, while our fork's marginal rows are hidden entirely (c521def-L
vs -ctrl-L moved 2006 rows for <= 0.01 ms, i.e. >= 2.26 TB/s effective). So
gamma is a per-KERNEL constant, not a machine constant, and almost all of this
win is kernel quality rather than compression.

So the claim this pair supports is "our kernel AND fewer rows beat a dense
substrate by 1.067-1.070x at 36k", never "compression buys 1.07x". The same
measurement also explains why the DSA comparison cannot cross: against DSA both
arms run the SAME kernel, so gamma is identical and only dA differs -- and dA is
capped by DSA's constant 2048 rows at <= 12.8 us against a 230 us tax.

c521pf RESULT -- the 230 us attributed, and the fusion plan is dead.

Per-step GPU kernel time: DSA 11626.2 us; vestigekv 12071.9 (TP-0) / 11808.1
(TP-1). TP-0's extra 283 us over TP-1 is entirely ncclDevKernel_AllReduce
(+295.7), which does NOT appear in TP-1's top ten -- so that term is rank 0
WAITING at the collective, double-counting the chain's latency rather than
costing anything of its own. The real added GPU work is +150 to +162 us/step,
consistent with the +230 us wall clock once host time and imperfect overlap are
included.

    kernel                          us/step  calls  grid        MB   achieved  vs roofline
    _vk_dsa_decode_split_kernel    66.8/67.3    11  per layer  10.6  159 GB/s     11.4x
    _scan_batched_kernel           33.4/33.6     1  cap 128    13.4  401 GB/s      4.5x
    _prologue_scores_kernel        32.1/32.4     1  (11,32)     7.5  234 GB/s      7.7x
    _prologue_merge_kernel         30.3/30.6     1  (11,)       7.5  248 GB/s      7.3x
    memcpy32_post                  10.3/10.4    11
    pack/compact/cub                  ~10     1 ea
    DSA's _sparse_mla_decode_split      -76.6   11            23.1  300 GB/s

FUSION IS NOT THE LEVER. The three chain kernels are called ONCE PER STEP each
-- three launches, worth 2-6 us. Their 96 us is real GPU work, so there is no
launch overhead to remove. And the attention fork, despite 11 calls/step, sits
inside the captured graph where launches are free, and it already SAVES 9.8 us
against DSA's own kernel (66.8 vs 76.6).

THE LEVER IS OCCUPANCY. This GPU has 188 SMs and the chain's natural
parallelism at bs=1 is 11 (layer, request) pairs. _prologue_merge_kernel
launches 11 programs -- 5.9% of the GPU -- and costs 30.3 us.
_prologue_scores_kernel is already split 32 ways for exactly this reason (352
programs, 1.9 per SM) and still only reaches 234 GB/s. All three run 4.5-11x off
the bandwidth roofline.

REACHABLE TARGET, and it is not 50 us. The chain must touch 13.4 MB (the shared
33 B scan) + 7.5 MB (kept scoring) ~ 21 MB: 11.7 us at peak, ~26 us at a
realistic 800 GB/s for gather-heavy work, plus ~10 us of pack and misc. So the
GPU floor is ~40 us against today's ~115 us of chain work. 230 -> ~120 us looks
reachable; 230 -> 50 us does not, since 50 would need the GPU work at its floor
AND zero host cost AND perfect overlap.

WHAT tau=120 us BUYS, from the analytic model: the dense crossing moves 37k ->
19k tokens, and the batching requirement moves B=71 -> B=37 against a
pool-allowed 38 -- i.e. the batching route just barely opens. That is the prize,
not the 1% of step time.

CAVEAT that bounds every number here: _router_triton_kernel moved -41.1 us on
TP-0 and +36.0 on TP-1, opposite signs, because the two profiled runs decode
different tokens and route to different experts. No per-kernel attribution below
+-40 us is trustworthy. The chain kernels survive it only because they are new
kernels (0 -> 33), not deltas.

c521kq / c521kp: verify the K-major sketch basis on the served path
(engine 614780e6c9). _prologue_merge_kernel went 34.87 -> 16.15 us offline,
bit-identical on all five outputs, n_spills 0, and the registered reference test
passes against an independent torch implementation.

Two jobs, and they are NOT redundant:

  c521kq-mk3  QUALITY, and it is the load-bearing one. The offline parity test
              covers the KERNEL; it does not cover the host side of the layout
              change -- the allocation in batched_step.py and the transposing
              assignment self.v[i] = t.V.T. A wrong basis there computes garbage
              that every byte counter reports as healthy, which is exactly the
              failure byte-counters-cannot-see-wrong-rows was written about.
              One draw, as a wiring check against a known 1.000, not as a
              comparison.
  c521kp-vk   PROFILE, because ITL cannot see this. The win is ~18 us against
              an arm whose own two-draw median spread is 20 us, so a stream job
              would report noise either way. The trace is diffed against
              c521pf-r1-vestigekv (same shape, same steps) to confirm the kernel
              time dropped in situ, and it also re-measures the other two chain
              kernels, which decides whether steps 2 and 3 have any headroom.

Neither c521pf finding applies to those two: _scan_batched_kernel's dots carry
no input_precision="ieee" and no tl.trans, and its grid is (36, 11) = 396
programs; _prologue_scores_kernel's dot is bf16, where the measurement says an
in-loop trans costs nothing, and its grid is (11, 32) = 352. So the specific
defect fixed here is absent from both, and any headroom they have needs its own
isolated bench -- the roofline figures quoted for them earlier were built on
byte estimates, and the one I made for the merge kernel was wrong by 4x.

{"args": {"ctxs": "36864", "steps": 200}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "profile", "env": {"CHUNK": "512", "CTX": "135168", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "1", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "1", "MAX_REQS": "1", "MEM_FRAC": "0.95", "VK_JOB": "c521kp-vk"}, "id": "c521kp-vk", "probe": false}

c521kq / c521kp RESULT -- step 1 verified; step 2 is not what it looked like.

QUALITY WIRING: c521kq-mk3 niah_multikey_3 @65536 n=50 seed 0 = 1.000. That is
the only detector for the host half of the K-major change (the allocation and
the transposing assignment self.v[i] = t.V.T); a wrong basis there computes
garbage every byte counter reports as healthy.

IN SITU, diffed against c521pf-r1-vestigekv at the same shape and steps:

    kernel                        before  after    delta
    _prologue_merge_kernel          30.3   10.6    -19.8
    _scan_batched_kernel            33.4   32.9     -0.5
    _prologue_scores_kernel         32.1   32.0     -0.1
    _vk_dsa_decode_split_kernel     66.8   66.9     +0.1

Surgical: the target dropped 19.8 us (offline bench predicted 18.7) and nothing
else moved. The chain is now ~95 us against ~115.

BUT THE STEP IS NOT MEASURABLY FASTER, and that was predicted here before the
run. Total GPU time in the same diff went UP +54.7 us: a cutlass GEMM whose
CALL COUNT differs between the two profiles (3.0 -> 6.0, +57.8 us) and the
nccl wait term (+77.7). Both are the +-40 us/kernel noise this register already
records, worse here because a call count changed. So the claim is "the kernel is
19.8 us cheaper", never "the step is faster" -- at 18 us against an arm whose
own two-draw median spread is 20 us, no stream job can see this.

STEP 2 FINDINGS, and two corrections I owe.

Neither remaining chain kernel has step 1's defect: _scan_batched_kernel's dots
carry no input_precision="ieee" and no tl.trans, grid (36,11)=396;
_prologue_scores_kernel's dot is bf16, where an in-loop trans measures free,
grid (11,32)=352.

Recomputing the scan's real traffic from the buffers it loads -- side[64] bf16 +
csk[64] + rho/arch/aidx/spread per archive entry, ~272 B per entry of 4 tokens
-- gives ~27 MB/step and ~817 GB/s achieved, 45% of peak. NOT the 401 GB/s and
4.5x headroom quoted earlier in this register: that figure came from a byte
estimate, and the same kind of estimate was wrong by 4x for the merge kernel.
Treat the earlier roofline column for the scan and the scores kernel as void.

I then hypothesised the whole sketch chain (prologue + scan + compacts, ~86
us/step) was DEAD on the branch arm, since group_fetch computes its rows from
DSA's logits and never reads max1g/qsk_t/qres, and VKSTATS reports build=0.0ms
x0. MY OWN MEASUREMENT REFUTES IT: the merge kernel's q-side projection only
runs when a_len > 0 (it returns at the top otherwise), and step 1 moved that
projection 30.3 -> 10.6 us in situ. So a_len > 0, tiers exist, and the chain is
doing real work. build x0 counts the calibrated/async builds only; the
provisional index is a separate path.

(Also: an earlier claim in this investigation that st["tier"] is only ever
assigned None was a bad grep -- it misses the tuple assignments at :3250 and
:3446.)

What remains open and is worth one instrumented run, not more code reading: the
sketch chain's compacts write fetch_buf/fetch_len, and branch_fire writes the
same buffers every step. Which one the fork actually consumes on the branch arm,
and whether the other is redundant, decides whether ~86 us/step is recoverable.
Served behaviour will not be changed on the strength of inference that has
already been wrong twice here.

c521sk-*: is the sketch chain the redundant half? engine 8b27109864 adds
SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_DISABLE_SKETCH_CHAIN (default OFF, served behaviour untouched)
which skips the fused prologue, the batched scan and the compaction, leaving
branch_fire and the CSR pack.

The question: on the branch arm the compaction writes fetch_buf/fetch_len and
branch_fire writes the same buffers every step, so one of the two is redundant,
and the chain costs ~86 us/step at 36k by CUPTI -- more than half the +150 us of
added GPU work, and more than step 1 recovered.

  c521sk-mk3  QUALITY is the detector. 1.000 means the chain is the redundant
              half and ~86 us/step is recoverable. Anything below means the
              chain is load-bearing and the knob goes back in its box. This is
              the ONLY sound detector: VKSTATS gathers fetch_len on the host
              after the step, so it reports whichever write landed last and
              cannot say which one the fork consumed.
  c521sk-pf   PROFILE is the actuator trace, and a better one than a log line:
              the prologue, scan and compact kernels must be ABSENT from the
              trace. If they are still there the gate did not reach the capture.

Why a knob and not a default change: inference has already been wrong twice
here. I read build=0.0ms x0 as "no tier" when it counts only the calibrated
builds, and I concluded the chain was dead until my own K-major measurement
refuted it -- the q-side projection only runs when a_len > 0, and it moved
30.3 -> 10.6 us, so tiers exist and the chain does real work. Real work whose
output may still be overwritten, which is exactly what these two jobs settle.

{"args": {"ctxs": "36864", "steps": 200}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "profile", "env": {"CHUNK": "512", "CTX": "135168", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "1", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "1", "MAX_REQS": "1", "MEM_FRAC": "0.95", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_DISABLE_SKETCH_CHAIN": "1", "VK_JOB": "c521sk-pf"}, "id": "c521sk-pf", "probe": false}

c521sk-cp / c521sk-mk3b / c521sx-*: the sketch chain IS removable -- 80.2 us/step
by CUPTI -- and these decide whether that is free.

What c521sk already showed. Quality 1.000 on multikey with
SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_DISABLE_SKETCH_CHAIN=1, and the actuator trace is unambiguous:
_scan_batched_kernel 32.9 -> 0, _prologue_scores_kernel 32.0 -> 0,
_prologue_merge_kernel 10.6 -> 0, _compact_write 3.4 -> 0, _compact_scan 1.3 ->
0, and "only in a" lists exactly those five. The CSR pack kernels stay, which is
right -- the fork reads them. 80.2 us of the ~150 us of added GPU work.

What it did NOT show, and why these jobs exist. The chain's compaction and
branch_fire write the SAME fetch_buf/fetch_len, so "the chain was dead" and "the
chain's rows were being attended and branch_fire's are just as good" both
produce 1.000 on multikey -- a metric that reads 1.000 at every rho from 1/32 to
1/512 and so cannot resolve a row-set change at all.

  c521sk-cp    the COPY probe, which can: it has full dynamic range on this
               question (served 1.0000 vs tier-1-alone 0.0000) and was verified
               to read intermediate values. 1.0000 here is a real statement.
  c521sk-mk3b  the second multikey draw, because one is not quotable for a
               change that would become the default.
  c521sx-*     cost, two draws. 80 us is 0.7% of an 11.04 ms step against ~0.2%
               of two-draw noise, so unlike every earlier kernel win on this
               line this one may actually resolve in ITL.

The knob is inert on a DSA arm (_ingraph_device_step is a vestigekv method), so
the baseline rows carry it too -- that keeps each draw's two arms identical and
lets the audit's pair guard check them, which it refused to do otherwise.

c521dflt / c521fence: the branch lineage, now that it is the only one that can
merge. engine 605362463c makes the sketch-chain skip AUTOMATIC on the branch arm
(_skip_sketch_chain(); the env knob is tri-state, 0 forces the chain back on).

Why only this lineage ships, with the byte accounting behind it. The sketch
scan reads side[64] + csk[64] + metadata per archive entry, ~272 B per group of
4 = 68 B/token, over the WHOLE archive every step, and nothing in the baseline
pays it. The branch rule's scan is DSA's own 33 B/token index-k, which the
baseline pays too, so it cancels. At S=131072 across 11 layers that is 98 MB of
sketch scan per step against DSA's entire 23 MB row budget -- the non-branch
scheme reads 4x more than the baseline it is trying to beat, and the gap grows
linearly with context. It cannot pass on bytes at any implementation quality.

(That also resolves a discrepancy flagged earlier in this register: the scan was
measured at 68 B/token while the VKSTATS formula carries 35.25, and I marked it
as possibly under-counted. It was not -- the 68 belongs to the sketch scan,
which the branch arm does not run, and the five kernels leaving the CUPTI trace
proves it does not.)

  c521dflt-mk3 / -cp   the new default path. The gate changed from an env read
                       to `_branch_q() is not None`, which is different code, so
                       the c521sk draws do not carry over. The copy probe is the
                       load-bearing one: it has range on a row-set change where
                       multikey does not.
  c521dflt-ctrl-mk3    the chain forced back ON, which is both the matched
                       control and the proof the tri-state resolves.
  c521fence-*          the budget-derived fence. WF today comes from the fetch
                       buffer WIDTH (512 groups), so a lane firing 511 groups
                       reads 4+288+tail+2044 > DSA's 2048 and the "never worse
                       than the baseline" claim is an average, not a bound.
                       Deriving WF from the budget instead -- 2048 - sinks -
                       kept - worst tail, which is 127 groups at S=65536 -- makes
                       it a BOUND: a lane either fits under 2048 or fences to
                       DSA's own selection and reads exactly 2048. Measured f is
                       68-75 groups, so 127 should not fire; fallback is the
                       instrument and it is 0.00000 today.

Note the bound needs FIXED kept to hold at long context: with rho proportional,
worst-case kept at S=131072 is 4 + 1024 + 1023 = 2051 rows, which leaves no
budget at all. So the fence and fixed kept are one change in two parts, and this
pair only demonstrates the near end of it.

c521tri-*: close the triangle, because the MECHANISM I gave for the sketch-chain
default was wrong and the contradiction it leaves is unresolved.

What is measured, same shape, STATS on where applicable:
  chain ON,  BRANCH_Q set      fetched 273.2  ratio 0.607  multikey 1.000
  chain OFF, BRANCH_Q set      fetched  11.0  ratio 0.474  multikey 1.000 x2,
                                                           copy probe 1.0000
  INDEXER_ONLY, no BRANCH_Q    (tier 1 alone)               multikey 0.000

RETRACTED: that the chain was "the selector the branch rule replaced". The
_branch_* kernels are ABSENT from both CUPTI traces -- 61 distinct kernels, no
branch/fire/kept/hist match -- while _vk_dsa_decode_split_kernel is present, so
in-graph kernels do get captured and branch_fire is simply not running. And
fetched collapses 273.2 -> 11.0 when the chain is skipped, so the CHAIN was
doing the fetching. What the default change actually did is cut tier 2 from 273
rows to 11, and quality held on four instruments including the only one with
range on a row-set change.

The contradiction: 11 fetched rows scores 1.000 while tier 1 alone scores 0.000.
Those cannot both hold unless BRANCH_Q changes something besides firing rows --
and I have now tried to derive that from the code twice and been wrong twice.

  c521tri-noq-mk3   chain OFF with BRANCH_Q REMOVED. Against the two rows above
                    this is the missing corner: if it reads 1.000, BRANCH_Q is
                    not what carries the arm and the 0.000 belongs to something
                    INDEXER_ONLY does beyond gating the certificate recall. If
                    it reads 0.000, BRANCH_Q is load-bearing through a path that
                    is not branch_fire, and that path is what to find.
  c521tri-noq-L     the same with STATS, to see what the 11 rows are.

The default is NOT being reverted on this. Its evidence -- two multikey draws,
the copy probe on all three scores, the byte ratio, and a two-draw cost pair --
is independent of the mechanism I got wrong.

c521tri RESULT -- the triangle closes, and it corrects TWO of my readings.

    arm                          fetched  fetch p50/p90/p99   multikey
    chain ON,  BRANCH_Q set        273.2    0 / 1380 / 1984    1.000
    chain OFF, BRANCH_Q set         12.4    0 /    0 /  396    1.000 (copy 1.0000)
    chain OFF, no BRANCH_Q           0.0    0 /    0 /    0    0.000

branch_fire DOES run. It fetches on about 1% of steps -- p90 is zero, p99 is 396
rows or ~99 groups -- and that 1% carries all of the quality: remove it and
multikey goes to 0.000. The sketch chain fetched ~273 rows on nearly every step
(p90=1380) and removing it costs nothing measurable, so 261 rows per step were
waste and the byte ratio goes 0.607 -> 0.511.

FIRST CORRECTION, to the mechanism originally given for the default change: the
chain was NOT "the selector the branch rule replaced" -- it was doing bulk
fetching of its own, and the branch rule is the sparse mechanism that matters.
What the default change did was delete bulk waste, not a redundant selector.

SECOND CORRECTION, to the retraction itself: I then said branch_fire "basically
does not work" because the _branch_* kernels are absent from the CUPTI trace's
61 kernels. That inference is invalid for a kernel firing on 1% of steps -- two
firings averaged over 200 steps sit below every other entry. Absence from a
profile's top-N is evidence about the MEAN, never about whether a path executes.
The fetch percentiles settled it and they were in VKSTATS the whole time.

What the final picture changes downstream:
- kept now carries 99% of steps, so its size is a main-path decision. The
  earlier "kept has no lower bound" reading needs re-checking: if the copy
  probe's fetches also concentrate in that 1%, it measured whether those rare
  steps suffice rather than whether kept does.
- maintaining kept is therefore main-path cost too, which is the argument for
  the incremental merge at close rather than the O(closed-prefix) topk at
  backend:2959.
- the budget-derived fence is free: fetch p99 is ~99 groups against a 127-group
  trigger, fallback 0.00000 measured.

c521cpq-*: re-check "kept has no lower bound", because the triangle changed what
that claim could have measured.

The claim came from the copy probe reading 1.0000 at rho 1/512, i.e. 68 kept
rows. But c521tri then showed fetch is not spread over steps at all -- p90 is
zero and p99 is 396 rows, so branch_fire carries about 1% of steps and those
steps carry all of multikey's quality. If the copy probe's fetches concentrate
in the same 1%, then what it measured was whether those rare fetches suffice,
not whether 68 kept rows do, and the lower-bound reading has to be withdrawn.

  c521cpq-noq        the copy probe with BRANCH_Q REMOVED at the served rho:
                     kept alone, no 1% mechanism. 1.0000 means kept really does
                     carry the copy; anything lower means it never did.
  c521cpq-noq-r512   the same at rho 1/512 -- the exact 68-row point the claim
                     rests on.
  c521cpq-q-r512     rho 1/512 WITH branch_fire, the matched control, so the
                     pair isolates the 1% rather than the rho change.

This gates the constant-kept size: if kept alone carries the copy at 68 rows the
budget can be small, and if it does not then 99% of steps are running on a kept
set that is only adequate because the other 1% repairs it, which is a different
design question.

c521cpq RESULT -- "kept has no lower bound" is RETRACTED. The matched pair:

    rho 1/512 (68 kept rows), branch_fire ON   lead 1.0000  bag 1.0000  num 1.0000
    rho 1/512 (68 kept rows), branch_fire OFF  lead 0.0001  bag 0.0634  num 0.0772
    rho 1/128,                branch_fire OFF  lead 0.0007  bag 0.0456  num 0.0350

Same rho, same 68 rows, the only variable being whether the ~1% of steps that
fetch anything do so. 0.0001 against 1.0000. So the copy probe never measured
kept's lower bound; it measured whether tier 2's sparse fetches suffice, and
kept's own contribution to the copy is indistinguishable from zero.

What this does NOT retract: the probe's range. 1.0000 vs 0.0001 on a matched
pair is exactly why it could settle this where multikey reads 1.000 either way.
Every quality verdict in this register that rests on the copy probe stands; what
falls is one inference drawn from it.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE CONSTANT-KEPT SIZE, which this check was queued to gate.
The argument "32 groups is enough because kept has no measured lower bound" is
gone. The replacement is cleaner and does not depend on kept carrying retrieval
at all: kept's remaining jobs are the sinks, the recent window, and the
q-quantile sample for the branch threshold, and a quantile's precision depends
on the SAMPLE size rather than the population -- 32 groups gives a q0.75
standard error of 0.077, 64 gives 0.054. Nothing about that scales with context,
so nothing about kept should.

And the quantity that genuinely needs a bound is no longer kept but tier 2's
per-fire budget, measured at p99 = 396 rows ~ 99 groups. That retires the
"conservative margin" framing of the budget-derived fence: 127 groups is not
slack above a measured 68-75 mean, it is the smallest budget that covers the
p99 of what actually fires.

c521ck32-*: the constant tier-1 budget, 32 groups = 128 rows (engine 6c9b373512).

The size comes from the quantile argument, not from kept carrying retrieval --
that reading was retracted today (c521cpq: lead 1.0000 with branch_fire on,
0.0001 off, same 68 rows). kept's jobs are the sinks, the recent window, and the
q0.75 sample, and a quantile's standard error is 0.077 at 32 groups and 0.054 at
64, neither depending on the context.

  c521ck32-mk3 / -cp   quality. The copy probe is the one that matters: it has
                       range on a row-set change (1.0000 vs 0.0001 measured on a
                       matched pair) where multikey reads 1.000 either way.
  c521ck32-L / -S      bytes at both shapes. The claim is specifically that the
                       ratio STOPS tracking context: proportional rho reads
                       0.511 long and 0.645 short today and crosses DSA near
                       158k, while a constant budget is flat.
  c521ck32-fence-L     the hard bound, now possible because the budget is
                       constant: WF = 2048 - 4 - 128 - 1023(worst tail) = 893
                       rows = 223 groups, context-INDEPENDENT. Every lane then
                       either fits under DSA's 2048 rows or fences to DSA's own
                       selection and reads exactly 2048. Measured fetch p99 is
                       396 rows ~ 99 groups, so 223 should not fire and fallback
                       is the instrument.

With proportional rho that bound does not exist at long context at all: worst
kept at 131k is 4 + 1024 + 1023 = 2051 rows, leaving nothing for recall.

c521ck32-smoke: a 4k/256 stream ahead of the batch, because the batch's five
jobs all died with "server did not start" on an init-order crash I introduced
(engine 2426216c81 fixes it and guards the class). Nine unit test files passed
through that bug -- the backend's test skips __init__ with __new__ -- so a job
whose only requirement is that a server comes up is the cheapest instrument for
it, and it belongs ahead of any batch that changes construction.

{"args": {"input_len": 4096, "output_len": 256, "seed": 0}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "stream", "env": {"CHUNK": "512", "CTX": "135168", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "1", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "1", "MAX_REQS": "1", "MEM_FRAC": "0.95", "SGLANG_DEBUG_VESTIGEKV_STATS": "1", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_BRANCH_Q": "0.75", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_KEPT_GROUPS": "32", "VK_JOB": "c521ck32-smoke"}, "id": "c521ck32-smoke", "probe": false}

c521xl-*: the long-context byte point, which is the ONE claim a constant budget
makes that nothing on disk has tested.

Everything measured so far is at 36k or shorter, and there the constant budget
LOSES on bytes: 0.578 against the proportional 0.511 at 4k/32k, because kept
dropped 27.9 rows while fetch rose 208.6 -- the q0.75 threshold falls with a
smaller kept set and the branch rule fires far more groups. The budget-derived
fence then recovers it (fetch 221.0 -> 31.3, ratio 0.578 -> 0.508, fallback
0.00000), which says those extra fires were almost all low value.

So at 36k the pair is a wash on bytes and the gain is the hard bound. The whole
case for a constant budget rests on what happens FURTHER OUT, where
proportional rho's kept grows as closed/128 and crosses DSA's flat 2048-row read
near 158k while a constant budget does not move. 4k/124k is the longest shape
this pool has served (CTX 135168, MEM_FRAC 0.96, and this exact launch has run
before).

  c521xl-prop    proportional rho, the control at this shape
  c521xl-ck32    constant 32 groups
  c521xl-ck32f   constant 32 groups plus the budget-derived fence at 223 groups

Predicted, on the record: proportional degrades toward 1 (the model puts its
rows at 4 + 1024 + 512 + fetch against DSA's 2048), the constant arm stays near
its 36k value, and the fence arm is the lowest of the three. If instead the
constant arm also degrades, the shape argument is wrong and the only thing left
in it is the bound.

c521xl RESULT -- the long-context point, and the 36k conclusion gets a boundary.

    shape            proportional   constant 32   constant + fence(223)
    4k/32k   (36k)       0.511          0.578            0.508
    4k/124k (128k)       0.849          0.719            0.706

    at 128k:  kept 1035.2 -> 639.4   fetched 226.8 -> 71.3 -> 16.2
    at 36k:   kept  666.9 -> 639.0   fetched  12.4 -> 221.0 -> 31.3

All six at 126950/126976 or 32750 steps, rc=0, fallback 0.00000 throughout,
actuator confirmed per job from the tier-1 log line.

THE PREDICTION logged above was half right. Proportional does degrade (0.511 ->
0.849) and the constant arm does win at 128k and the fence arm is lowest of the
three. But I predicted the constant arm would "stay near its 36k value" and it
did not: 0.578 -> 0.719.

Why, and it matters more than the win. The ROW term falls with the constant
budget -- 0.686 MB at 36k to 0.671 MB at 128k -- so kept and fetch are not what
degrades. It is the shared 33 B/token index scan, 1.217 MB at 36k and 4.325 MB
at 128k, which is 63.9% of our read at the near shape and 86.6% at the far one.
That term cancels in the DIFFERENCE against DSA, since both arms pay it, but it
dilutes the RATIO toward 1. So a constant budget removes the crossing near 158k
and slows the decay; it cannot stop it.

That retires "kept" as a byte lever: at 128k the part of the read that is ours
is already down to 0.671 MB against a 4.325 MB scan we share. The only
degradation source left is the scan, which puts a coarser first-pass index of
our own -- breaking the tie with DSA's pooling rather than reusing it -- as the
next structural lever rather than an idea to keep in reserve.

And the 36k verdict needs its boundary stated: the constant budget's sign
depends on the shape, because a smaller kept set lowers the q0.75 threshold and
fetch rises (12.4 -> 221.0 there) while proportional kept's growth has not yet
bitten. Quote the pair, never one shape.

c521cpx-*: does the two-level scan's loss cost quality? Asked with the proxy
(engine HEAD) before any of the index is built.

Offline the trade is 33 -> 18.56 B/token of scan at C=16, f=0.50 keeping 0.905
of what branch_fire fires, and 26.84 at f=0.75 keeping 0.980. The proxy drops
the same shape -- whole runs of 16 fine groups -- and picks survivors by hash
rather than score, so it is strictly worse than the real filter.

  c521cpx-smoke  a server has to come up; the batch before this one lost five
                 jobs to an init crash because I skipped this step.
  c521cpx-cp50   the copy probe at f=0.50, the aggressive setting. This is the
                 decisive one: it reads 1.0000 on the served arm and 0.0001 with
                 tier 2 off, so it can see a 9.5% loss of the fired set if that
                 loss matters.
  c521cpx-cp75   the copy probe at f=0.75, the conservative setting (2% loss).
  c521cpx-mk75   multikey at f=0.75, for the record; it reads 1.000 on arms the
                 copy probe separates, so it is not the instrument here.

If cp50 holds, the design is worth building at the aggressive setting and the
scan drops 44%. If only cp75 holds, the win is 19% for a new index, new buffers
and two-level kernels, which is probably not worth it. If neither holds, the
direction closes here.

c521fb-*: measure fallback on RULER, which has never been measured.

Every fallback=0.00000 in this register comes from a STREAM job. The quality
jobs run with STATS off and emit no VKSTATS at all, so RULER and MRCR have no
fallback number -- and quoting the stream figure beside a quality result, as I
have been doing, implies one where none exists.

STATS is safe to add here: its measured effect is ~0.19 on MRCR but NULL on
RULER niah at 131k n=150 (p=1.0), so a STATS-on multikey compares directly with
the 1.000 already on disk.

  c521fb-mk3-stats     the served arm with STATS on: what IS the fallback on a
                       synthetic needle workload?
  c521fb-cpx50-stats   the f=0.50 proxy, which took the copy probe from 1.0000
                       to 0.2480. If the fence does not fire here either, that
                       settles the direction question: the predicate is
                       `fenced = ng > WF`, so it only ever trips on over-firing,
                       while the synthetic-data failure is UNDER-recall, which
                       produces fewer fires and is invisible to it.

Measured already, from the stream runs: the f=0.50 proxy's own smoke reports
fallback=0.00000 while the copy probe at that setting reads 0.2480 -- quality
down 75 points with the fence never firing. These two jobs test whether RULER
tells the same story.

## c521kap-*: kappa as a served parameter, and the instrument that can rank it

The compiled-in LOWPASS_KAPPA = 16 was chosen on the **mla** arm's signal -- the
latent row's own salience branch, at m = rho*T groups with rho = 1/32 -- and the
docstring records the curve as flat to +-0.3% over kappa in [4, 64]. The dsa arm
does not compute sigma on that signal at all: `_index_sigma` runs the projection
over DSA's **index-k** in group space, window `close_block/pool`. Nobody ever
tuned kappa for it, and constant kept moved the used size from ~104 groups to 32.

Re-measured at the served size, on the served signal, over all 88 calibration
dumps (704 real queries, all 11 DSA layers, seq 13k-18k), with two criteria --
captured attention mass from the real softmax over qcal and the latent rows,
and precision against DSA's own top-512:

    captured attention mass          m=32     m=64    m=128    m=256
      sigma kappa=2                0.1551   0.1758   0.2022   0.2433
      sigma kappa=4                0.1523   0.1698   0.1940   0.2342
      sigma kappa=8                0.1480   0.1647   0.1889   0.2267
      sigma kappa=16  (served)     0.1410   0.1608   0.1828   0.2207
      sigma kappa=32               0.1358   0.1532   0.1761   0.2132
      oracle (attention mass)      0.3327   0.3809   0.4376   0.5089
      oracle (DSA score)           0.1705   0.2066   0.2459   0.3057
      random (closed form)         0.0082   0.0164   0.0328   0.0655

    precision vs DSA top-512         m=32     m=64    m=128    m=256
      sigma kappa=2                0.6626   0.5369   0.4110   0.3070
      sigma kappa=16  (served)     0.6264   0.5004   0.3844   0.2913
      oracle (attention mass)      0.6491   0.5724   0.4867   0.3873
      random (closed form)         0.1310   0.1310   0.1310   0.1310

Monotone in kappa, no interior optimum, +10% captured mass at every m. Two
things worth stating plainly. At m=32 a query-FREE sigma at kappa=2 has higher
precision against DSA's selection (0.6626) than the true attention-mass top-32
does (0.6491) -- which is the NoPE spectral property, not an artifact of the
budget. And kappa is **free**: the basis is zero-padded to 32 columns whatever
kappa is, so the projection costs exactly the same and no byte moves.

The mass criterion had to be built, not reused. DSA's own score

    s_g = sum_h w_h * relu(<q_h, k_g>)

takes w from a plain linear projection with no activation: 53% of the gates and
99.6% of the scores are negative, so "share of in-budget score mass" is ill-posed
on it -- the budget sum is negative, a subset can exceed it, and random comes out
below zero. On a signed score only the SET is well defined. The dumps carry qcal
and the latent rows, so the mass criterion uses the real attention softmax.

Offline evidence has never decided quality on this line, so it only shortlists.
The problem is that both in-situ instruments are SATURATED: niah_multikey_3 at
65536 reads 1.00 and the copy probe at ctx 32768 reads lead=1.0000 for every
healthy configuration, including constant 32 groups. They move only when
something is broken (the coarse proxy at f=0.50 took copy to 0.2480 and multikey
to 0.38). A kappa A/B against 1.0000 measures nothing.

So this batch builds the instrument first and A/Bs on it at the same time: the
copy probe hardened along its two axes, context 32768 -> 65536 and the span
384 -> 1024 -> 2048 words with the span moved deeper (pos 0.1 -> 0.5 -> 0.8),
run at kappa 16 and kappa 2 as a matched pair. Sustained verbatim copying is
this line's known weakness, so a long span late in a long context is where tier
1's selection should start to matter; the paired form means the rungs that stay
at 1.0000 cost nothing but the rung that breaks is already an A/B.

The smoke rung is not optional: a Triton kernel behind an env flag has no
compile-time coverage, and the last two defaults batches died at serve time on
exactly that (an int32 Knuth constant, and an init-order read). kappa=2 builds a
3-column basis the served path has never compiled.

  c521kap-smoke     kappa 2, 4k/256 stream, STATS on: the basis compiles, the
                    tier-1 log line names the served cutoff
  c521kap-k16-s*    kappa 16 (explicit, so the pair differs in one value)
  c521kap-k2-s*     kappa 2

### c521kap-k1-*: the DC-only limit, added after the baselines were fixed

The first run of the grid let its two extra baselines rank the whole group axis
while sigma, by construction, ranks only the CLOSED prefix -- so a "centred row
norm" that reached into the un-closed tail scored 1.3x sigma's captured mass and
looked like a result. It was not: the tail is the most recent ~1000 tokens, held
at full magnitude while every closed row had its block mean removed, so that
baseline was picking recency, not salience. Restricted to the prefix the served
code actually selects from, the baseline reproduces sigma k=1 to four digits --
which is the identity (kappa=1 keeps bin 0 alone, so the residual IS the row minus
its block mean) and a check on the whole chain.

    closed prefix only       m=32     m=64    m=128    m=256
      sigma kappa=1        0.1578   0.1805   0.2088   0.2511    mass
      sigma kappa=2        0.1551   0.1758   0.2022   0.2433
      sigma kappa=16       0.1410   0.1608   0.1828   0.2207
      centred row norm     0.1578   0.1805   0.2088   0.2511    == kappa=1
      raw row norm         0.1077   0.1121   0.1207   0.1464
      oracle (att mass)    0.1969   0.2356   0.2857   0.3518
      oracle (DSA score)   0.1446   0.1640   0.1887   0.2259
      random (closed form) 0.0068   0.0135   0.0271   0.0542

      sigma kappa=1        0.6825   0.5625   0.4416   0.3312    precision
      sigma kappa=16       0.6264   0.5004   0.3844   0.2913
      oracle (att mass)    0.5680   0.5083   0.4371   0.3512
      raw row norm         0.1406   0.1033   0.0888   0.0824

Three things follow, and the first is uncomfortable. **On the dsa arm's signal the
best cutoff is DC-only**, so the trigonometric basis buys nothing there: a block
mean and a row norm carry all of it. The 1.7% between kappa 1 and 2 is inside any
honest error bar, but the direction is monotone across six values and the endpoint
is a structurally different object than a low-pass. Second, the centring is the
whole mechanism -- the raw row norm is barely above random on precision (0.1406 vs
0.1234) while the centred one is 0.6825. Third, at m=32 a query-FREE sigma k=1
captures more real attention mass (0.1578) than a per-query oracle over DSA's own
indexer score (0.1446), and reaches 80% of the attention-mass oracle.

This does NOT license removing the projection kernel. The mla arm serves kappa=16
on a different signal, the basis is zero-padded to 32 columns whatever kappa is so
there is no cost to recover, and nothing in-situ has confirmed any of it. What it
licenses is measuring kappa=1 on the same ladder, which is what these three add.

### c521kap-*-s1024-rep: the noise floor the batch needs to conclude

The s1024 rung is the only one with dynamic range (384 saturates at 1.0000 on both
kappas; 2048 lifts bag/num back up because a longer span gives more material to
recover after a first divergence, so it discriminates less than s1024 at 1.5x the
wall time). It reads kappa=16 lead 0.7314 / bag 0.8672 / num 0.8627 against kappa=2
lead 0.7252 / bag 0.8248 / num 0.8322 -- kappa=2 lower on all three, which already
refuses the offline +10%, but the batch has no same-config replicate at this
operating point and therefore no noise floor. The decode path agrees with itself on
48% of greedy tokens, so the floor is not assumed small.

These two re-run s1024 at both kappas with identical env and seed. Same seed means
the same 20 prompts, so the only source of difference is the decode path's own
nondeterminism -- which is exactly the quantity needed. Without them the kappa
comparison is a singleton pair and cannot be quoted either way.

The s1024 triple came in INVERTED against the offline grid -- kappa 16 / 2 / 1 read
lead 0.7314 / 0.7252 / 0.6489 and bag 0.8672 / 0.8248 / 0.7541, while offline
captured attention mass ordered them 0.1410 / 0.1551 / 0.1578, i.e. exactly
backwards. kappa=1 is the DC-only limit, the centred row norm with no trigonometric
basis, and it is the worst configuration measured: removing the basis hurts. The
doubt recorded above -- that the basis "buys nothing a mean and a norm could not" --
is withdrawn on this evidence; that reading came from a criterion that scores only
one of the kept set's two jobs.

The other job is setting the tier-2 threshold, which is the q0.75 quantile of the
kept GROUPS' scores. A set chosen for magnitude raises that quantile and suppresses
the firing that does the retrieving, which would explain an anti-correlation rather
than mere non-correlation. Hypothesis, not finding: testing it needs a STATS stream
job per kappa comparing fetched/call, which these copy jobs cannot show.

k1-s1024-rep makes the three-way replicate complete, because "the spectral basis
earns its place" is a paper-level claim and currently rests on one draw.

### c521kap-* VERDICT: kappa is UNRESOLVED, and the mechanism I wrote for it is withdrawn

The first same-config replicate settles the batch, against the reading I gave it.
`k16-s1024` and `k16-s1024-rep` are identical env, identical seed, therefore the
identical 20 prompts, and they read

      k16-s1024       lead=0.7314  bag=0.8672  num=0.8627   wall=1022s
      k16-s1024-rep   lead=0.8687  bag=0.9183  num=0.9124   wall= 991s
      spread          0.137        0.051       0.050

The `lead` noise floor is 0.137. The whole kappa=16 -> kappa=1 gap was 0.083, so
every comparison in this batch sits INSIDE the floor. Withdrawn, all of it:

  - "in-situ ordering is kappa 16 > 2 > 1, monotone" -- three single draws from a
    distribution this wide; not established.
  - "kappa=1 is the worst configuration measured, removing the basis hurts" -- not
    established. The offline grid is therefore neither confirmed nor inverted.
  - "two span lengths agree on the direction" -- the s2048 cells are single draws
    too, so their agreement carries no more weight than the s1024 one did.
  - "DSA_LOWPASS_KAPPA = 16 now has a derivation, not just a measurement" -- the
    mechanism (bins 1-15 are neighbourhood-common topical drift and so cannot
    discriminate within a neighbourhood, while bins >= 16 carry token-level anomaly)
    stands as THEORY and has no in-situ support. I published a mechanism for an
    effect I had not shown exists.

This is the failure mode the method rules name explicitly, and the sequencing error
is the whole of it: I noted the missing noise floor two checks before writing the
mechanism, queued the replicate, and then reasoned past it instead of waiting.

What the batch did establish, which is about the INSTRUMENT and is worth more than
the kappa answer would have been:

  - the copy probe at ctx 65536 / span 1024 / n=20 has a `lead` replicate spread of
    0.137, so it cannot resolve an effect of this size. Resolving 0.08 needs roughly
    11 draws per arm, about 9.5 GPU-hours, for a knob that is free either way.
  - `bag` and `num` are the tighter metrics here (spread ~0.05) and are what should
    be quoted at this operating point.
  - ctx 65536 / span 384 is dead: 1.0000 on all three metrics at all three kappas.
  - span 2048 lifts bag/num while lead falls, so it discriminates less than s1024 at
    1.5x the wall time.

Served default: DSA_LOWPASS_KAPPA stays 16 because nothing displaced it, NOT because
it was shown better. No further kappa jobs.

## c521dnxl-*: the dense-MLA comparison at the long shape, which was never run

The byte model, with no free parameters, reproduces the one measured >1 on this line
to within 1%. Per decode step at bs=1 the context-INDEPENDENT weight traffic is
4.983 GB (42 MoE layers x 9 experts x 12.58 MB, plus 3 dense FFN layers at 226 MB),
cross-checked against a measured ITL: 5.05 GB at 11.3 ms is 447 GB/s aggregate, 224
GB/s per rank at TP=2, i.e. the 13-18%-of-peak figure the profiles show.

Against that denominator:

    seq      dense MLA KV   DSA attn   VK attn   VK vs DSA   VK vs dense   dense KV share
    4096           46.1M      24.6M     12.8M      1.0023        1.0067            0.9%
    36864         415.2M      36.5M     24.7M      1.0023        1.0780            7.7%
    65536         738.2M      46.9M     35.1M      1.0023        1.1401           12.9%
    131072       1476.4M      70.6M     58.9M      1.0023        1.2811           22.9%
    262144       2952.8M     118.2M    106.5M      1.0023        1.5593           37.2%

Two readings, and they point opposite ways.

**Against DSA, >1 is structurally closed.** DSA's whole attention read is 1.40% of
the step at 131k, and the only part we can remove -- its 2048 rows -- is 23.07 MB =
0.46%, CONSTANT in context. Reading zero rows AND zero scan caps at 1.0142x; at the
measured row count it is 1.0023x, identical at every length, because DSA's prize does
not grow. Batching cannot rescue it either: MoE traffic saturates once 9B >= 289
while KV grows linearly, so KV reaches 10% of the step only near B=240, and the pool
is 1.17 GB ~ 101k tokens, which holds ONE 131k sequence. Long context on this box is
structurally bs=1.

**Against dense MLA the ceiling GROWS with context**, because dense reads the whole
archive -- 23% of the step at 131k against 0.9% at 4k. And the 36864 prediction,
1.0780x, lands on the measured 1.067-1.070x. That agreement contradicts the reading
in memory that the dense win is kernel quality rather than compression, and it makes
the distinction testable in one pair: compression-driven predicts ~1.28x at 4k/124k,
kernel-quality-driven predicts it stays near 1.07x.

Only the 32768/4096 shape was ever run, so this is the gap. Two replicates of the
matched pair, STATS OFF (never time a VestigeKV arm with STATS on), identical pool at
MEM_FRAC 0.96, decode-only so median AND p99 ITL are both quotable. The vestigekv arm
carries the selected design: constant 32 groups, budget-derived fence at 223 groups,
close block 1024, branch threshold q0.75.

If this comes back near 1.07x the byte model is wrong about WHY, and the >1 claim has
to be attributed and re-derived. If it comes back near 1.28x then the line's headline
is a compression-driven speedup that grows with context against dense attention, and
the DSA comparison should be reported as byte parity-and-better rather than latency.

The s1024 replicate set completed. All six draws:

    kappa 16   lead 0.7314, 0.8687   bag 0.8672, 0.9183
    kappa  2   lead 0.7252, 0.7303   bag 0.8248, 0.9216
    kappa  1   lead 0.6489, 0.6767   bag 0.7541, 0.8624

The metrics disagree and that is the result. On `lead` both kappa=1 draws are the two
lowest of six (sorted: 0.6489 k1, 0.6767 k1, 0.7252 k2, 0.7303 k2, 0.7314 k16, 0.8687
k16), exact one-sided p = 1/C(6,2) = 0.067. On `bag` they interleave (0.7541 k1,
0.8248 k2, 0.8624 k1, 0.8672 k16, 0.9183 k16, 0.9216 k2) and there is no separation.
`bag` is the lower-variance metric at this operating point, so the `lead` result is
not taken as real.

Also withdrawn: the "noise floor is 0.137" figure I quoted from the first pair. The
per-config spreads are 0.137 / 0.005 / 0.028 on `lead` and 0.051 / 0.097 / 0.108 on
`bag` -- kappa=16 is widest on one metric and narrowest on the other. A two-draw range
does not estimate a spread, so no single floor should be quoted from one pair.

Final: kappa=16 and kappa=2 indistinguishable, kappa=1 a weak candidate for worse at
p~0.067 on one metric and nothing on the other. UNRESOLVED. No further kappa jobs --
p~0.067 on a free knob does not repay another 9 GPU-hours.

## Where the DSA comparison's remaining headroom actually is (trace, not model)

The byte model said the addressable term is 0.46% of the step and closed >1 against
DSA. The traces say the surface is bigger than the byte term and it is COMPUTE, and
they say we are not touching most of it. Per step, ctx36k, 200 steps, TP-0:

                                              DSA baseline      vestigekv
    sm120_fp8_paged_mqa_logits                 44.6us x13      44.6us x13
    kpool_topk_transform_kernel<512>            ~87us x11      87.1us x11
    _sparse_mla_decode_split + _reduce         98.5us x22      29.8us x11
    kpool_decode_update_and_maybe_write        101.2us x23     102.3us x23
    VK tier-1 sigma                                   --      +42.6us x2
    VK tier-2 branch                                  --      +75.6us x15
    total GPU                                   11.634ms        12.136ms

**DSA's indexer top-k is identical on both arms, to the microsecond.** The fence's
fallback target is DSA's 2048-row selection, so the top-k is computed unconditionally
for the fallback to exist. We remove only the attention kernel (68.7us) and add
118.2us: net +49.5us, which is why the arm sits below 1.

    situation                          save    add     net        speedup
    today                              68.7   118.2   -49.5us      < 1
    skip DSA's top-k                  155.8   118.2   +37.6us     1.0032x
    + fuse branch into the attn kernel 155.8   42.6   +113.0us     1.0098x
    whole DSA path gone (limit)      202-230     0        --       1.0177x

So the one algorithmic change that puts this arm above 1 against DSA is a predicate
that decides BEFORE the top-k whether the step needs DSA's selection, firing it on
~1% of steps rather than all of them. That is the under-recall quality floor already
on the open list, and this re-prices it: it is the largest single item on the
addressable surface (0.74% of the step, 42% of the total), not just a correctness gap.

Even executed perfectly it is ~1.01x. The DSA comparison's claim stays BYTES; the
latency claim belongs to the dense comparison, which is what c521dnxl-* is measuring.

Bucketing trap recorded: `fusedBuildExpertMapsSortFirstToken` is 130us/step x42 and
matches both "cutlass" and "sort" -- it is MoE expert routing, not the indexer. I
inflated a "top-k/sort" bucket to 221us/step by catching it before splitting by name.

## c521pf-xl-baseline: is DSA's top-k work-bound or latency-bound?

From the ctx36k trace, `kpool_topk_transform_kernel<512>` costs 87.1 us/step over 11
launches = 7.9 us per layer for G = 36864/4 = 9216 fp32 logits. Four radix passes over
that is ~147 KB, so the kernel achieves ~19 GB/s -- about 2% of what the device can
do. On that reading it is launch/occupancy bound on a tiny problem, and reducing the
candidate count would buy almost nothing.

But that is an ESTIMATE, not a measurement: a single 36k trace cannot separate the two,
because its 200 steps grow the context by 0.5%. The separation is a context sweep, and
G goes 9216 -> 32768 from 36k to 124k:

    latency-bound  -> top-k grows to ~1.4x (about 11 us/layer, 120 us/step)
    work-bound     -> top-k grows to ~3.6x (about 28 us/layer, 310 us/step)

If it is work-bound at the long shape then the top-k becomes the dominant item on the
addressable surface and work-side reduction -- a smaller candidate set -- is back on
the table. This is the one measurement that can overturn that conclusion, and it is
the same shape as the c521dnxl pair so the trace sits next to those ITL numbers.

baseline arm only: the question is about DSA's own kernels, and the vestigekv arm runs
them identically (87.1 vs 87.1 us at 36k), so one trace answers it for both.

## c521cd3-*: re-collect the dumps, because cd_* predate the qidx clone fix

All 88 dumps in `results/glm53/cd_{w3,w2,g2}` hold 8 BIT-IDENTICAL `qidx`/`qgate`
(`max|q0-qi| = 0.000e+00`) at 8 distinct `qpos`. That is the aliasing defect fixed at
vestigekv_dsa_backend.py:3112 -- `_indexer_query_for` row-selects out of a
fixed-address per-layer buffer, so appending the view made every entry hold the last
step's write. The comment at the fix site says so in as many words. These dumps were
taken before it, the re-collection was listed as pending, and four offline studies ran
on them today reporting "704 queries" when 88 were distinct.

Two results are void, both of which were the session's most promising lead at the
moment they appeared:

  - step-to-step persistence r(k) = 1.0000 at every lag 1..7, in every layer, all 88
    dumps. It compared one query with itself seven times.
  - the static-pool oracle at recall 1.0000 for M/G = 0.25. The "union of 8 queries'
    top-512" was one query's top-512, which fits in 831 groups trivially.

A perfect 1.0000 in every cell is a provenance alarm, not a finding.

What survives, because it rests on structure rather than sample size: the norm bound's
0.0000 (a non-negative bound against a negative tau, with ||k|| held near-constant by
indexer.k_norm -- architectural), the flat wq_b spectrum, and rank-r truncation's flat
recall curve (0.27 at r=2 to 0.53 at r=64, where it saves no bytes at all -- far too
large an effect for n=44 to overturn). The kappa grid's ORDERING remains a valid paired
comparison but its error bars are ~2.8x wider than I implied, and it decided nothing
anyway.

Three changes from the retired dump jobs: ENGINE pinned (the old ones predate that
requirement and would serve the Kimi tree), a fresh directory so the stale dumps can
never be mixed in, and max_tokens 512 rather than 64 -- the dump appends one
(qidx, qgate) per decode step per layer, so 512 steps give lags 1..511. That is the
measurement the persistence question needs and the one 8 consecutive steps cannot give.

## Is an approximate top-2048 feasible? The mass criterion, done properly

The exact identity, no assumptions: for S subset of T with dropped mass m under
p = softmax over T,

    o_S - o_T = m/(1-m) * (vbar_S - vbar_D),  so  ||o_S - o_T|| <= m/(1-m)*diam(V)

Output error is linear in dropped MASS; the set size never appears. So recall is the
wrong axis and concentration is the question -- and the reference distribution decides
the answer, which is where my first pass went wrong.

Measured, 88 dumps, one honest query each (qidx is aliased, so only index -1 pairs
correctly with qcal):

    renormalised WITHIN DSA's 2048     n_eff = 30.1 mean head, 3.2 worst
    rows for 50/90/99% within T        22 / 188 / 714 (mean head)
    keep top-M by INDEXER score        M=256 -> drops 0.380 mass mean, 0.701 worst head,
                                       relative output error 0.595 / 1.122

    against FULL attention             DSA's 2048 rows hold 0.5631 of the mass,
                                       0.2231 at the worst head
    rows for 90% of full mass          2293.6 per head
    union over 32 heads @ 90%          8526.6 rows = 4.16x the budget
    union @ 99%                        14403 = 7.03x

The within-T number is a CONDITIONAL distribution and is not the reference; against
full attention the layer is diffuse, and the mass criterion demands a budget 4x LARGER
than DSA's. So the criterion is sound but too conservative to design with: it is
sufficient for output fidelity and nowhere near necessary for task quality -- DSA keeps
56% of the mass and scores 1.00 on niah_multikey_3.

Two structural facts fall out. The indexer score is a poor ranker of post-softmax mass
(top-256 by indexer score loses 70% of the worst head's mass), because the selection is
ONE shared set for a layer whose 32 heads are each sharply peaked at DIFFERENT rows --
the 2048 budget is the union of 32 peaked distributions, and MLA's shared latent means
the cost IS the union rather than the sum. And DSA's worst head receives only 22% of
its mass, so the baseline is not claiming mass fidelity either, which is a point the
paper can use rather than defend against.

### c521pf-xl-baseline result: the top-k is 63% work-bound at 124k

                           36k us/step   124k us/step   growth
    indexer logits                44.6           67.3    1.51x
    indexer top-k                 85.9          157.4    1.83x
    sparse mla split+reduce       98.5           98.1     1.00x
    index-k cache write           14.4           14.3     0.99x
    MoE expert sort (unrelated)  135.5          134.5     0.99x
    total GPU                   11.634ms      11.758ms

G goes 9216 -> 32768, a 3.56x rise in elements, and the top-k grows 1.83x. Fitting
cost = L + W*G: W = 0.00304 us/group, L = 57.9 us. So the kernel is 33% work at 36k and
63% work at 124k, and the single-shape estimate that called it launch-bound was wrong
at the long shape -- candidate reduction DOES pay there, on the logits kernel too.

Addressable surface: 337.1 us = 2.87% of the step at 124k against 1.98% at 36k; the
ceiling for removing the whole DSA path rises to 1.0295x. The top-k alone is 1.34% of
the step and 47% of the DSA path.

Unchanged: making that kernel faster is baseline-neutral, since DSA runs the identical
one. The relative win needs a predicate that lets our arm skip it, and whether a
sigma-ranked candidate pool can supply one is exactly what c521cd3-* re-measures --
the 0.348 recall at M/G=0.25 came from the aliased dumps.

### kappa RESOLVED to 4 (offline, cost-matched, on the re-collected dumps)

Both earlier attempts scored the tier-1 kept set alone, and on this arm tier 1 alone is
0.000 on multikey. kept's second job -- the one those grids missed -- is that it SETS
the tier-2 threshold, the q0.75 quantile of the kept groups' indexer scores. A kappa
that picks high-scoring rows raises that quantile and suppresses the firing that does
the retrieving, so the two jobs pull opposite ways and only the end-to-end row set can
rank kappa.

240 honest queries from cd3_64k, figure of merit = real softmax mass over the rows the
arm actually attends (kept + fired + sinks + un-closed tail), at EQUAL fired count:

    fired   k=1      k=2      k=4      k=8      k=16     k=32
      96    0.4038   0.4034   0.4034   0.4029   0.4016   0.3992
     110    0.4103   0.4102   0.4100   0.4097   0.4089   0.4073
     130    0.4189   0.4195   0.4187   0.4183   0.4176   0.4169

Monotone at every cost point. UNMATCHED the ordering inverts -- raw mass reads
k=32 0.4108 > k=16 0.4085 > k=1 0.4039 -- but k=32 got there by firing 116.3 groups
against k=1's 96.2, and its mass per row is LOWER (3.085e-4 vs 3.225e-4). Same trap as
matched-work-control-beats-auc.

Served 4 rather than 1: kappa=1 keeps DC alone, so sigma degenerates to a block-mean
subtraction with no margin if the block statistics shift; 4 keeps 7 basis columns, is
within 0.0004 of kappa=1 at every cost point and 0.0018 above the old 16, and costs
nothing either way. Committed on glm53-pr as 3172ef4527; needs a cherry-pick to the
served branch plus a serve smoke once the queue drains.

Stated as an offline-justified default and NOT a measured quality gain: +0.45% is far
under the copy probe's 0.137 same-config spread.

Two things worth more than the kappa answer. **branch_q carries ~3x the leverage** --
at kappa 16, q0.75 -> q0.70 moves mass 0.4085 -> 0.4134 for 11 more fired groups --
bounded by the fence rate (0.194 / 0.246 / 0.342 at 96 / 110 / 130 fired), since a
fenced lane reads DSA's full 2048 rows. And the arm holds 0.404 mass on 1252 rows
against DSA's 0.563 on 2048: **3.23e-4 vs 2.75e-4 mass per row**, so it is more
mass-efficient per row than the baseline it approximates.

## c521k4-*: validating kappa=4 on the served tree

Cherry-picked to glm53-v0521 as 3e0af3fc3d (the dsa/ isolation, a prerequisite) and
78ce52f083 (kappa 4). 107 registered tests pass; both backends import; the arms' knobs
are now genuinely separate -- dsa reads kappa=4 / rho=1/128 / close=1024 with ceilings
1/32 and 4096, mla reads kappa=16 / rho=1/32 / close=4096, and the DSA_-prefixed twins
are gone from the shared module.

A tree change cannot be validated by byte counters: a purge once made attention read the
trash slot for ten hours while every byte, kept and ITL number reproduced. So a quality
draw comes first.

  c521k4-smoke   4k/256 stream, STATS on. Confirms the server starts and the tier-1 log
                 line reports the served cutoff -- 1024/4 = 256 tokens, basis 7 dims.
                 kappa=4 builds a 7-column basis the served path has never compiled.
  c521k4-mk3     niah_multikey_3 @65536 n=50, the detector. The constant-32 arm read
                 1.00 here at kappa=16 (c521fb-mk3-stats), so this is a matched
                 comparison and anything below 1.00 is a regression, not noise.

No cost job: kappa is free by construction (the basis is zero-padded to a fixed width
whatever kappa is), so there is nothing for an ITL draw to find.

### c521k4-smoke measured the WRONG ARM; c521k4-smoke2 is the fix

c521k4-smoke read `overflow=722 fallback=0.26255 fetched=532.7 ratio=0.421` against the
matched kappa=16 control c521ck32-smoke's `overflow=0 fallback=0.00000 fetched=442.6
ratio=0.380`, which looks like a kappa regression and is not one. The two jobs differ
in more than kappa: I omitted `SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_BRANCH_Q` from the new one.

`_branch_q()` is `envs.SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_BRANCH_Q.get()` and the descriptor is
`EnvFloat(None)` -- there is NO compiled-in default. `_skip_sketch_chain()` returns
`self._branch_q() is not None`. So with the variable unset the branch tier 2 never runs
AND the sketch chain is not skipped: the job served the certificate/sketch lineage, the
one ruled out of the merge. Its overflow and fallback are properties of that lineage at
a short shape.

**The defect this exposes is worth more than the smoke.** The branch rule is the only
lineage that can merge, and it has no served default -- every run must set BRANCH_Q by
hand, and forgetting it silently selects the lineage we rejected. On the submission
branch, where the sketch chain is deleted outright, an unset BRANCH_Q would leave the
arm with no tier 2 at all. It needs a compiled-in default in dsa/defaults.py, with
`_branch_q()` falling back to it, before the deletions land.
