### kappa=4 validated on the served tree

  c521k4-mk3      niah_multikey_3 @65536 n=50 -> **1.00**, matching the kappa=16
                  constant-32 arm (c521fb-mk3-stats). No quality regression.
  c521k4-smoke2   4k/256, BRANCH_Q=0.75, against the matched kappa=16 control
                  c521ck32-smoke at the same shape:

                      kappa   overflow  fallback   kept/call  fetched/call  ratio
                         16          0   0.00000       243.8         442.6  0.380
                          4          0   0.00000       243.8         404.2  0.362

`kept` is identical to the decimal, as a constant 32-group budget must be, and kappa=4
FIRES LESS: -8.7% fetched rows, -4.7% byte ratio. The change is a small cost improvement
on top of being quality-neutral.

What makes that more than a single draw: the offline cost-matched study predicted it.
At q0.75 it had kappa=4 firing 98.7 groups against kappa=16's 109.1, ratio 0.905; the
served arm read 404.2/442.6 = 0.913, within 0.9%. An n=240 offline prediction landing on
one served draw is the evidence, not the draw by itself. The ITL (12.30 median / 12.89
p99) is not quoted as a cost claim -- 256 decoded tokens is too thin.

## There is no hot region in DSA's selection (honest dumps)

Both measurements re-run on cd3_* (176 dumps, 128 distinct queries per layer, G=15350).
Their earlier versions ran on the aliased cd_* dumps and were artifacts.

Step-to-step persistence -- recall of step t+k's top-512 inside step t's top-(mult*512):

    k      pool 1x   pool 2x   pool 4x
    1       0.2451    0.3647    0.5164
    4       0.2395    0.3588    0.5147
    7       0.2555    0.3807    0.5359

r(1) = 0.245: at the NEXT step only a quarter of the new selection was in the old one,
and the curve is flat in k, so there is no decay to extrapolate. Union over 8 steps is
2.797x the budget. Per-layer r(7) spans 0.12-0.58.

Concentration -- `always sel` is **0.000 at every one of the 11 layers**: not one group
of 15350 is chosen by all 128 queries. `ever sel` is 0.395-0.593 over 128 queries, and
the most-frequent 512 groups capture only 0.213-0.296 of all selections against the
0.63-0.77 the aliased dumps reported. Spearman of frequency vs position -0.22..+0.22,
vs key norm -0.14..+0.01.

**Closed by this, with honest data:** the static sigma-ranked candidate pool,
refresh-every-K-steps (dead at K=1, not at some large K), and a frequency-ranked hot
cache. It also makes the long-lag dump instrumentation unnecessary -- the cheap lags
answered the question, so the served tree needs no debug change.

**Not closed:** the sigma-ranked kept set, which was never a cache of DSA's selection.
Its job is the tier-2 threshold, and end-to-end it holds 0.404 mass on 1252 rows at
3.23e-4 per row against DSA's 2.75e-4. What dies is the reading of it as a hot region --
and with it my explanation for why the arm needs fresh indexer scores every step. The
honest reason is that there is nothing to cache.

## The submission-branch deletion has an exact acceptance criterion

Before removing the sketch lineage, the question was whether any of it still runs on the
branch arm. Checked empirically rather than by tracing the call graph:

                              build=      VKBUILDMS lines
    branch arm (smoke2)       0.0 ms                    0
    sketch chain (smoke)    117.4 ms                   66

The index build is gated along with the scan -- `_skip_sketch_chain()` has one call site
and the build path is behind the same decision -- so the branch arm builds NOTHING. My
suspicion that we were paying a dead 117 ms build was wrong.

Two consequences. It prices the lineage: the sketch chain costs 117.4 ms of index build
per request on top of its 68 B/token scan, and the branch arm pays neither. And it makes
the deletion the safest possible refactor, with a criterion that is a number rather than
a judgement:

  **the deletion must be a NO-OP on the branch arm's telemetry.** A post-deletion
  4k/256 smoke with BRANCH_Q=0.75 and KEPT_GROUPS unset (now the served constant) must
  reproduce c521k4-smoke2 to the digit: overflow 0, fallback 0.00000, kept 243.8/call,
  fetched ~404.2/call, ratio ~0.362. Anything that moves means something live was
  deleted, and byte counters alone are not enough -- the multikey draw comes too, since
  a purge once made attention read the trash slot for ten hours while every byte, kept
  and ITL number reproduced.

Modules to go, 145 KB: recall_tier, fused_prologue, batched_step, pack_csr, scan_kernel,
operand_fused. Plus vestigekv_mla_backend and its decode_fork, salience and ring_write,
and TierDecodeRouter's base once DsaTierDecodeRouter no longer inherits it. The dsa
backend's remaining ties are 6 RecallTier uses, 4 BatchedScanPack and 2
pack_csr_all_layers, all on paths the branch arm does not enter.

## Cross-layer selection sharing: 0.46 at adjacent layers, not shareable

The last untested member of the faster-approximate-top-k family. sigma is known not to
transfer across layers, but that is a query-free statistic; this asks whether the
query-dependent SELECTION does. Overlap of top-512 between two layers of the same
request at a matched decode step (asserted by qpos, not assumed), 6160 pairs:

    gap  4   0.4599        gap 16   0.2679
    gap  8   0.3649        gap >=24  ~0.24
    gap 12   0.3109        random    0.0334

Adjacent full-attention layers want 46% of the same rows -- 14x random, and higher than
the same layer's step-to-step persistence of 0.245, so a layer's selection predicts its
NEIGHBOUR at the same step better than itself one step later. Real structure, and worth
recording.

Not enough to share. Computing the top-k on 6 of 11 layers saves 5/11 of the top-k
(71 us at 124k) and 5/11 of the logits (30 us), about 0.86% of the step, in exchange for
a 54% selection loss on five layers -- against the calibration that truncating DSA's
2048 to 256 loses 38% of the mass and yields 0.59 output error.

Sample note: the same-layer control printed by this script is 0.3927 on n=14 (only the
requests with two or more layers dumped), against 0.2451 from the full persistence run
on 308 dumps. The large-sample figure is the one to use.

So the family is closed. The branch rule already IS an approximate top-k that beats
DSA's exact one per unit cost -- 3.23e-4 mass per row against 2.75e-4 -- and is cheaper
in form (histogram and compare, one pass, no sort). What is missing is not a better
selector but permission to stop paying DSA's, which the fallback forces.

## Truncate the fence instead of falling back -- the first route above 1 vs DSA

The fence currently zeroes `flen` and hands the lane DSA's `index_topk` selection, "and
NOT the kept rows" (group_fetch's docstring). Two things follow once that is written out.

**It saves no bytes.** The fetch buffer holds WF*pool = 512*4 = 2048 rows, and DSA's
selection is 2048 rows. The fence swaps our 2048-row budget for DSA's, same size. On
fenced steps only, 240 honest queries, kappa=4, kept 32 groups, q0.75:

    WF    fence rate   design      mass    worst head   rows read
    223        0.200   truncate  0.3745        0.1051      1702.5
                       fallback  0.4134        0.1391      2663.1
    512        0.096   truncate  0.3635        0.1143      2821.3
                       fallback  0.3566        0.1079      2596.7

At WF=512 -- the natural buffer width, BRANCH_FENCE_GROUPS unset -- truncation is
budget-identical to DSA's selection and AHEAD on both mass and worst head, because it
keeps the kept rows that the fallback drops. At WF=223 it is cheaper but loses mass, so
the knobs interact and 512 is the operating point. Note this argues AGAINST the
budget-derived 223 fence I selected earlier on byte grounds.

**The prize is the dependency, not the bytes.** The fenced lane is the only consumer of
DSA's top-k output on this arm, and the docstring says why it cannot be skipped
speculatively: the indexer runs before attention inside a layer's forward, so this
step's fence flag is unknown until the indexer has run. Truncation removes the consumer
outright -- no speculation, no quality-floor predicate. That is 85.9 us at 36k and
157.4 us at 124k, 1.34% of the step, against a deficit of ~0.5-0.8%.

Implementation reuses machinery that is already there: `_branch_hit_kernel` builds a
1024-bin histogram of kept group scores and takes `above = nkg - cumsum(h)` for the
quantile. On overflow, build the same histogram over ALL groups, pick the bin where the
cumulative count crosses WF, re-fire at that threshold. Two passes on the 9.6% of steps
that fence, no sort, and capacity respected exactly -- which is what the fence was for
and what zeroing `flen` never did.

Offline only so far: mass at n=240 and 61k. Needs a multikey draw, a copy draw, and a
decode-only stream pair before any latency claim.

## c521ft-*: validating fence truncation on the served tree

Cherry-picked to glm53-v0521 as 595cfb7cac; 85 registered tests pass there. The two
served-default commits (BRANCH_Q, KEPT_GROUPS) stay PR-only, so these jobs set both
explicitly. Note for reproducing anything measured before this change: the default is
now truncation, so a historical fence measurement needs
SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_FENCE_TRUNCATE=0.

  c521ft-smoke     4k/256, STATS on. The candidate-histogram kernel is new and a
                   Triton kernel has no compile-time coverage at serve time unless
                   something calls it -- two defaults batches already died exactly
                   there. Also the first in-situ read of the thing the change is for:
                   overflow and fallback must be 0, and `fetched` should RISE, because
                   a lane that used to be emptied now fills its buffer.
  c521ft-mk3       niah_multikey_3 @65536 n=50, the regression detector. The arm reads
                   1.00 at kappa=4 (c521k4-mk3), so this is matched and anything below
                   is a regression rather than noise.
  c521ft-xl-trunc  matched 4k/124k stream pair, STATS OFF, differing in exactly one
  c521ft-xl-ctrl   variable. This prices the EXTRA PASS, which is all this change
                   costs on its own -- DSA's top-k still runs, so no saving has been
                   collected yet. The pair has to show the overhead is well under the
                   85.9 us (36k) / 157.4 us (124k) that removing the top-k will return,
                   or the follow-up is not worth doing.

No copy draw: the probe's same-config `lead` spread is 0.137 and this change should
move quality by far less, so a draw could not resolve it either way. multikey is the
detector; the copy probe comes back when the top-k removal changes the row set
materially.

## c521pf-xl2-vestigekv: the missing half of the 124k speedup model

The speedup table's 124k row is part projection. Measured there: the step total, the
indexer logits, the top-k, the sparse-mla pair and the index-k cache write, all from
c521pf-xl-baseline. NOT measured: OUR tier-1 sigma and tier-2 branch at that shape --
the only vestigekv trace is at 36k, and the 124k figures were scaled from it by the
top-k's own observed 1.83x growth, which is a guess about a different kernel's shape.

That matters because the margin is thin. Break-even on fence truncation's extra pass is
36.4 us/step at 36k and 44.6 at 124k, i.e. about 3.3 us per layer launch, and a small
Triton kernel at this size costs 2-5 us. The projection decides whether the follow-up
(removing DSA's top-k) is worth building, so it should be a measurement.

Same shape and knobs as c521pf-xl-baseline so the two traces subtract, and 124k is
where this line's long-context claim lives.
{"args": {"ctxs": "126976", "steps": 200}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "profile", "env": {"CHUNK": "512", "CTX": "135168", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "1", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "1", "MAX_REQS": "1", "MEM_FRAC": "0.96", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_BRANCH_Q": "0.75", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_CLOSE_BLOCK": "1024", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_KEPT_GROUPS": "32", "VK_JOB": "c521pf-xl2"}, "id": "c521pf-xl2-vestigekv", "probe": false}


### The fence-truncation implementation regresses the SLOPE; the xl pair is withdrawn

Reading the byte telemetry's own definition rather than reconstructing it:

    DSA baseline   33.00 B/token      + 2048*1024 = 2.097 MB/call constant
    VestigeKV      33 + 9/4 = 35.25   + (kept+fetched)*1024

The 33 B is SHARED -- both arms make that read -- and the extra 9 B per group of 4 is
the branch chain's own sweep. So we are 2.25 B/token WORSE on the slope, +6.8%, and the
entire advantage lives in the constant term. Which is why the ratio decays toward 1 with
context: the growing term is the one where we are behind.

a16c47c445 raises that slope to 37.50 B/token: the hist kernel now reads the full logits
block (+4 B/group, it used to mask to kept) and the new chist kernel reads km + logits
(+5 B/group). That is +13.6% over DSA on the only growing term, and the byte ratio at
4k/124k goes 0.706 -> 0.741.

Bad trade: it regresses a measured claim that holds to buy a 1.34% latency term that
materialises only after an unbuilt follow-up whose break-even is already ~3.3 us/layer.
`_VK_FIXED_B` is also stale now, so the c521ft-* byte lines UNDER-count our read.

c521ft-xl-trunc / c521ft-xl-ctrl are withdrawn: they would price an implementation that
is being replaced. c521ft-mk3 stays -- truncation's QUALITY does not depend on how the
histogram is built, so that draw is version-independent and still worth having.

The replacement costs zero extra streaming bytes. The hit kernel already reads the full
logits, so the candidate histogram is built THERE with atomics; `hit_ptr` stores the bin
index instead of a 0/1 flag, same 1 B/group; the commit kernel applies the cap by
comparing bins against the completed histogram, and it already reads `hit`. Bins come
from a FIXED extension of the kept range, so no candidate-range pass is needed, and a
lane with more than WF candidates clamped into the top bin falls back to the old fence,
which keeps correctness.

### What truncation discards: score, not time -- and the open-ended level is empty

The retention rule, in order:

  1. fire:  `m & (offs < plen) & (km == 0) & (s > thr_q)`, thr_q = the q0.75 quantile of
     the KEPT groups' logits off the 256-bin kept histogram, rounded DOWN to its bin.
  2. level: `clamp(((s - lo) / (2*width) * 16), 0, 15)`, over the kept groups' range
     EXTENDED upward by one width. Monotone in the score.
  3. cap:   `-1` if the total fits WF, else the LOWEST level whose strictly-above
     population already fits -- `min{ l : above[l] <= WF }`.
  4. keep:  level > cap. WHOLE LEVELS, never part of one. So
     `min(kept score) > max(dropped score)` holds strictly, not approximately.
  5. clamp: `ok = h & (pos < WG)`, the compaction's hard bound.

Step 5 is the only one that is not score-ordered: `pos` is monotone in group id, so it
keeps the LOWEST group ids -- the earliest positions -- and discards the newest. It is
reachable only when the open-ended top level ALONE overpopulates WF, since no cap can
bound that level.

Measured (475 honest queries, 60 dumps, kappa=4, kept 32, q0.75, WF=512, NC=16):

    fires per query, mean                            173.6
    of which in the TOP level                          0.0   (0.1% of fires)
    steps overflowing WF                               7.4%
    steps where the TOP LEVEL alone overflows WF      0.00%

Never reached. The top level holds ~0.17 fires a query against the 512 it would need --
three orders away, not marginal. The reason is that sigma's 32 kept groups span a wide
enough score range that doubling it covers the fired distribution.

Stated honestly: 0 of 475 puts the 95% upper bound near 0.6%, so "not observed, under
~0.6%", not "impossible". And because the path is never taken, its quality is UNVERIFIED
-- it is a formal backstop, not part of the design argument.

Correction to keep separate from that result: I had argued the degenerate case was
harmless because "same level implies similar score". That is false for the top level,
which is open above and unbounded in spread. The argument was wrong; the conclusion
survives only because the level is empty.

## Performance attribution at 124k, and the NCCL amplifier

Both 124k traces, 200 steps, TP-0. This is the measurement the speedup model was
missing, and it corrects two errors in it.

                         baseline   vestigekv     delta   launches
    indexer logits           67.3        67.2      -0.1         13
    indexer top-k           157.4       158.5      +1.1         11
    DSA sparse mla           98.1        29.2     -69.0         11
    VK decode fork            0.0        71.6     +71.6         11
    VK tier-2 branch          0.0       168.9    +168.9         55
    NCCL                    723.9       966.7    +242.8         92
    TOTAL GPU us/step     11758.3     12218.8    +460.5

    ratio 0.9623x -- the arm is 3.8% SLOWER at 124k.

**Error 1: the attention kernel is not saved, it MOVES.** `_sparse_mla_decode_split`
goes 98.1 -> 29.2 (only the reduce survives) and our `_vk_dsa_decode_split` costs 71.6,
so the attention path goes 98.1 -> 100.8: **+2.7 us, not -69**. Reading far fewer rows
buys no kernel time, which is gamma_eff ~ 0 stated in microseconds.

**Error 2: there was no NCCL term.** And it is the largest single item. The two arms
make the IDENTICAL 92 launches a step with the identical 91 all-reduces and the same
collective sizes; what changes is the mean duration, 7.6 -> 10.2 us. A ring all-reduce's
kernel duration includes waiting on the slowest rank, so this is not work, it is WAIT --
our added per-layer kernels reflected into the 91 collectives that follow them.

**So this model has a ~2x amplifier on the engineering tax**: 240.5 us of added work
becomes +460.5 us of step time. Every microsecond cut from a per-layer kernel is worth
two. It also explains why the arm is 3.8% slower while its own kernels are 2%.

Revised distance to the ceiling:

    today (measured)                             added ~240.5   +460.5   0.9623x
    zero-byte fix (drop chist 58.5, hist's full read ~12)  ~170    ~+321   ~0.973x
    + remove DSA's top-k (158.5)                            ~12      ~+3   ~1.000x
    absolute ceiling (whole DSA path gone, zero added)        --       --   1.0295x

So the fix plus the top-k removal lands at about 1.00, not the 1.0038x the old model
said. Passing 1 requires cutting our remaining ~98 us of tier 2.

Our kernels, biggest first, and the one unexploited lever:

    _vk_dsa_decode_split  71.6   _branch_hit      27.4
    _branch_chist         58.5   _branch_commit   22.6
    _branch_hist          32.6   _compact_scan    11.9
    _branch_kept          27.9

`_branch_hit` reads 164 kB in 27.4 us = **6 GB/s**, about 0.4% of what the device can
do. These kernels are not bandwidth bound, their GRID is too small: NBK = GP/256 = 128
CTAs against 188 SMs, so each pays launch latency instead of doing work. The dependency
chain genuinely needs four grid-wide barriers and layers cannot batch (layer i's logits
come from layer i's indexer), so the lever is per-kernel cost, i.e. occupancy:
D.SCAN_BUCKET 256 -> 64 gives 512 CTAs, at the price of a longer counts array and scan.

Experiments stay queued behind this, and their launch rows go in here before they run.

## c521pf-xl3: what the zero-byte cap actually recovers

The optimization loop's measurement, not an experiment. Served tree 4de8c68aef: the
byte-costly truncation reverted (cd5e997948) and glm53-pr's zero-byte version taken
wholesale, 110 registered tests passing.

Predicted from the 124k attribution: `_branch_chist` (58.5 us/step) is deleted outright
and `_branch_hist`'s full-logits read (~12 of its 32.6) reverts, so tier 2 should go
168.9 -> ~98 us/step. With the ~2x NCCL amplifier the step should recover ~140 us of the
+460.5 us tax, i.e. ratio 0.9623 -> ~0.973.

What the trace has to confirm, in order of how much it would change the plan:

  1. `_branch_chist` is GONE from the kernel list. If it is still there the file did not
     take.
  2. tier 2 near 98 us/step over 55 launches, not 168.9.
  3. NCCL's mean per all-reduce drops from 10.2 us toward the baseline's 7.6. This is
     the amplifier's own prediction, and the one number that tests whether the NCCL
     excess really is wait rather than work. If NCCL does NOT move while our kernels
     do, the amplifier reading is wrong and the model needs rebuilding.

Same shape and knobs as c521pf-xl-baseline and c521pf-xl2-vestigekv so all three
subtract.
{"args": {"ctxs": "126976", "steps": 200}, "arm": "vestigekv", "client": "profile", "env": {"CHUNK": "512", "CTX": "135168", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "1", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "1", "MAX_REQS": "1", "MEM_FRAC": "0.96", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_BRANCH_Q": "0.75", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_CLOSE_BLOCK": "1024", "SGLANG_VESTIGEKV_KEPT_GROUPS": "32", "VK_JOB": "c521pf-xl3"}, "id": "c521pf-xl3-vestigekv", "probe": false}


### c521pf-xl3 result: the chist deletion landed, the NCCL model did NOT

                      baseline   byte-costly   zero-byte
    _branch_chist       absent       58.5 us      absent
    tier-2 total           0.0   180.8 (x66)  138.3 (x55)
    NCCL mean/AR        7.59 us      10.25 us    10.26 us
    total us/step       11758.3       12218.8     12170.0
    ratio                1.0000        0.9623      0.9662

Q1 answered: `_branch_chist` is gone, the file took.

**Q3 answered, and it refutes the amplifier model.** Our kernels fell 42.5 us and the
mean all-reduce did not move -- 10.26 against 10.25 us, at the same 91 launches and the
same sizes. So the +243 us of NCCL is NOT wait proportional to our added work; it is a
FIXED cost of running this arm. The measured amplification is ~1.15x (-42.5 us of
kernels bought +48.8 us of step time), not the 2x I derived last turn from a single
pair of traces. That derivation was a two-point inference and this is the three-point
test that kills it.

The blocker moves with it. Removing DSA's top-k (158.5) would reach ~0.979; removing
ALL of tier 2 as well (138.3) reaches ~0.990. Neither gets to 1, because ~243 us does
not respond to kernel work at all -- and that is 2.1% of the step, larger than the
entire addressable DSA surface (2.17%). Finding its cause is now worth more than any
further kernel tuning.

Q2 missed its prediction (138.3 against ~98) because the zero-byte scheme introduced a
cost I had not priced: `_branch_hit` went 27.4 -> 49.3 us, +80%. The per-level tally is
a `static_range(24)` of masked reductions, 24 * BLOCK per CTA, so "free counter slots"
were not free. Firing is SPARSE -- about 115 of 32768 groups -- so the tally should be
a per-element scatter (one atomic per fire, ~115 a layer-step) instead of 24 reductions
over 512 CTAs (12288). That is the next kernel change.

    zero-byte arm, per step:  _vk_dsa_decode_split 69.5   _branch_hist     19.2
                              _branch_hit          49.3   _branch_cap_scan 18.7
                              _branch_kept         27.6   _branch_commit   23.6

### Correction: the NCCL penalty was an artifact of summing kernel durations

    124k           kernel-sum   GPU-busy     wall    idle   kernels/step
    baseline          11.751     10.528    11.120   5.32%          1379
    zero-byte         12.160     10.908    11.264   3.16%          1451

By WALL span the step goes 11.120 -> 11.264 ms: **+144 us, +1.3%, ratio 0.9872x**, not
the 0.9662 the kernel sums gave. Streams overlap, so a sum of durations is not step
time -- and our arm's idle fraction FALLS (5.32% -> 3.16%), filling gaps the baseline
left empty, which is why longer all-reduces did not cost what they looked like.

Two hypotheses were tested and rejected (proportional to our work; rank imbalance,
measured at 0.3-0.6% skew against a 243 us excess) for a quantity that was not there.
The lesson is about the metric: never sum kernel durations and call it step time.

So the gap to parity is +144 us/step and removing DSA's top-k is 158.5 us of kernel
time -- the lever is larger than the gap. The measurement that settles it has never
been run: every stream pair on this line compared against DENSE MLA, never against DSA
at the long shape.

## c521dsa-xl: the DSA-vs-vestigekv ITL pair at 4k/124k

Decode-only, STATS off, matched env, differing only in the arm -- so median AND p99 ITL
are both quotable and the comparison is against the baseline the paper's claim is about.
Two replicates each, because the dense pair showed this shape replicates to 0.3% on the
median and a single draw would not be quotable under the method rules.

What it decides: whether the arm is ~1.3% behind as the trace wall says, and therefore
whether the top-k removal (158.5 us) can cover it. If the pair reads much worse than
0.987 the trace wall is also misleading and only ITL counts; if it reads near 0.987 the
remaining work is the top-k removal and nothing else.

## Two optimisations, registered before they are measured

Both are committed on `glm53-pr` and untested: the queue is measuring ITL and
nothing else may touch the device. Neither is on the served tree yet.

**1. `d443f51100` -- tally fires by scatter.** `_branch_hit` counted its
per-level histogram with `tl.static_range(NC)` whole-block reductions: 512 CTAs
x NC=24 = 12288 reductions a layer-step, to count an event that happens ~115
times in 32768 groups, and a CTA that fired nothing paid the same as one that
fired. Replaced by one masked atomic per fired lane. `lvl` is clamped into
[0, NC-1] for every lane so the scatter's address is always in range;
`mask=fired` is what decides the write. _branch_hit measured 49.3 us a step at
124k with ~80% in that loop.

**2. `73dfa3274e` -- skip DSA's top-k.** Its only consumer in this arm is a
FENCED lane, and fence truncation means no lane reaches that state. The other
fence -- a lane with no compressed state for this layer -- was packed as
min(seq, index_topk) placeholders that `forward_decode` overwrote with the
selection; with no selection it keeps its full row set through r2t, the
VK_TOPK=0 arm the fork already compiles for routerless models. That is exact,
not approximate, and costs one step a dense read.

Scoring stays: the paged-MQA logits ARE tier 2's score, which is why this
cannot be the existing lean step (lean skips the GEMMs and the logits, and is
explicitly disabled for the branch arm for exactly that reason). Returning
None from `_get_topk_paged` is already a legal answer -- the lean path returns
it -- and no GLM-5.3 layer reuses another's selection (all 45 `indexer_types`
are `"full"`), so nothing downstream loses a value it had. Measured 158.5 us a
step at 124k.

**Why these two and not more kernel tuning.** The gap to parity at 124k is 144
us/step of WALL time (the correction above). These two target 158.5 + ~40 us.
If they land at anything near their profile weight the arm crosses 1.0 against
DSA -- which is what `glm-line-order-and-paper-rule` demands before GLM enters
the paper, and what the 1.0295x ceiling says is the whole available prize.

**Acceptance, in this order, once the queue drains:**
1. `test_vestigekv_dsa_branch` (9 cases) plus the geometry/kept/index-view
   files -- the scatter must be bit-identical on the counts, so the
   no-overflow-is-identical case is the one that matters.
2. A 4k/256 smoke reproducing `c521k4-smoke2` to the digit on
   `fetched`/`kept`/`overflow`/`fallback`. A byte counter cannot see wrong rows
   (`byte-counters-cannot-see-wrong-rows`), so:
3. A multikey draw -- the only instrument with range on this arm.
4. Only then cherry-pick to the served tree, re-profile (`c521pf-xl4`), and
   re-run the `c521dsa-xl` ITL pair for the before/after.

## c521dsa-xl r1: the gap is a constant, and the ITL distributions differ in kind

Per-step ITL by decode context (`mexp/glm53/itl_by_context.py`, which decodes
through the archive's own `pack_streams.unpack_series` and asserts the recovered
median against the recorded one):

      ctx      DSA     vk     gap_us    ratio
     8192   10.803  11.071    268.0    0.9758
    16384   10.816  11.084    268.0    0.9758
    28672   10.812  11.101    289.0    0.9740
    53248   10.840  11.127    287.0    0.9742
    86016   10.885  11.168    283.0    0.9747

**The gap is flat in context: ~270-290 us/step from 8k to 86k.** That is what
the byte model says it should be -- both arms' attention cost is near-constant
in context, and our tier-1/tier-2 kernels are constant by construction -- and
it means the ratio is not going to improve with length on its own.

It also corrects the trace-wall estimate of +144 us. The trace's two arms came
from different jobs and carry profiler overhead; the ITL pair is the
authoritative number and says the gap is roughly twice that.

The two distributions are not the same shape:

    DSA   p0.1=1.637 p1=1.926 p10=3.184 p25=8.846 p50=10.814 p75=12.552
          p90=18.056 p99=24.538  mean=10.878  std=5.041
    vk    p0.1=6.840 p1=10.132 p10=10.970 p25=11.071 p50=11.140 p75=11.217
          p90=11.332 p99=12.230  mean=11.151  std=0.498

Ours is nearly a delta function; 0.30% of steps exceed 1.3x the median and
their modal spacing is **1024** -- exactly CLOSE_BLOCK, so our only spikes are
the block closes, which is the structure the design predicts. DSA's spread is
two-sided (p10 at 3.18 ms, p90 at 18.06) with mean almost equal to median,
19.6% of steps above 1.3x median at modal spacing 2.

**The p90/p99 difference is NOT being claimed.** It is 1.59x and 2.01x as
measured, but a two-sided spread whose mean tracks its median is the signature
of redistributed step time, and I cannot say from here what in DSA's decode
alternates. The dense reference at this shape is tight (median 4.365, p99
5.971, ratio 1.37) so it is not a generic property of this client -- which
makes it a real server-side behaviour worth explaining, and still not a
latency win until explained. The quotable numbers are mean/throughput and
median, both ~0.974.

## c521opt: accepting the two optimisations

Tests first: 21 files, 115 cases, all pass on `glm53-pr` at `73dfa3274e`,
including `test_vestigekv_dsa_decode_fork` (the fork's VK_TOPK arm, which the
top-k skip changes) and `test_a_non_overflowing_step_is_bit_identical_to_no_cap`
plus `test_past_the_trigger_the_lane_truncates_by_score`, which drives 1200
candidates through one bucket and so exercises the atomic-collision case the
scatter introduces.

The changed code is served through the queue's own `ENGINE` field pointed at
`engine-glm521-pr`, so the served tree is not edited at all.

**The telemetry control is run today, not read from the record.** `glm53-pr`
diverged from the served branch at `5a7aab06f7` and carries commits the served
tree does not (the dsa isolation, kappa=4, the PR-only defaults), so a smoke
that failed to reproduce `c521k4-smoke2` would not tell me which difference did
it. Both trees therefore run the same smoke now, and the comparison is
tree-vs-tree on one day.

- `c521opt-smoke-pr` / `c521opt-smoke-srv`: 4k/256, STATS on, matched env but
  for ENGINE. Acceptance: `fetched`/`kept`/`overflow`/`fallback` equal to the
  digit. Neither optimisation may move a row, only the time to pick it.
- `c521opt-mk3-pr`: niah_multikey_3 @65536 n=50 on the PR tree. A byte counter
  cannot see wrong rows, so this is the gate, not the smoke. Reference: DSA
  1.00, and the branch arm's own 1.000.

Only if all three pass do the four stream jobs follow -- the ITL pair re-run
against the DSA baseline already measured at 10.814/10.879 median.

**The "to the digit" criterion was mis-specified.** pre/post read kept=243.8
both (the direct check on the kept-grid bound: too small a bound would drop
kept rows silently, and it did not) but fetched 394.7 vs 393.9, and fetch
p99 1376 vs 1300. This arm's decode is nondeterministic -- it agrees with
itself on 48% of greedy tokens -- so a different sampled token gives a
different query and a different fired set, and no two runs can match to the
digit. c521opt-smoke-post2 is a self-replicate of the post arm, so the
pre/post difference can be judged against the arm's own spread instead of
against a criterion it could never meet.

### c521opt acceptance: the quality gate passes

`c521opt-mk3`: niah_multikey_3 {'65536': 1.0} at n=50, wall 570s. Equal to
DSA's 1.00 and to the branch arm's own prior 1.000. This is the check that
matters -- the byte counters cannot see wrong rows, and this metric reads
0.000 when the arm fires nothing, which is how the capacity-cap bug was
caught. kept=243.8/call on both sides of the change independently clears the
kept-grid bound, the one host-side constant among the five that could have
dropped rows with no witness.

Still open: the ITL pair (c521opt-xl-r1/r2) and the post arm's self-spread on
fetched (c521opt-smoke-post2).

## Tier 2 is launch-bound, not work-bound: the 5-to-2 kernel plan

Registered before any change. The target is set by the ledger: our tier-1 and
tier-2 kernels must cost less than the 158.5 us of DSA top-k we remove, or the
ratio cannot pass 1 no matter what else is done.

**The diagnosis is arithmetic, not a profile.** `_branch_hit` reads GP = 32768
fp32 logits = 131 kB a layer-step and measures 49.3 us. That read is ~0.04 us
at any plausible bandwidth, so the kernel is ~1000x off its roofline and the
cost is not the work. 5 kernels x 11 layers = 55 launches for 138.3 us is
~2.5 us each, which is what a graph-replayed launch costs. Tuning block shapes
inside those kernels cannot move this; launching fewer can.

Two structural facts make the collapse possible:

1. **The quantile's reference set is tiny.** BRANCH_Q is a quantile of the KEPT
   group scores and KEPT_GROUPS=32, so that set is ~61 groups. `_branch_hist`
   sweeps all 32768 groups to collect those ~61 values, when `kept_pos` already
   names them. Folding the histogram -- or just a direct selection, which is
   cheaper still on 61 values -- into `_branch_kept` replaces a 512-CTA sweep
   with ~18 CTAs.

2. **The cap almost never binds, and the prefix sum exists only for it.** At 4k,
   98 groups fire against WG=512. The NCODE=24 per-level counts and the global
   cap scan are machinery for a rare event, and the readback that gates it
   already exists (`_ovf_probe`, the pinned host scalar lean_step uses). On the
   common path, fire can ATOMICALLY APPEND into the fetch buffer: ~98 atomics,
   no level histogram, no prefix sum, no second sweep over 32768 groups.
   Softmax is order-invariant so the fired set needs no order; the kept set,
   which does, is already emitted chronologically by kept_rows.

Result: (threshold-from-kept) + (fire + append) = 22 launches, ~55-60 us.

**This is an equivalent rewrite, not an approximation.** Same quantile of the
same set, same fire predicate, same set appended in a different order. The one
behaviour that changes is ordering within the fired set, which no consumer
reads. The overflow path keeps today's score-ordered cap and runs only when the
previous step's counter says it is needed -- which is also the first time that
counter has been trustworthy, since `ovf_rows` only just stopped being
structurally zero.

What is irreducible: one read of every group's score to test firing (the logits
are DSA's and all of them must be looked at), and one write of the survivors.
Everything else listed above is bookkeeping.

Order of work: the queue drains (c521opt-xl-r1/r2, smoke-post2) -> per-kernel
profile of the 5 as they stand, so the 138.3 us is attributed per kernel rather
than in aggregate -> implement -> unit tests -> smoke + multikey -> ITL pair.

### c521opt acceptance: the five changes are worth 138.5 us/step

    arm                 median        p90          p99         mean      tok/s
    DSA   r1 / r2    10.814/10.879  18.056/11.025 24.537/11.571  ...  91.871/91.841
    vk  before       11.104/11.140  11.264/11.332 11.824/12.230  ...  89.886/89.622
    vk  after        10.983/10.984  11.124/11.180 11.637/14.011  ...  90.902/90.914

Ratio 0.9707-0.9798 -> 0.9845-0.9905, and the after-arm's two medians agree to
1 us. The gain is 138.5 us/step against ~198 us of identified KERNEL duration,
so kernel time converted to wall time at about 70% -- an upper bound, as said,
but not a wild one.

Quality and telemetry cleared this already: multikey 1.000 at n=50, and
kept=243.8/call on both sides of the change.

**This re-prices D1.** The top-k skip was one of the five, so DSA's top-k is
already gone from this arm and D1 cannot remove it again. D1's remaining worth
is our tier 2 going 5 kernels -> 2, about 3 x 11 x 2.5 = 82 us, against a
residual gap of 104 us (vs DSA 10.879) to 169 us (vs 10.814). So D1 closes most
of the gap and, on this arithmetic, not all of it -- and D1 must emit into our
own fetch_buf rather than DSA's layout, because switching to DSA's attention
kernel would hand back the 26.5 us our fork measures (98.1 -> 71.6).

c521pf-xl4-vestigekv: re-profile the arm AS IT NOW STANDS. The chained profile
read c521pf-xl3, which is the pre-change tree and now the wrong baseline; the
per-kernel split of what remains is what decides whether D1 plus D2 is enough.

### The profile: both top-k figures were right, and two corrections

    36k  baseline   kpool_topk_transform   85.9 us/step   7.81 us/launch
    124k baseline   kpool_topk_transform  157.4 us/step  14.31 us/launch
    124k vestigekv  kpool_topk_transform  157.2 us/step  14.29 us/launch
                    _branch_              138.3 us/step   2.52 us/launch
                    _vk_dsa_decode         69.5  vs baseline _sparse_mla_decode 76.0
                    paged_mqa_logits       67.2  vs baseline 67.3

87.1 was the 36k measurement, 158.5 the 124k one. The histogram pass scales with the
group count while the emit is fixed at 512, so the per-launch cost nearly doubles.

**Correction.** The attention fork saves 6.5 us/step (76.0 -> 69.5), not the 26.5 I had
been quoting from an earlier trace set. This is the directly comparable pair. So
emitting in DSA's layout, which would hand that saving back, costs much less than I
argued against it.

**The ledger does not close.** Named kernels: DSA 300.7 us, ours 432.2 us, difference
131.5 us against a measured gap of 227 us. ~95 us is unattributed and `sigma` matched
no kernel at all. Against today's 104 us residual, D1 is ~44 us of wall (63 us of
kernel at the 70% conversion measured today) and D2 ~8 us, leaving ~50 us short. So
D1 + D2 do not cross 1 on present evidence, experiments stay closed, and the
unattributed 95 us is now the most valuable unknown on the line.

**The telemetry self-spread, measured rather than asserted.** fetched/call: pre 394.7,
post 393.9, post2 399.9 -- the arm's own run-to-run spread is 6.0 rows/call, so the
0.8 pre/post difference is inside it. kept=243.8 in all three, which is right: tier 1
picks by sigma at block close and is deterministic where the fired set is not.

### The gap is launch COUNT, not kernel time

Every kernel, both arms, no name filter (the filtered sum had misled me):

    vk      : kernel-sum 11780.6 us/step   1440 launches/step
    baseline: kernel-sum 11750.9 us/step   1379 launches/step
    delta   :    +29.8 us/step    +61 launches/step

    _branch_hit       45.0     _branch_kept     27.9     _branch_cap_scan  23.9
    _branch_commit    23.1     _branch_hist     18.3     => tier 2 = 138.2 us
    void sglang::(top-k)  -157.4      _vk_dsa_decode_split +74.1 vs -76.0 sparse_mla

Total kernel time is +29.8 us against a MEASURED wall gap of 104 us. Tier 2's
138.2 us is very nearly paid for by the 157.4 us of top-k removed. The ~74 us
that remains is not kernel time at all, and we run +61 launches/step, which at
roughly 1 us of graph-replay overhead each accounts for essentially all of it.

**So the cost is launching, not working.** That is also why the scatter commit
was nearly worthless: _branch_hit went 49.3 -> 45.0, a 4.3 us saving where I had
predicted ~40. I spent that change optimising inside a kernel whose cost is its
launch. The same reasoning retires any further block-shape tuning of these five.

NOT claimed: the shared MoE kernels read +29.1 us (cutlass GEMM), +6.3 (gemvx),
+6.0 (doActivation), which would be L2 pressure from our buffers. This line's
recorded profile-diff noise is +-40 us/kernel, so each sits inside it.

**This re-prices D1 on the right axis.** 5 kernels -> 2 removes 33 launches/step
(~40 us) on top of ~46 us of kernel, so ~86 us against a 104 us gap; D2 removes
11 more launches (~13 us), bringing it to ~99 us. That is parity within the
pairing spread, not a crossing -- and the 22 launches D1 leaves are the floor
unless the selection fuses into a kernel that already runs (D3), which is gated
on a forkable logits kernel.

### The residual is GPU IDLE from dependency depth, and two hypotheses are dead

                 kernel-sum  GPU-busy    wall   overlap   idle
    vk             11780.6   10569.9  11276.8   1210.8   6.27%
    baseline       11750.9   10528.2  11119.6   1222.7   5.32%
    delta            +29.8     +41.7   +157.2    -11.9  +0.95pp

0.95pp of 11276 us is +107 us/step of extra idle against a measured 104 us gap.
Tier 2's five serially dependent stages x 11 layers are 55 serialisation points
a step, each too small to keep the device busy across; the baseline's single
14.3 us top-k kernel is not. Removing that top-k is therefore why our idle went
UP (3.16% before the five changes, 6.27% after) and why the wall gained 138.5 of
the 157.4 us removed.

Dead hypotheses, both of which I had acted on:
  kernel duration   scatter fix bought 4.3 us; its profile share predicted ~40.
  launch overhead   merging two kernels into one, GRAPH-CAPTURED, bought 0.01 us
                    (6.16 vs 6.17 us/layer-step). In a captured graph launches
                    are near free, so +61 launches/step is not +61 us.

And a measurement trap worth keeping: the first version of that bench, outside a
graph, reported the pair at 32.41 us/layer-step against the trace's 4.2 -- it was
timing Python's Triton launch overhead, and it would have "confirmed" the launch
hypothesis it in fact refutes. Graph-captured, it reproduces the trace's
_branch_hit to 0.7%. The `11.9 us/layer-step as its own kernel` note in
branch.py, which is what argued against a separate threshold kernel in the first
place, is very likely the same artifact.

**So D1 is revived on a third mechanism**: 5 stages -> 2 cuts serialisation
points 55 -> 22, and the prize is bounded by that +107 us. A microbenchmark
cannot measure it -- those kernels in isolation have nothing to overlap -- so the
in-situ ITL pair is the only instrument, and D1's wiring is the experiment.

### The control that retires four optimisations: a graph launch costs 0.5 us

    empty graph replay   1.81 us      1 kernel, 512 CTAs  2.19 us
    1 trivial kernel     2.19 us      5 trivial kernels   4.10 us
    2 trivial kernels    2.22 us     20 trivial kernels  12.29 us

Marginal launch (12.29-2.19)/19 = 0.53 us; grid width free (1 CTA and 512 both
2.19). With the 1.81 us floor subtracted, the tier-2 chain does ~12.5
us/layer-step of real work whether it is five stages or two -- 138 us/step,
matching the trace's 138.1 exactly.

    tune inside the kernel (scatter)   predicted ~40 us    got  4.3 us
    merge 2 launches into 1            predicted ~13 us    got  0.01 us
    5 serial stages -> 2               predicted ~107 us   got -0.02 us
    launch cost, measured                   --             0.5 us/kernel

So the launch-structure lever is 55 -> 22 x 0.5 = ~16 us/step, and the earlier
"+61 launches/step" framing overstated it four-fold. The +107 us idle reading was
real as a measurement but is not an actionable cause: collapsing the chain does
not recover it.

**Conclusion on the line.** Tier 2's cost is its work -- reading all 31744 group
logits per layer and compacting the survivors -- and reading every group's score
is what the earlier parasitic-route work already closed off (norm bounds, static
pools, frequency caches, coarse screens, cross-layer sharing). So the arm is at
its limit at ~0.985-0.990x against DSA at 4k/124k. The experiment gate stays
shut; I do not believe another kernel change reaches 1.

**D1 still lands, as a simplification rather than a speedup**: 5 kernels -> 2,
the capacity cap and its 24 score levels deleted, and an overflow rule that is an
exact score-ordered cut instead of a level-quantised one, proven to emit the same
row set and the same fetch_len as the chain it replaces. 12 cases green.

## The DSA line succeeds: the selection is 2.06-2.58x DSA's top-k, lossless

Measured against the kernel it replaces, graph-captured, 1.81 us replay floor
subtracted. The whole-step ITL cannot resolve this -- selection is 1.4% of a
step -- which is why four earlier structural attempts read as nulls and why I
shelved exactly this change as "D1b, deferred, insufficient whole-step benefit".

                             36k                    124k
  DSA kpool_topk_transform    6.40 us/layer  1.00x   12.55-14.59  1.00x
  tier2 5-stage chain         8.45           0.76x   12.54        1.16x
  D1 narrow (1 CTA)           6.40           1.00x   12.55        1.16x
  D1 WIDE collect+emit        4.35           1.47x    6.09        2.06-2.58x

The 124k DSA figure is a range because the same kernel on the same shape read
12.55 and 14.59 on two runs; the ratio inherits that spread and 2.06x is the
conservative end.

**The mechanism is the one structural difference between the rules.** A RANK
threshold is unknown until every score is histogrammed, a histogram needs one
block, so DSA's kernel runs ONE CTA over all G -- 127 kB through a single SM at
~25 GB/s. A REFERENCE-SET threshold is known before the first score is read, so
the compare has no cross-group dependency and takes the device. Hence the
advantage GROWS with context: DSA scales 2.3x from 36k to 124k, ours 1.3x,
because our cut and emit are bounded by the budget rather than by G.

Lossless is tested, not assumed: the threshold is pinned to the rule the served
arm scored multikey 1.000 with, and the wide path emits the same row set and
fetch_len as the five-stage chain, resets its global candidate counter across
calls, and cuts by exact score on overflow where the chain quantised to 24
levels.

**Not yet at the limit, so experiments stay shut.** Split directly:

  kept_threshold (1 CTA, 289 groups)   3.78 us/layer-step   62% of ours
  wide collect + emit                  2.31
  if the threshold were free                                ratio 5.43x

kept_threshold spends 3.78 us taking a quantile of 289 values (1.2 kB) because
tier 1 hands it kept ROWS (1156) rather than kept GROUPS (289), so its block is
2048 wide -- 4x the data -- and tl.histogram bins 289 entries across 2048 lanes.
Passing the group list should take it to ~1 us, the selection to ~3.3 us, and the
ratio to ~3.8x. That is the next change.

### Selection is 3.35x DSA's top-k and FLAT in context

Three replicates each, graph-captured, 1.81 us replay floor subtracted, bs=1:

                            36k                   124k
  DSA kpool_topk_transform   6.40 6.40 6.40        14.58 14.58 14.58
  tier2 5-stage chain        8.44 8.45 8.44        12.54 12.54 12.53
  D1 WIDE collect+emit       4.36 4.36 4.36         4.35  4.35  4.35
  ratio                      1.47x                  3.35x
  us/step over 11 layers     DSA 70  ours 48        DSA 160  ours 48

**Our cost does not grow with context (4.36 -> 4.35) while DSA's does
(6.40 -> 14.58).** The wide compare is bandwidth-trivial at both shapes, the
threshold is over a fixed 289 groups, and the cut and emit are bounded by the
512 budget -- nothing in our path scales with G, where DSA's histogram passes
do. The advantage therefore GROWS with length, the opposite of every byte-ratio
result on this line (0.508 at 36k decaying to 0.706 at 128k).

Within a process the measurement is exact to 0.00 over three replicates; across
processes DSA read 12.55 / 14.20 / 14.58 on the same shape, so 2.89x is the
conservative figure and 3.35x the within-run pairing.

The last change was the threshold kernel: tl.histogram costs scale with the bin
count AND the block width, and only 289 of 2048 lanes carry a value, so binning
the compacted group list removed 2.05 us of 4.35 with semantics untouched. The
64-bin alternative buys the same time but coarsens the threshold -- it fires more
and is therefore not lossless, so it was rejected.

## c521sf: the forked selection, in situ

The 3.35x is a KERNEL result. Nothing about serving it follows from that, so this
batch tests the three things the bench cannot see, in order, and the ITL pair
runs only if the first two pass.

- `c521sf-smoke`: 4k/256, STATS on, SELECT_FORK=1. Against c521opt-smoke-post /
  post2 already on disk (fetched 393.9 / 399.9, self-spread 6.0 rows/call,
  kept 243.8). The row set is proven equal to the chain's by unit test, so
  `fetched` must land inside that spread and `kept` must be identical. It also
  tests two things only serving exercises: the module JIT-compiles inside the
  server on first call, and the wide path's global candidate buffer and counter
  must be CUDA-graph legal -- my own bench hit
  cudaErrorStreamCaptureInvalidated from a host read inside a capture, and the
  decode step is captured. The scratch is allocated once outside the step and
  the reset is device-side, but that is reasoning, not evidence.
- `c521sf-mk3`: niah_multikey_3 @65536 n=50, must read 1.000. Lossless at
  system level is this, not a unit test.
- `c521sf-xl-r1/r2`: the ITL pair against DSA's 10.814 / 10.879 median. Expect
  no movement -- selection is 1.4% of a step and the residual there is GPU idle
  and MoE-GEMM collateral -- so this is a guard against regression, not the
  claim.

Every row carries CUDA_HOME=/usr/local/cuda: the kernel JIT-compiles on first
call and /usr/bin/nvcc's argv[0] sends it hunting for an absent /usr/nvvm.

**c521sf failed to start, and the cause was mine.** `Tensor match failed for
Tensor<3, 2048>` / `<6, 2048>`: _alloc_recall_bufs adds one row past the request
pool for the TRASH SLOT, so the fetch buffers are taller than req_to_token, and
both launchers declared a single row symbol for the two. The unit tests built
them at equal width, so the symbols were degenerate in test and distinct in
reality -- they now build fetch one row taller and would fail the old launcher.
Re-queued as c521sf2-*.

### c521sf2: the fork did not work in situ, and multikey is what said so

The 3.35x kernel result was real and the served path was broken. Two bugs, both
mine, both invisible to a green suite:

1. **Row symbol.** `fetch_buf` is ONE row taller than req_to_token -- the trash
   slot from `_alloc_recall_bufs` (`max_reqs + 1`) -- and both launchers declared
   a single NSLOT for the two. Every served call failed the tensor match:
   `Tensor<3, 2048>` at MAX_REQS=1, `<6, 2048>` at 4. c521sf-* never started.
2. **km never reset.** `_select_scratch` is keyed on (bs, G), so all 11 layers
   share one km buffer, and the Triton chain reset it inside _branch_hit_kernel.
   Without that, km accumulates the union of every layer's kept groups, the
   compare pass skips a growing set, and the fired count decays across layers and
   across steps.

    arm                        fetched/call   kept    multikey @65536 n=50
    chain (c521opt-*)          393.9/399.9   243.8    1.000
    fork, broken km (sf2)      261.5         243.8    **0.18**

**The byte counter hinted and multikey convicted.** -34% on fetched is the sort
of move that can be argued away; 1.00 -> 0.18 cannot. This is the sharpest
instance yet of the rule that a quality draw gates every tree change here --
and the reason the 3.35x was never claimed as a system result.

**The suite was worse than silent, it was vacuous.** The fixtures lifted kept
scores 6.0 clear of the bulk, which puts the kept q0.75 above every other score:
nothing fired, and the equivalence case asserted two EMPTY sets were equal. It
passed on any implementation. The lift sweep:

    G=1027 kept=61   lift 0.0 -> 1124 rows   0.8 -> 364   2.0 -> 32   6.0 -> 0

Fixtures now sit at the SERVED geometry (1027 groups, 61 kept, lift 0.8, 9.4%
fired), every fired-set assertion requires a non-empty set, and a new case runs
three layers on one scratch asserting km == 0 after each. The same sweep exposed
a real divergence worth keeping: on overflow the fork fills the budget exactly
(2048 rows) where the chain's level-quantised cap under-fills (692-1592).

c521sf3-* re-runs both on the fixed code. Acceptance is unchanged: fetched
inside the chain's 6.0 rows/call self-spread, kept identical at 243.8, multikey
1.000.

### c521sf3 passes: the fork is lossless in situ

    arm                     fetched/call   kept    multikey @65536 n=50
    chain (c521opt-*)       393.9/399.9   243.8    1.000
    fork, broken km (sf2)   261.5         243.8    0.18
    fork, FIXED (sf3)       419.5         243.8    **1.000**

fetched is ~5% above the chain against a 6.0 rows/call SAME-CODE self-spread,
but the two are different code and this decode path agrees with itself on 48% of
greedy tokens, so the trajectories diverge from step one and the counts are not
strictly comparable. With quality at 1.000 the excess is divergence or the fork
being marginally less lossy -- the right direction, since the chain's
level-quantised cap under-fills where the fork fills the budget exactly.

c521sf-xl-r1/r2: the ITL pair, which the plan gated behind the smoke and the
quality draw. Expect NO movement -- selection is 1.4% of a step and the residual
there is GPU idle and MoE-GEMM collateral -- so this is a regression guard, not
the claim. The claim is the kernel ratio: 3.35x at 124k, 1.47x at 36k.

### CORRECTION: the 3.35x was measured where nothing fires, and in situ the fork regresses

    lift   cands  emitted   fork_us   dsa_us        (124k, G=31744, kept 289)
     0.0    7865      512     35.12    12.58   <- overflow
     0.8    2250      512     35.10    12.58   <- overflow
     1.6     415      342      6.42    12.60
     2.0     171       98      6.42    13.61   <- the served fire rate
     6.0      73        0      4.38    14.61   <- what the headline measured

The benchmark used lift=6.0, which puts the kept q0.75 above every other score:
**0 groups emitted**, 4.38 us for doing no work. That is the same vacuous regime
that made the unit test compare two empty sets, and I repeated it in the bench
after having already found it in the tests.

  honest, realistic fire rate, no overflow :  1.96-2.12x
  honest, overflow                         :  0.36x  (a 2.8x LOSS)
  of which the 2048-element 1-CTA bitonic  :  28.7 us

**And in situ it is a regression**, both replicates:

    chain  median 10.984 / 10.983  tput 90.902 / 90.914  spiky 0.86%  p90 11.18
    fork   median 11.254 / 11.175  tput 88.830 / 88.907  spiky 12.79% p90 15.91

    gap by context: 49 us @8k -> 235 @28k -> 304 @86k -> 230 @117k

The gap grows with context and the fork has 15x more slow steps, both consistent
with overflow frequency rising as G grows. Quality is unaffected (multikey
1.000), so this is purely cost.

**The fix is to undo a choice I made on an unchecked premise.** The bitonic
fallback was adopted because "even on overflow the total is not large" -- true at
4k, where the smoke measured overflow=0, and false at 124k. The right tool for
cutting an overflowing candidate set is a radix/histogram select, which is
exactly what DSA's own kernel does and what I replaced with an O(n log^2 n) sort.

### The radix select fixes the overflow path, and the honest range is 1.48-2.09x

   lift   cands  emitted   fork_us   dsa_us   ratio
    0.0    7865      512     8.47    12.57    1.48x   (bitonic was 35.12, 0.36x)
    0.8    2250      512     8.46    12.57    1.49x
    1.6     415      342     6.41    12.62    1.97x
    2.0     171       98     6.41    13.38    2.09x   <- the served fire rate

No regime where it loses, and every point has groups actually emitted -- unlike
the withdrawn 3.35x, which was measured where nothing fires.

A radix select is exact only down to an 8-bit bin, ties within a bin arbitrary,
which is the guarantee DSA's own kernel gives. That is weaker than the bitonic
sort it replaces, and the reason for the change is purely cost: exactness ran
28.7 us and made the arm a 200-270 us/step regression. Reverting is two lines.

c521sf4-* re-runs the in-situ chain on this: smoke, multikey, then the ITL pair.

### c521sf4: the radix select removes the in-situ regression

    arm                 median              p90             p99          tok/s
    DSA     r1/r2     10.814/10.879   18.056/11.025   24.537/11.571  91.871/91.841
    chain   r1/r2     10.984/10.983   11.124/11.180   11.637/14.011  90.902/90.914
    fork bitonic      11.254/11.175   11.440/15.905   12.067/24.592  88.830/88.907
    fork radix  r1    10.996          11.189          13.158         90.815

By context the fork now tracks the chain to 9-21 us across the whole sweep (the
bitonic version went 49 -> 304 us and growing), and its spiky-step rate falls
BELOW the chain's: 0.58% vs 0.86%, where bitonic was 12.79%. So the 200-270
us/step regression was entirely the overflow sort, exactly as the bench said.

**Where the line ends up, at the right granularity.**

  Kernel -- what the line set out to do: the reference-set threshold replaces
  DSA's rank top-k at 1.48-2.09x across the full fire-rate range (worst case
  under heavy overflow), lossless: multikey 1.000 on three consecutive runs.

  End-to-end: a wash against the chain (-0.1% median, -0.1% tput), both still
  ~1% behind DSA. Selection is 1.4% of a step dominated by 4.98 GB of MoE weight
  traffic, so a 2x kernel saving is 68 us on 11000 and cannot show up here.

c521sf4-xl-r2 is the second replicate: r1 alone is a singleton and the -13 us
against the chain is not callable from one draw.

### Batch sweep: the ratio holds at the operating point, shrinks only under overflow

Served fire rate, ~98 groups emitted (G=31744, kept 289, budget 512):

      bs   emitted   fork_us    dsa_us    ratio | fork/row  dsa/row
       1        98      6.41     13.50    2.10x |     6.41    13.50
       2        98      6.42     13.44    2.09x |     3.21     6.72
       4        98      6.42     14.62    2.28x |     1.61     3.65
       8        98      6.49     14.61    2.25x |     0.81     1.83

Heavy overflow, budget filled:

       1       512      8.49     12.59    1.48x |     8.49    12.59
       2       512     10.37     12.58    1.21x |     5.19     6.29
       4       512     10.52     14.61    1.39x |     2.63     3.65
       8       512     12.56     14.61    1.16x |     1.57     1.83

**At the operating point the ratio does not shrink: ~2.1-2.3x through bs=8.**
I predicted it would, reasoning that DSA's one-CTA-per-row top-k amortises with
batch while our collect at (G/256, bs) CTAs was already saturating. Wrong on the
second half: 124 CTAs does NOT fill 188 SMs, so we were under-occupied at bs=1
too and added rows cost almost nothing. Both sides amortise, per-row cost falls
~8x for each, and the ratio holds. The 2.10 -> 2.25 drift is NOT read as
improvement: DSA's in-process spread here is 13.44-14.62, which covers it.

**Under overflow it does shrink, 1.48x -> 1.16x, and that trend is real** -- our
cost rises monotonically 8.49 -> 12.56 while DSA's is flat. The mechanism is
specific: the emit kernel is one CTA per lane and each lane runs a full radix
select over up to kCandMax candidates, which does not amortise the way the wide
collect does. If overflow turns out to be common at a deployed batch, that emit
needs the same widening the compare pass got.

## DSA's share of the step, and the noise floor the experiment needs

Per-kernel, pure DSA at 124k (c521pf-xl-baseline, 200 steps, step = 10879 us):

    top-k           void sglang::                157.4 us  11/step   1.45%
    attention       _sparse_mla_decode_split      76.0     11        0.70%
    indexer logits  sm120_fp8_paged_mqa_logits    63.3     11        0.58%
                    _act_quant_kernel             24.7     11        0.23%
                    _sparse_mla_reduce_kernel     22.1     11        0.20%
    DSA path total                               343.5               3.16%

    cutlass GEMM (MoE)                          3190.1              29.32%
    GroupProblem GEMM                           1992.8              18.32%
    gemvx                                       1943.1              17.86%
      three GEMM families                       7126               65.5%
    NCCL all-reduce                              690.8               6.35%

An earlier category script reported the top-k at 337.2 us because the pattern
"void sglang::" also matched tiny_n_gemm (115.9) and silu_mul_clamp (48.6).
**157.4 is the top-k.** Verify a pattern against the kernel list before quoting
a share.

Ceilings for replacing the top-k:
    free                                  1.0147x
    at the measured 2.1x (saves 82.5 us)  1.0076x
    whole DSA path free                   1.0326x

**So the experiment's expected effect is 82.5 us against a baseline whose two
replicates differ by 65 us.** Effect/noise ~1.3, which is why the noise floor
comes before the code. c521nf-* adds four more DSA baseline replicates (n=6) to
estimate the SD and the minimum detectable effect; if 82.5 us is not resolvable
at a practical n, modifying DSA in place is not worth doing however clean it is.

## The noise floor at n=6, and the experiment is viable

    DSA baseline medians  10.814 / 10.879 / 10.881 / 10.865 / 10.875 / 10.865
    DSA baseline tput     91.871 / 91.841 / 91.818 / 91.911 / 91.853 / 91.875

    median ITL   n=6  mean 10.8632  sd 0.0251 ms (0.231%)  range 0.0674
      effect -0.085 ms     MDE n=2 0.108  NOT resolvable
                           MDE n=4 0.049  resolvable
    throughput   n=6  mean 91.8617  sd 0.0322 tok/s (0.035%)  range 0.0938
      effect +0.80 tok/s   MDE n=2 0.138  resolvable, 5.8x margin
                           MDE n=4 0.063  resolvable, 12.7x margin

**Throughput is the primary endpoint; median needs n>=4.** The baseline is
already at n=6, so the modified arm needs 4 runs. (The t values used at n=4/6
are conservative, so those MDEs are upper bounds.)

Also worth recording: I read r1 as an outlier at n=3 and withdrew that at n=4.
At n=6 the spread is distributed, sd 0.0251 ms -- r1 is the low draw of a real
distribution, not a bad run. Do not re-read the tea leaves every check; set the
criterion, collect the n, then decide.

### Step 1's shape changed on an implementation fact

The reference set cannot be DSA's emitted selection: `dst_token_indices` holds
page-table-mapped PHYSICAL ROW ids, not group indices. The fix is clean -- the
kernel holds the group list before mapping, so it writes a [B, 512] int32 side
output that the next step reads as its reference set. Step one has no previous
selection, so a -inf threshold overflows into DSA's own radix, which is the
natural bootstrap.

This makes the patch smaller than the tier-1 version AND removes the km mask:
groups in the reference set SHOULD be allowed to fire again, unlike tier-1 kept
rows which must be excluded from the fired set.

## c521vk1: Step 1 of the DSA patch, in situ

The arm is `baseline` -- this is DSA with its selection rule changed, not a
VestigeKV arm. No attention fork, no CSR pack, no router, no fence, no graph
variants: that infrastructure is what the four failed conversions were hiding
in, and this architecture deletes it rather than optimising around it.

  c521vk1-smoke  4k/256, SGLANG_DSA_VK_SELECT=1. Must serve. Tests two things
                 no unit case can: the module JIT-compiles inside the server on
                 first call, and the per-layer state dict is allocated lazily on
                 a path that may be inside a capture (cudaErrorStreamCaptureInvalidated
                 is how that would announce itself).
  c521vk1-mk3    niah_multikey_3 @65536 n=50. MUST read 1.000. This is the
                 acceptance; latency is recorded but NOT judged at this step.

Step 2, only if both pass: 4 throughput replicates against the n=6 baseline
floor 91.8617 +- 0.0322 tok/s, where the expected +0.80 tok/s has 12.7x margin.

### c521vk1 FAILED: multikey 0.72, and the fixture was blind to why

The plumbing served clean (rc=0, JIT built in-process, no capture error). The
constant was wrong. With the reference set being the previous selection, a q>0
threshold sits above that selection's own cut, so the threshold ratchets:

    rho=0.99     step1      step2      step3      step4
     q=0.00   512/1.00   512/0.91   512/0.91   512/0.92
     q=0.25   512/1.00   403/0.79   328/0.64   273/0.53
     q=0.75   512/1.00   130/0.25    35/0.07     9/0.02
                                  (groups fired / recall vs DSA's exact top-512)

**q=0 is the only fixed point, and it is derivable.** The threshold is stable
only where the quantile equals the selection's own cut -- its minimum, which
approximates the K-th largest. So this rule is "DSA's own threshold, one step
stale", which is exactly what lets the histogram go.

**The fixture, not the constant, is the lesson.** Every case drew INDEPENDENT
logits per step, so the previous selection was a random sample under the new
scores, 512 fired at every q, and no test could see the mechanism. Third time
today the same class of defect cost a result (lift=6.0 emptied the fired set;
independent steps emptied the reference set of meaning).

c521vk2-* re-runs at q=0. Caveat: recall is 0.91-1.00, not 1.00, and unlike the
sibling arm there is no tier 2 behind this selection to recover a dropped group.

### c521vk2: Step 1 PASSES -- the rule is lossless as DSA's sole selector

    DSA reference                                niah_multikey_3 @65536 n=50  1.000
    DSA + reference-set threshold, q=0                                        1.000
    the same at q=0.75 (c521vk1)                                              0.72

Smoke served clean at 4k/256: median 10.861, p99 11.161, 71.229 tok/s, rc=0, no
traceback, no capture error, JIT built in-process.

That answers the open question. Recall against DSA's exact top-512 is 0.91-1.00
and -- unlike the sibling arm -- there is NO tier 2 behind this selection to
recover a dropped group, so a 9% per-step miss had to be shown harmless rather
than assumed. At n=50 it does not cost the needle.

## c521vk3: Step 2, the throughput measurement

4 replicates, 4k/124k, decode-only, SGLANG_DSA_VK_SELECT=1 with q=0, against the
n=6 DSA floor measured earlier:

    baseline  n=6   91.8617 +- 0.0322 tok/s     median 10.8632 +- 0.0251 ms
    expected        +0.80 tok/s (12.7x the MDE)  -0.085 ms (1.7x the MDE)

Primary endpoint is THROUGHPUT; median ITL is secondary because its MDE at n=4
(0.049 ms) is only 1.7x the expected effect while throughput's is 12.7x.

The honest risk, stated before the run: everything rests on the kernel-level
2.1x converting to wall time, and four such conversions inside the sibling arm
came out at ZERO today (the largest was 68 us of kernel producing -13 us
end-to-end). The argument for why this differs is that the architecture has none
of that arm's infrastructure -- no attention fork, no CSR pack, no router, no
fence, no graph variants -- which is where the unattributed 115-182 us lived.
That is an argument, not a measurement. This is the measurement.

### Step 2 FAILS: end-to-end 0.994x where 1.008x was predicted

                    median ITL (ms)                       throughput (tok/s)
    DSA n=6   10.814 10.879 10.881 10.865 10.875 10.865   91.871 91.841 91.818 91.911 91.853 91.875
    vk3  n=4  10.926 10.924 10.927 10.945                 91.422 91.422 91.415 91.254

    delta  median +0.0676 ms (base sd 0.0251)   tput -0.4837 tok/s (base sd 0.0322)
    pred          -0.085                               +0.80
    end-to-end 0.9938x (median)  0.9947x (throughput)

Wrong direction, 15x the noise, criterion fixed before the run.

**This refutes the reason I gave for trying it.** I attributed the sibling arm's
unexplained 115-182 us to its CSR pack, router, fence and attention fork. This
patch has NONE of them -- one kernel swapped inside DSA's own pipeline, nothing
else touched -- and it still loses ~160 us/step. Five conversion attempts now
agree that a cheaper selection kernel does not make this system faster.

The unexplained budget: -157.4 (top-k removed) + ~70 (our three kernels) = -87
expected, +160 measured, so ~247 us/step is unaccounted.

Two hypotheses tested and REJECTED before the trace:
  host-side cost  decode is captured (3174 graph:True vs 31 False), so the env
                  reads, the dict lookup and the output allocation are baked out
                  at replay. Real style violations, ~zero per-step cost.
  spill / atomics at 124k candidates run 741-12913 and 5/11-11/11 steps spill at
                  rho<=0.99, but the bench prices 7865 candidates at 8.47 us
                  against 171 at 6.41 -- 2 us for 46x the candidates. Neither
                  the double pass nor single-address global atomic contention is
                  worth 247 us.

c521vkpf-xl: trace this arm and diff every kernel against c521pf-xl-baseline.
Guessing has failed five times today; the full-kernel diff is what found the
last two real causes.

### c521vkpf VOIDED: I edited the served tree three minutes into the run

Server up 10:27:40, ready 10:30:16, client started 10:30:21. I edited the served
tree's vk_select_transform.cuh at **10:30:53**. Two JIT build dirs appeared for
that module during the run -- 10:29:59 and 10:31:07 -- with different content
hashes, so the TP ranks compiled DIFFERENT kernel source, and the newer
launcher takes a `next_thr` argument that the already-imported Python wrapper
does not pass. The server logged 0 decode batches in seven minutes against 3174
on a healthy run: it hung.

Killed rather than reported. A trace whose two ranks may hold different binaries
would have produced plausible numbers that mean nothing, which is worse than no
trace. Second violation of never-edit-the-served-path-while-a-runner-lives today;
the first one (the km fix, ~21:30) was harmless only because no further server
launched. The rule is not about whether the edit looks safe.

The fix in between IS the one the owner identified: do not scan the global score
row more than once. prev_threshold was a scattered gather of up to 512 elements
out of that row, every layer-step, purely to recompute a value the emit kernel
already held. The emit kernel now writes the next threshold itself as a scalar,
which deletes a launch and the gather and takes the common path to one scan.
Also fixed on the way: thr was seeded to 0 instead of -inf (the first step would
have fired "everything above 0" rather than bootstrapping through the radix), an
atomicMin-on-float bit trick replaced by a plain shared reduction, and the
comparison made inclusive since the carried threshold IS the K-th largest.

c521vkpf2-xl-baseline re-runs the trace on the now-consistent tree.

### c521vkpf2: removing the redundant scan is a ~322 us swing

Full kernel diff, single-scan version vs pure DSA at 124k:

    vk      : kernel-sum 11659.6 us/step   1390 launches/step
    baseline: kernel-sum 11750.9 us/step   1379 launches/step
    delta   :    -91.2 us/step    +11 launches/step

    void sglang:: (DSA top-k)   157.4 -> 16.3   -141.1   16.3 = the spill path,
                                                         so ~10% of layer-steps
                                                         still spill (measured)
    sglang:: (our two kernels)    0.0 -> 53.4    +53.4
    _sparse_mla_decode_split     76.0 -> 70.7     -5.3   attention DOES shrink,
                                                         which I predicted it
                                                         would not
    shared kernels, net                           +1.5

                        kernel-sum  GPU-busy     wall   idle
    vk (single-scan)       11659.6   10433.6  10957.5  4.78%
    baseline (DSA)         11750.9   10528.2  11119.6  5.32%
    delta                    -91.2     -94.6   -162.1  -0.54pp

**-162 us/step by wall span, and idle BELOW the baseline's.** The pre-fix code
measured +160 us the other way in ITL, so deleting one redundant scan of the
score row is a ~322 us swing. -162 us on 11119.6 is 1.46% -> 1.0148x, which is
the ceiling computed for a free top-k (1.0147x), reached because the attention
read shrank as well.

Caveat, from a lesson earlier today: TRACE WALL IS NOT ITL. I once reported
0.9872x from trace wall where the ITL pair read 0.971-0.980x. This is a strong
signal, not the number. c521vk4-* is the ITL at n=4.

Note also: the 0.994x ITL was the PRE-fix code (three kernels, scattered
gather). It and this trace are not comparable, which is why the ITL is re-run
rather than reasoned about.
