## c521vk4: end-to-end CROSSES 1 -- the redundant scan was the whole failure

    DSA baseline n=6       med  10.814 10.879 10.881 10.865 10.875 10.865
                           tput 91.871 91.841 91.818 91.911 91.853 91.875
    pre-fix (2 scans) n=4  med  10.926 10.924 10.927 10.945
                           tput 91.422 91.422 91.415 91.254
    single-scan       n=4  med  10.811 10.796 10.810 10.822
                           tput 92.421 92.414 92.426 92.318

                 throughput            ratio    median      ratio
    pre-fix      91.3780 (-0.484)     0.9947x   10.9308    0.9938x
    single-scan  92.3948 (+0.533)     1.0058x   10.8098    1.0049x

+0.533 tok/s on a 0.0322 sd: 16.5 sigma, criterion fixed before the run. The
swing from the owner's one observation is 1.017 tok/s.

**The engineering failure, named exactly.** The threshold -- the minimum of the
previous selection -- was recomputed by a separate kernel that GATHERED up to
512 scattered elements out of the global score row every layer-step, to recover
a value the emit kernel already held in registers at the moment it selected.
Carrying it forward as a scalar deleted a launch and a whole pass.

Why it mattered so much on a 1.45% term: a redundant scattered gather is cheap
in isolation (~3 us/layer) but lands on the same path, so it ate about twice the
entire prize. On a term this small, any extra global traffic in the replacement
is comparable to the thing being replaced. That is the general lesson.

Caveats kept: trace wall said -162 us (1.0148x) and ITL delivered -53 us
(1.0058x), ~40% of the 1.0147x ceiling -- trace wall overstates ITL, again. And
p99 is noisy on this MACHINE: vk4 r2/r4 read 23.557/17.175 while r1/r3 read
11.38/11.40, and DSA's own r1 read 24.537 against its siblings' ~11.5.

**Line status.** Kernel 2.1x vs DSA's rank top-k (1.48x worst case under
overflow), holding through bs=8; quality multikey 1.000 at n=50 as DSA's SOLE
selector with no tier 2 behind it; end-to-end 1.0058x at n=4 against n=6.

Remaining headroom: ~10% of layer-steps still take the spill path into DSA's
radix (16.3 us/step in the trace), and the emit's single-CTA structure is the
next kernel-level item.

### kappa=4's precision, and a retraction: the (kappa,m) grid was on ALIASED dumps

Re-run on cd3_64k (176 dumps, non-aliased, G=15350), kappa=1,2,4,8,16:

    precision vs DSA top-512   m=32     m=64    m=128    m=256
      sigma k=1              0.6776   0.5928   0.4840   0.3692
      sigma k=2              0.6727   0.5813   0.4716   0.3610
      sigma k=4              0.6533   0.5698   0.4624   0.3550
      sigma k=8              0.6348   0.5560   0.4492   0.3412
      sigma k=16             0.6099   0.5319   0.4311   0.3267
      centred row norm       0.6776   0.5928   0.4840   0.3692   == k=1
      raw row norm           0.0722   0.0502   0.0345   0.0268
      oracle (att mass)      0.9215   0.9043   0.8770   0.8099
      oracle (dsa score)     1.0000   1.0000   1.0000   1.0000
      random (closed form)   0.0313   0.0313   0.0313   0.0313

    captured attention mass  m=32     m=64    m=128    m=256
      sigma k=1              0.0246   0.0354   0.0490   0.0695
      sigma k=4              0.0233   0.0328   0.0463   0.0665
      sigma k=16             0.0212   0.0293   0.0409   0.0589
      oracle (att mass)      0.0608   0.0796   0.1063   0.1447
      random (closed form)   0.0019   0.0038   0.0076   0.0152

**RETRACTED: "sigma beats the attention-mass oracle on precision".** The earlier
grid (register ~line 3252) ran on cd_w3,cd_w2,cd_g2 -- the ALIASED dumps, where
each of 88 files holds 8 bit-identical qidx/qgate, so "704 real queries" was 88.
On those the oracle read 0.6491 against sigma k=2's 0.6626 and I called it the
NoPE spectral property. On cd3 the oracle reads 0.9215 against sigma k=1's
0.6776: sigma does NOT beat it, it reaches 74% of it. The whole earlier table is
superseded by this one.

kappa stays monotone with k=1 best, which does not change the served value: 4 was
chosen on the cost-matched end-to-end criterion (real softmax mass over
kept+fired+sinks+tail at EQUAL fired count, 240 honest queries from cd3_64k),
where k=1 leads k=4 by 0.0004 and k=1 degenerates to a bare block-mean
subtraction with no margin if the block statistics shift.

### The cut/expand split is dead, and that makes eight

    budget  out_cols   before   after(split)
       128       515     2.96      4.39
       256      1027     2.31      4.35
       512      2051     4.35      4.35

The wide expand does what it was designed to -- the out_cols dependence is gone
(flat 4.35, was 2.31 -> 4.35) -- but the extra launch plus the sel_groups global
round-trip costs exactly what the write loop saved, and below budget 512, where
there was less to save, it is a straight loss. Reverted; both trees clean, 24
cases green, pre-split timings reproduce.

That is eight structural predictions of mine refuted in two days:

    tune inside _branch_hit (scatter)   predicted ~40 us    got  4.3 us
    merge 2 launches into 1             predicted ~13 us    got  0.01 us
    5 serial stages -> 2                predicted ~107 us   got -0.02 us
    contention explains the gap         predicted large     got  4% of the ratio
    page-table scatter is the emit cost predicted ~4 us     got  0.00
    n_sel is the emit cost              predicted some      got  0.00
    wide expand                         predicted +0.2 t/s  got  0 / worse

**The only two that worked were deletions of work**: skipping DSA's top-k
(157.4 us/step) and deleting the redundant pass over the global score row
(1.017 tok/s, 0.9947x -> 1.0058x). At 1.45% of an 11 ms step the fixed costs --
a 0.53 us launch, a global round-trip, one CTA's latency -- are comparable to the
whole prize, so relocating work does not change the number. Recorded as
memory/only-deleting-work-helps-here.

**Line final state.** end-to-end 1.0058x (n=4 vs n=6, 16.5 sigma); multikey
1.000 as DSA's sole selector; kernel 2.1x its rank top-k, flat in context,
holding through bs=8; spill eliminated through rho=0.99. The remaining ~0.9% to
the 1.0147x ceiling needs the selection's WORK to shrink, and what is left is one
pass over all G scores -- the irreducible part of the rule.

## c521q-full: the quality half of the crossover, on the arm that crossed

The latency claim is settled: **1.0058x end-to-end** (tput 92.421/92.414/92.426/
92.318 against a DSA baseline of 91.8617 +- 0.0322 at n=6, 16.5 sigma), kernel
2.1x DSA's rank top-k, spill eliminated through rho=0.99. The quality claim is
not. The entire quality record of that arm is **one cell** -- `niah_multikey_3`
@65536, n=50, `c521vk2-mk3` = 1.000 -- and `glm-line-order-and-paper-rule` gates
GLM's inclusion on a crossover vs DSA, which a latency number alone does not
establish.

The full v1 `ruler` group HAS run on this line, but only at n=10 and only on arms
that predate the fork: `ruler-*` and `rulerv0520*` carry no
`SGLANG_DSA_VK_SELECT` key at all. Nothing comparable exists for the served
selector.

So: the full 13-task group, the default 5-length ladder, n=50, both arms.

    {"args": {"lengths": "4096,8192,16384,32768,65536", "n": 50, "seed": 0}, "arm": "baseline", "client": "ruler", "env": {"CHUNK": "1024", "CTX": "73728", "CUDA_HOME": "/usr/local/cuda", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "4", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "4", "MAX_REQS": "4", "MEM_FRAC": "0.955", "SGLANG_DSA_VK_SELECT": "0", "SGLANG_DSA_VK_SELECT_Q": "0.0", "VK_JOB": "c521q-full-dsa"}, "id": "c521q-full-dsa", "probe": false}
    {"args": {"lengths": "4096,8192,16384,32768,65536", "n": 50, "seed": 0}, "arm": "baseline", "client": "ruler", "env": {"CHUNK": "1024", "CTX": "73728", "CUDA_HOME": "/usr/local/cuda", "ENGINE": "/home/user/vestigekv-wt/engine-glm521", "GRAPH_BS": "4", "MAMBA_SLOTS": "4", "MAX_REQS": "4", "MEM_FRAC": "0.955", "SGLANG_DSA_VK_SELECT": "1", "SGLANG_DSA_VK_SELECT_Q": "0.0", "VK_JOB": "c521q-full-vk"}, "id": "c521q-full-vk", "probe": false}

**Why this pair is the cleanest control this line has produced.** Both rows are
`arm=baseline` -- the DSA arm -- on one tree (`glm53-v0521` at `b3ad8ee706`,
clean) at one geometry, with `args` byte-identical. The only env keys that differ
are `SGLANG_DSA_VK_SELECT` and `VK_JOB` (the latter only so the runner does not
reuse a server and accumulate VKSTATS across the pair). The hook is a single
early return at `dsa_indexer_kpool.py:1200`, so `SELECT=0` falls through to DSA's
own `_kpool_fused_topk_mapping` and its radix top-k. There is no second chain, no
sidecar, no per-layer split: the diff is which kernel picks the 512 groups, and
nothing else. Every confound that made the earlier GLM arms arguable -- a
different tree, a different budget, a sidecar index, group-mean dilution across
arms measured months apart -- is absent by construction.

**Acceptance, stated before the data.** Per-cell, paired, 65 cells per arm
(13 tasks x 5 lengths). Not the group mean: `glm-quality-claim-did-not-replicate`
is the record of a group mean hiding a 0.54-vs-1.00 collapse on one cell, so the
mean is reported only alongside the cells and never instead of them. The read is
"no cell regresses beyond the paired noise", and the deciding cells are the
retrieval ones -- `niah_multikey_*`, `niah_multivalue`, `niah_multiquery` -- since
`mrcr-is-blind-to-admission-width` and `fence-is-a-cost-guard-only` both record
that the aggregate metrics on this line cannot see a selection change that the
needle tasks resolve immediately.

**A free self-replicate.** The vk row reproduces `c521vk2-mk3` exactly: same
task, same length, same n, same seed, same tree. If `niah_multikey_3` @65536 comes
back 1.000 a second time, that singleton becomes a pair, which is what Method
rule 1 has demanded of every other number on this line. If it does not, that is
the more valuable outcome and it invalidates the one quality figure quoted for
this arm.

**Cost: ~20.5 h for the pair, not the ~10 h first estimated.** The estimate
scaled `rulerv0520-baseline` (650 requests in 3604 s = 5.5 s/req) by five. The
run itself reports 11.5 s/it over 3250 requests, holding at an ETA of 10 h 16 m
for the DSA arm alone, so the pair is a little over twenty hours. That rate is
already an average over the whole ladder -- lm-eval interleaves all 13 tasks and
all 5 lengths into one request stream rather than running cells in sequence -- so
it is not an artifact of starting on the long cells. The n=10 precedent ran at a
different geometry and does not scale.

Kept at n=50 anyway: the GPUs are otherwise idle, nothing is preempted, and n=50
is what makes the vk arm's `niah_multikey_3` @65536 cell an exact replicate of
`c521vk2-mk3`. Cutting to n=25 would halve the wall time and give up the one
free replicate of the only quality number this arm has. `check_builds.py` is
clean for GLM -- its eight flagged runs are all Kimi jobs already retired.

## c521q-full result: the crossover is a trade, not a win

Both arms rc=0 (`c521q-full-dsa` 15538 s, `c521q-full-vk` 15158 s). 65 paired
cells each, scored per sample and paired by `doc_id` on identical seeds, so every
number below is a within-item difference and not two independent means.

**The registered free replicate passed.** vk `niah_multikey_3` @65536 n=50 =
1.000, reproducing `c521vk2-mk3` exactly. The one quality figure ever quoted for
this arm is now a pair.

**`ruler_cwe` regresses, monotonically in context length.**

    len     dsa      vk     paired diff      t      samples changed
     4k    1.000   0.998      -0.002      -1.00          1/50
     8k    0.982   0.938      -0.044      -2.00          6/50
    16k    1.000   0.938      -0.062      -2.41         10/50
    32k    0.998   0.860      -0.138      -3.86         26/50
    64k    0.960   0.758      -0.202      -5.62         37/50

t = -5.62 at 64k. Five lengths, all negative, magnitude and the count of
perturbed samples both rising with length (1 -> 37 of 50). This is not the ~0.02
noise band and it is not one cell: it is a dose-response in exactly the direction
sparser selection predicts, since top-2048 is half the sequence at 4k and 3% of
it at 64k.

**Why cwe and nothing else.** `ruler_cwe` is the only task here that needs BROAD
coverage rather than a needle: it asks which words recur most across the whole
list. The carried-threshold rule is built to hold a selection stable, which is
right for a needle and wrong for an aggregate -- the q=0 fixed-point derivation
asked whether the threshold is self-consistent, never whether the set it
stabilises covers a flat, spread-out target. The needle tasks agree:
`niah_multikey_*`, `multiquery`, `multivalue` and `ruler_vt` all sit at or within
0.02 of DSA.

Nothing else reaches significance. `ruler_fwe` is negative at all five lengths
but |t| <= 1.80 with large se; `qa_squad` and `qa_hotpot` straddle zero;
`niah_single_3` loses 1-2 samples per cell (|t| <= 1.43).

**The grand mean is 0.9509 -> 0.9390, and quoting it would be the same error this
line already made once.** The acceptance registered before the data said
per-cell, and here the mean actively understates: it dilutes a -0.202 collapse
across 37 cells that sat at ceiling on DSA and could never have moved.

**Two of my own predictions were wrong.** I wrote that the discriminating rows
would be `fwe`/`qa_squad`/`qa_hotpot` because they sit off ceiling. The
discriminating row is `cwe`, which sat AT ceiling on DSA and therefore had room to
fall rather than rise -- "off ceiling" identifies where a gain could show, not
where a loss will. And my first two extractions of the per-sample scores were
wrong because I guessed the schema instead of reading it: lm-eval writes
`metrics: ['4096', <the sample's real length>]` with `s['4096']` a sentinel on
every non-4k sample, which yielded an impossible mean of -0.600.

**Standing on `glm-line-order-and-paper-rule`: the crossover is NOT established.**
1.0058x end-to-end comes with -0.202 at 64k on an aggregation task. The defensible
claim is a speed/quality trade with a workload dependence, not a free win over
DSA. Whether that is worth the paper is the user's call, not a measurement.
